Reused the `create_shared` scoped-`FlutterError.onError` 'A RenderFlex overflowed'-only filter (the scaffold+bottom-nav+centred illustration overflow vertically on a tall surface). Behaviors pinned: loading text+indicator, empty title+explanation+CTA, error message+retry, retry→refreshAllContent, CTA→home-route. Diff: only this one test file (+ `@Skip` removed); `dart analyze` clean; `+5 All tests passed`.

### 2026-06-01 — BUT-1171 PhotoImportViewModel @visibleForTesting setters [Pattern]
**Retiring a leaky test double: give production a single `@visibleForTesting` setter on the REAL field and delete the shadow field + getter overrides — do NOT add a parallel getter override.** `setOcrTextForTesting`/`setImageBytesForTesting` assign the real `_ocrText`/`_imageBytes` (+ `notifyListeners()`), so getters AND the pipeline observe the same state — this is the fix for the 2026-05-30 leaky-double finding (a shadow field diverging from the field the pipeline reads is the exact shape that produces "Expected true, Actual false" with zero production defect). The now-real save path is adequately covered (success / no-recipe-guard / save-failure, unblocked by the ServiceLocator bridge + empty `HeirloomBridge` in setUp). GENUINE GAP (file LOW): the heirloom-PENDING branch of `_attachHeirloomIfPending` is never exercised (bridge has no pending draft → early-returns); the whole BUT-953/1086/1161 upload sub-path (auth re-check → `errorAuthentication`, content-addressed path build, `uploadImageData`, draft-restore-on-failure) stays unpinned at the VM level — the new setters make a VM-level heirloom test cheap. Minor pre-existing smell: a `Future.delayed(10ms)` for processing-state (should be `fakeAsync`+`elapse`).

### 2026-06-03 — BUT-1180 FriendsListView (most service-entangled of the social set) [Pattern + Bug]
Rebuilt the ~50-test ULTRATHINK smoke-suite into 5 real behavior tests. Mirrors the `shared_with_me`/`create_shared` harness (prod bridge + `ViewTestHelpers.setupViewTestEnvironment` + `registerMock<T>` + plain localized MaterialApp). Six DI resolves in initState/build; the traps, in build-order so you can pre-wire all of them:
- **`MockSocialEventsTracker` (via `_NoOpFutureMock`) returns `Future<void>` for `logSocialOnboardingStartedIfFirstEntry`, which production declares `Future<bool>` → runtime cast fail in initState.** The shared no-op only special-cases the `logFirst\w+IfMilestone` regex; this `...IfFirstEntry` method slips to the `Future<void>` default. Fix WITHOUT touching production (parallel session): local `_FakeSocialEventsTracker extends Fake implements SocialEventsTracker` overriding just that method to return `Future<bool>`, + `_TestAnalyticsService extends MockAnalyticsService` overriding `social` to return it, `registerMock<AnalyticsService>(...)`. (Shared-mock gap worth a production follow-up: widen `_NoOpFutureMock` to cover `IfFirstEntry` too.)
- **`Provider.value(value: MockUnifiedFriendsService)` trips `Provider.debugCheckInvalidValueType`** — the mock mixes in `ChangeNotifier` (Listenable) while the real service isn't. `Provider.debugCheckInvalidValueType = null` in `setUpAll` (same as the create_shared entry).
- **TabBar Groups tab reads `friendsService.invitations.pendingReceivedInvitations`** — default `MockUnifiedFriendsService.invitations` returns a fresh unstubbed `MockFriendsInvitationsOperations` (noSuchMethod → null → crash on `.isNotEmpty`). Build one with `when(()=>ops.pendingReceivedInvitations).thenReturn(<GroupInvitation>[])` and pass via `setFriendsState(invitations: ops)`.
- **The view captures `FriendsViewModel` AND `ActivityFeedViewModel` in initState (both registered as FACTORIES) — override BOTH with singletons** via `registerMock`. The default tab is Feed (index 0) → `FeedTab.build` reads `ActivityFeedViewModel.isLoading`/`error` (BaseViewModel getters, noSuchMethod → null → cast-to-bool crash). Stub `isLoading`/`error` with `when()`. **DON'T stub `loadFeed`/`events`/`filteredEvents` — they're CONCRETE bodies on `MockActivityFeedViewModel`** → `when()` throws "No method stub was called from within when()". Drive `events` via `setFeedState(events: const [])` instead.
- **`FriendsViewModel` widget-mock only defines `friends`/`isLoading` concretely** — the view branches on `searchQuery`/`searchResults`/`incomingRequests`/`sentRequests`/`friendsCount` (all noSuchMethod) and `updateSearch` (returns `Future<void>`, stub `thenAnswer((_) async {})` NOT `thenReturn(null)`). The Find-friends tab (index 3, empty search) shows `RequestsTab` which reads BOTH `incomingRequests` AND `sentRequests` — miss `sentRequests` and you get `Null is not a subtype of List<FriendRequest>` only after the tab-switch, not at initial pump.

### 2026-06-03 — BUT-1180 CollaborativeShoppingView rebuild + the REAL re-entrancy root cause [Pattern + Bug]
Rebuilt the @Skip'd ~30-test ULTRATHINK smoke-suite (topology `findsOneWidget` on `ChangeNotifierProvider<VM>`/`Consumer<VM>` + Stopwatch "performance" asserts) into 5 real behavior tests. **The brief's premise that the 17 failures were a sync-StreamController re-entrancy was WRONG** — investigated empirically by running the unskipped suite. Actual root causes (two, neither is a re-entrancy):
- **(1) Type-mismatched `Provider.value` made the old "controlled VM" tests a no-op.** The old `TestableCollaborativeShoppingViewModel extends MockBaseViewModel` does NOT implement `CollaborativeShoppingViewModel`, so `ChangeNotifierProvider.value(value: testableVM)` registered the WRONG type — the view's `ChangeNotifierProvider(create: ...)` ALWAYS built its own production VM and the `.value` provider was dead. So every "controlled state" test was actually exercising the real VM against the default factory `MockUnifiedShoppingService`. There is no `BehaviorSubject`/sync controller in the test path — `MockUnifiedShoppingService.stateStream` is `.broadcast()` (emits nothing); the production `_stateSubject` (rxdart `BehaviorSubject`) is never constructed because the mock replaces the whole service. **The "Bad state: Cannot fire new event" in the @Skip note was misdiagnosed — the real failures were (2).**
- **(2) PRODUCTION LAYOUT FRAGILITY: the view shell's add-item Row goes horizontally-unbounded in a bare test Scaffold → `BoxConstraints forces an infinite width` from `FilledButton.icon` (`collaborative_shopping_actions.dart:173`).** Shell shape: `Scaffold > SafeArea > Center > ConstrainedBox(maxWidth: LayoutComponents.valueFor(mobile: double.infinity, ...)) > Column > [..., buildAddItemSection → Row[Expanded(TextField), FilledButton]]`. On the bare test harness the `Center`→`ConstrainedBox(maxWidth: infinity)` path leaves the Column (hence the Row) width-unbounded, so the non-flex `FilledButton.icon` is measured with infinite width and asserts. (In the shipped app a parent gives the Scaffold body a finite width, masking it — but it's a latent fragility: any host that hands this view an unbounded-width slot crashes. Reported, not "fixed" — parallel session owns production.) A SECONDARY framework assertion (`_RenderObjectSemantics.debugCheckForParentData` deep recursion + `Null check` in `_ViewportElement.debugVisitOnstageChildren`) cascades from the broken layout frame (the claimable items wrap in `Dismissible`); it disappears once the layout is sound.
- **The fix that keeps the tests faithful AND deterministic (group_detail template): drive the REAL production VM + REAL sub-widget, NOT the full shell.** Construct `CollaborativeShoppingViewModel(listId:, shoppingService: <our MockUnifiedShoppingService>)` directly (resolves `PermissionService`/presence from the bridge; `_presenceModule` is null because `ShoppingPresenceModule` isn't registered → presence skipped), `await Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero)` to drain the ctor's fire-and-forget `_initialize()/_loadList()`, then render `CollaborativeShoppingItems(viewModel:, onToggleItem:)` inside a width-bounded `Scaffold(body: SizedBox(width: 600, ...))`. This pins the user-visible item behaviour (each item renders by Swedish name; empty list → "Inga varor ännu"; checkbox tap → `onToggleItem(id)`; bought item → strikethrough title) without depending on the fragile add-item Row.
- **Absent-list outcome is a VM-level test, not a widget test — because `executeAsync` RETHROWS.** `_loadList` throws on a missing list; `executeAsync` (StateNotifierMixin) does `setError(sanitizeErrorForUser(e))` THEN `rethrow`. The view's fire-and-forget `_initialize()` doesn't catch → the rejection surfaces as an uncaught zone error that fails ANY test that constructs the VM with the list already absent. **Recipe: construct with the list PRESENT (ctor `_initialize` succeeds, no uncaught rejection), drain the microtask, THEN remove the list and `await vm.refresh()` inside a `try/catch`** (refresh routes through the SAME `executeAsync(_loadList)` path) → assert `vm.hasError` + `vm.error == 'Ett fel uppstod. Försök igen.'` (the SANITIZED generic, NOT the raw `errorUnexpected` the throw carries — `sanitizeErrorForUser` collapses it).
- **Copy traps (assert exact ARB values):** `collaborativeNoItemsYet` = "Inga varor **ännu**" (not "...än"); `collaborativeListNotFound` = "**Lista** hittades inte" (not "Listan...") — and that not-found EMPTY state is unreachable when the list is absent because the ERROR branch (error != null) wins in `LoadingStateBuilder`. **`toggleItemBought(itemId)` takes ONE positional arg** (the old guess of two compile-fails). **`amount: 0` on `UnifiedShoppingItem` → `displayText == name`** (no amount/unit prefix) so `find.text('Mjölk')` works without a quantity. Diff: only this one test file (+ `@Skip` removed); `dart analyze` clean; `+5 All tests passed`. **Lesson: when a @Skip note names a root cause, RE-DERIVE it empirically — run the unskipped suite and read the first real exception — before designing around it; the note here blamed a re-entrancy that doesn't exist and the real blocker was a layout-constraint bug + a rethrow-out-of-fire-and-forget.**
- **Duplicate-copy collision**: with `friendsCount==0` the Feed tab's no-friends empty-state CTA `feedEmptyNoFriendsCta` is ALSO "Hitta vänner" — identical to the Find-friends TAB label. `find.text('Hitta vänner')` matches twice. Scope tab-label asserts to `find.descendant(of: find.byType(TabBar), matching: find.text(...))`.
Behaviors pinned: 4 Swedish tab labels + controller.length==4; Friends-empty branded state (headline + find-by-username CTA); empty-CTA → tab controller animates to index 3; Groups tab → create-group FAB appears (absent on Feed); social-flag OFF → hardcoded disabled fallback + no TabBar. Diff: only this one test file (+ `@Skip` removed); `dart analyze` clean; `+5 All tests passed`.

### 2026-06-03 — BUT-1185 PersonalTagSelectionManager [Pattern]
Pure-`ChangeNotifier` multi-select managers (`personal_tag_selection_manager.dart`, mirror of `recipe_selection_manager.dart`): zero deps, no `setUp` bootstrap beyond `addListener(()=>notifyCount++)`. **Pin the auto-exit invariant explicitly** — toggling the LAST selected id out sets `_isSelectionMode=false` (line 27-29); that's intended UI (empty selection bar is confusing), so pin-as-correct, don't flag. **`selectedTagIds` getter returns `Set.unmodifiable` → `expect(()=>ids.add('x'), throwsUnsupportedError)`** proves callers can't corrupt internal state. notifyCount==1 per mutation (no double-fire). Mirror sibling test for style — same group/intent structure, no `flutter_test` scaffolding needed.
### 2026-06-03 — BUT-1049 comment-image attachments [Pattern]
**`CommentFormWidget` cap-enforcement is testable through the existing harness**: the attach IconButton is gated `if (!_atImageCap)` (hidden, not disabled) once `_selectedImages.length >= RecipeComment.maxImageUrls`. Mock `picker.pickMultipleImages` to return `[tempImage]` each call, tap attach `maxImageUrls` times, assert `find.byIcon(Icons.add_photo_alternate_outlined)` goes `findsOneWidget`→`findsNothing`. The mock returning the SAME file each tap is fine — the cap counts entries, not uniqueness. **Upload-failure safety test is load-bearing and correct**: it pairs `errorShown==true` with `verifyNever(() => vm.postComment(...))` — proves no partial post when `uploadCommentImage` returns null (the "never publish a comment referencing images that didn't land" contract). Don't weaken to just the error-shown half.
**`CommentImageAttachments` renderer needs NO heavy DI** — pure StatelessWidget over a `List<String>`; only `context.l10n` (so the localized `_wrap` MaterialApp suffices, no ServiceLocator). `CachedNetworkImage` renders its placeholder synchronously in tests, so `findsNWidgets(N)` for N urls + `findsNothing` for `[]` (the `SizedBox.shrink` contract) work without network mocking. **The full-screen viewer (`_CommentImageViewer`) uses a `CircularProgressIndicator` placeholder → `pumpAndSettle` TIMES OUT** (recurrence of the 2026-05-26 DialogFactory CPI-pump rule); tap the thumbnail then `pump()` + `pump(Duration(milliseconds: 300))` and assert `find.byType(Dialog)`/`PageView`/`Icons.close`.
**VM→manager `imageUrls` threading is structural pass-through** (`postComment(id, {imageUrls}) => _commentsManager.postComment(id, imageUrls: imageUrls)`, no logic) — already pinned at the meaningful seam by the widget's "upload success: posts with the uploaded URLs" test (`verify(() => vm.postComment('r1', imageUrls: [url]))`). No separate VM test earns its keep; the viewmodel-test change was a Fake-signature update only. Renderer + form added 4 tests (1 cap + 3 renderer), full file now 7+3.

### 2026-06-03 — BUT-1185 PersonalTagSelectionManager [Pattern]
Pure-`ChangeNotifier` multi-select managers (`personal_tag_selection_manager.dart`, mirror of `recipe_selection_manager.dart`): zero deps, no `setUp` bootstrap beyond `addListener(()=>notifyCount++)`. **Pin the auto-exit invariant explicitly** — toggling the LAST selected id out sets `_isSelectionMode=false` (line 27-29); that's intended UI (empty selection bar is confusing), so pin-as-correct, don't flag. **`selectedTagIds` getter returns `Set.unmodifiable` → `expect(()=>ids.add('x'), throwsUnsupportedError)`** proves callers can't corrupt internal state. notifyCount==1 per mutation (no double-fire). Mirror sibling test for style — same group/intent structure, no `flutter_test` scaffolding needed.
**VM pass-through coverage rule**: `mergeTags`/`bulkDeleteTags` on `personal_tag_viewmodel.dart` (lines 277-283) are bare one-line `=> _service.x()` delegations — NO `safeExecute`, no state, no transform — already covered by `personal_tag_service_test.dart` (8 merge tests incl. same-id/empty-id edges). A VM test would only re-verify "delegation calls the method" = structural, skip it. **The real untested logic is the merge-LOOP in `personal_tag_bulk_dialogs.dart` (lines 88-119)**: filters out target, loops `mergeTags(fromId, target)` sequentially accumulating `updated`, partial-failure try/catch → snackbar. That loop + the "N>2 sequential merge is safe because source is deleted each call" assumption is a genuine coverage gap — but it lives in a dialog hitting `context.l10n`/`Navigator`/`SnackBarUtils`, so it's widget-test territory or In-Review manual pass, not a unit test.


## Relocated 2026-07-04 — consolidation batch (entries 2026-06-04 → 2026-06-26; durable lessons distilled into the active file's principles section)

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-706 AdaptiveAppBar: platform-dispatch tests are behavioural, not topological [Pattern]
`find.byType(CupertinoNavigationBar)` vs `find.byType(AppBar)` IS a behavioural assertion when the widget's entire purpose is to dispatch between those two widget types — do not classify as the DO-NOT-WRITE "structural topology" anti-pattern. `debugDefaultTargetPlatformOverride` reset: set at top of body + `addTearDown` + inline null at end of body is belt-and-suspenders (the framework verifies debug vars before tearDown, so the inline null is load-bearing when the body throws mid-way through). One real gap: the null-actions iOS branch (`trailing: null` path in the `actions == null || actions!.isEmpty` guard) is not tested — cheapest to add a `testWidgets('renders on iOS with no actions', ...)` with `const AdaptiveAppBar(title: 'x')` and expect `findsOneWidget` + no throw. Collection-stats-view migration (2-line title-only) is an acceptable gap per BUT-387 Phase 6 (view mechanical tests deleted; `flutter analyze` catches compile errors). `leading` + `centerTitle` are pure pass-through to platform widgets — untested, low priority.

### 2026-06-14 — RealtimeSyncService conflictStream wire-up gap (BUT-1031) [Pattern]
**A fan-out wire-up gets an exception to the BUT-754 "no own test" rule when it carries a guard branch AND is the ONLY place an end-to-end contract holds.** `RealtimeSyncService` bridges `_conflictModule.onConflict` → public `conflictStream` via `(event){ if(!_conflictController.isClosed) _conflictController.add(event); }`. The module test proves the module emits to its injected sink; the `ConflictBanner` widget test stubs `conflictStream` with a hand-fed `StreamController`. **Neither proves the SERVICE delivers a real conflict to subscribers** — drop the `onConflict` callback in `_initializeModules`, or invert the `isClosed` guard, and all 29 service tests + the banner test stay green while the production banner goes dark. The existing "cache reflects the remote winner after a conflict the local lost" test (line 347) ALREADY drives `resolveConflict`→remoteWon→`_emitConflict`→`onConflict`, so closing the gap is a one-liner: subscribe to `service.conflictStream` before the `updateResource(losingLocal)` call and `expect` a `ConflictEvent` with `chosenStrategy == remoteWon` lands. **Lesson: when a module emits to an injected sink AND a widget consumes a stubbed stream, audit whether anything tests the service that wires the two — a stubbed-both-ends pair is a classic mocked-away-subject blind spot.** Otherwise the file is exemplary: real conflict resolution driven through `FakeFirebaseFirestore`, side-effects (cache + disk) asserted not just returns, correct local `_MockAuthRepository` (shared one's concrete `currentUserId` blocks `when()`).

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-1273 index-page expansion coverage review [Pattern]
**Three gaps survive an otherwise solid suite.** (1) `ExtractedContentAnalyzer` was extracted as a new file with no dedicated test; existing `analyzeExtractedContent()` tests go through the VM so a regression inside the analyzer is caught, but the pure-compute boundary is untested in isolation — low risk because the behavior-preservation comment says "byte-for-byte identical." (2) The `_fetchPage` conditional (`fetchFromUrl` succeeds → skip `detectIndexPage`) has no widget-level test; only `_fetchPage`-misses fire the detector. A test pumping the view with `hasExtractedText=true` and asserting no banner would be the closing gate. (3) The `isLoading` disabled-state for the index-page CTA is not asserted; a future refactor splitting the loading flag could silently re-enable the button mid-expand. **Pattern: when a new pure-compute file is extracted from a VM to keep under 500 lines, add at least a smoke test in the file's own `test/unit/services/` path — not for coverage optics but to prove the seam is addressable without the VM scaffolding.** Also noted: the widget test hardcodes the banner copy `'Det här ser ut som en receptsamling.'` — fine per-spec (it's a localization key assertion, not a theme value), but append a `reason:` quoting the l10n key so the failure message survives an ARB-file rename.

### 2026-06-08 — BUT-1210 announce-channel harness (a11y SemanticsService.announce) [Helper added + Pattern]
**New helper: `test/infrastructure/helpers/announce_channel.dart` → `AnnounceChannel.arm(tester)`.** First test category for the `SemanticsService.announce` family (zero existed before). Mechanism: `SemanticsService.announce(msg, dir)` Standard-codec-encodes `{type:'announce', data:{message: String, textDirection: int}}` onto the **outgoing** platform channel `flutter/accessibility`; intercept via `tester.binding.defaultBinaryMessenger.setMockDecodedMessageHandler<dynamic>(BasicMessageChannel('flutter/accessibility', StandardMessageCodec()), handler)`, decode the map, append `data['message']`. API: `.messages`/`.last`/`.count`/`.reset()` (forget but keep intercepting — needed for "no FURTHER announce" idiom) + `.dispose()` (auto-registered as `addTearDown` by `arm`). **HONESTY GATE: this intercepts the REAL channel the REAL `SemanticsService.announce` writes to — it does NOT stub `SemanticsService`. Always assert against the live `context.l10n.<key>` resolved from the pumped tree (via a `Builder`), never a hardcoded literal — survives ARB copy edits.** `createLocalizedTestApp` pins `sv`, so a sanity literal check (`a11yOcrComplete == 'Textavläsning klar'`) is a fine extra canary but never the primary assertion.
- **Coverage decision (5 announce sites, only photo_import was achievable):** the achievable site is the one whose VM comes from `ServiceLocator.get<T>()` — register a fake `extends ChangeNotifier implements T` (+ `noSuchMethod`) in `GetIt.instance` as a `registerFactory`, then bridge `app_provider.ServiceLocator.reset(); ...initialize(DIContainer())` (DIContainer reads the same GetIt). The view's `initState` resolves the fake; drive it with an `emit({hasOcrResult, isProcessing})` that calls `notifyListeners()` so the REAL VM listener (`_announceOcrCompletion`) + real announce run. Keep `hasImage==false` to dodge the HeirloomSection/image-preview render branches and isolate the announce.
- **Photo_import announce-once GUARD is the highest-value contract** (`_announcedOcr` bool): four tests pin it — announce-once on extract→review, NO re-announce on duplicate same-state notify (guard holds), RE-announce after `isProcessing` flips true→complete (guard re-arms per-extraction), silent while `isProcessing==true`.
- **Documented-deferred (harness-ready, not forced into brittle tests): collaborative-shopping toggle + recipe favorite toggle.** Both announce sites sit behind a view that constructs its REAL VM internally with NO injection seam: `CollaborativeShoppingView` uses `ChangeNotifierProvider(create: () => CollaborativeShoppingViewModel(shoppingService: ServiceLocator.get()))` (the `_FakeCollaborativeShoppingViewModel` from the items-widget test CANNOT reach the view's private `_toggleItem` announce — the view ignores any injected VM), and `RecipeDetailView` (610+ lines) needs UserService+PermissionService+UnifiedRecipeService+CookSnap+providers + a deep SliverAppBar tap. The announce decision in both is a thin read of already-VM-tested state (`completedItemsList` / `recipe.isFavorite`) → exactly the `ui-conventions.md:84` heavy-VM-scaffolding a11y exemption. comment-posted (`recipe_social_handler.postComment` static + ServiceLocator + network) likewise deferred. **The harness is ready the moment any of these views grows a VM-injection ctor seam or gets a `test/views/` journey test** — drop `AnnounceChannel.arm` into that journey beat.

### 2026-06-21 — feed_tab_navigation_test: NavigatorObserver captured.last safety [Pattern — reviewed]
`MaterialApp` pushes exactly one implicit route (the `home:`) before the test tap, so `navObserver.didPush` is called once at boot. `captured.last` in `verify(() => navObserver.didPush(captureAny(), any())).captured` therefore equals the SECOND call (the test's tap) only when pumpWidget is followed by pumpAndSettle BEFORE the tap — which settles the boot push first and thus the final `captured.last` IS the tap route. This is sound, not accidental, when `buildApp()` uses `home:` (not `initialRoute:`) and the tap is the only post-boot push. **If `buildApp` switches to `initialRoute:` + `onGenerateRoute` (which pushes named routes sequentially at start), the boot call count changes and `captured.last` may require adjusting — document this as a fragility.** Also confirmed: `fetchFriendRecipe` has no concrete `@override` body in `MockUnifiedRecipeService` (grep returned empty) → mocktail `when()` stubs work cleanly.

### 2026-06-19 — RecipeStatsRepository + RecipeOverviewViewModel [Pattern — reviewed]
**`collectionGroup` on `FakeFirebaseFirestore` IS safe for index-free `.limit(N).get()` queries.** The knowledge-file decision tree lists `collectionGroup` as emulator territory; that is a conservative default. `FakeFirebaseFirestore` handles plain collectionGroup fetches without a `where()` filter or composite index — the repo's `.collectionGroup('recipes').limit(5000).get()` is in that safe subset. Using the fake here is correct and avoids emulator-lane overhead for what is purely a Dart-classification problem.
**Boundary choice for two-layer tests (repo+fake-Firestore / VM+fake-repo):** seed + real `RecipeStatsRepository` for classification logic; inject a `_FakeRecipeStatsRepository extends RecipeStatsRepository` (concrete override of `getRecipeStats`) for VM tests. This is the right split — the classification switch is owned by the repo, not the VM.
**One missing case:** the `default:` arm of `_classify` (unknown/unrecognised type string → manual) has no test. A single extra seed document like `await _seedRecipe(db, 'u1', 'rX', 'legacyUnknown')` in the classification test would close this. Not a blocker pre-launch, but worth adding when the test is next touched — a future dev adding a new `case` arm might accidentally move 'legacyUnknown' to a non-manual bucket without noticing.
- Files: `test/infrastructure/helpers/announce_channel.dart`, `test/widget/a11y/announce_channel_harness_test.dart` (4 harness smoke tests), `test/widget/a11y/photo_import_announce_test.dart` (4 guard-contract tests). 8/8 green.

**MENU-05 swap scoring: the existing `menu_generator_test.dart` covered the cuisine-preference path probabilistically (20 iterations). It did NOT pin the HIERARCHY: cuisine(+3) > category(+2) > seasonal(+1) > zero.** Added `menu_generator_swap_scoring_test.dart` with 5 deterministic tests that:
- Run 15 iterations each (the score gap between adjacent tiers is ≥1 point — a single winner always dominates; 15 runs catches any shuffle bug reliably).
- Use `withClock(Clock.fixed(DateTime(2026,1)), ...)` to pin January → `SeasonUtils` returns `'vinter'` — seasonal tests are deterministic regardless of when CI runs.
- Pin the tie-break bucket: two equal-score candidates must BOTH be reachable in 50 iterations.
**Lesson: `MockMenuService` lives in `test/infrastructure/mocks/service_mocks.dart`, not `production_mocks.dart` — grep both files before concluding a mock class doesn't exist.**
**MENU-14 SharedShoppingListsViewModel: no test existed at all.** VM is thin (ctor triggers `loadSharedLists`, exposes `sharedLists` list, delegates error to `StateNotifierMixin.hasError`). Harness: `MockUnifiedShoppingService.setShoppingState(collaborativeLists: [...])` + `when(() => mock.loadLists()).thenAnswer((_) async {})` + `Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero)` to drain the ctor-fired async. Four tests: success with lists, empty result, service throws → hasError, refreshData replaces stale data. `_collabList` helper uses the real `UnifiedShoppingList.collaborative` factory (avoids positional ctor sprawl).

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1342 groups/messaging VM coverage (GRP-06/GRP-09/GRP-11) [Pattern]

**GRP-06 `CreateGroupConversationViewModel`** — no prior test existed. Key lessons:
- The VM uses `AppLocale.current` for validation-error strings but `AppLocale` defaults to Swedish at static init, so no locale setup is needed in unit tests — `validationError` is just asserted `isNotNull` (locale-agnostic), not matched against a literal.
- `UnifiedFriendsService` has concrete `@override` bodies on `MockUnifiedFriendsService.friends` (a `List<UserProfile> get` override), which would block `when(() => mock.friends).thenReturn(...)`. Use a local `_MockFriendsService extends Mock implements UnifiedFriendsService` with NO concrete bodies — same pattern as `add_members_to_group_viewmodel_test.dart`.
- `canCreateGroup` is the load-bearing invariant: test it as the aggregate gate (name + count) rather than testing `_validateGroupCreation()` directly (private). A refactor splitting the private method should not break the tests.
- The service-call argument-capture pattern via `verify(...captureAny(named: '...'))` is the right way to assert which participant names + trimmed title were forwarded.

**GRP-09 `ChatViewModel` poll/vote path** — already fully covered in `chat_viewmodel_test.dart` (5 tests: multi-choice metadata resolved, single-choice, no-op on missing message, no-op on missing poll metadata, closePoll delegate). No new tests needed; counted as already-covered.

**GRP-11 `ConversationsViewModel.startDirectConversation` edge cases** — happy path + error + disposed + loading already existed. Added three edge cases appended directly to `conversations_viewmodel_test.dart`:
1. **Get-or-create idempotency**: service called twice with same userId returns same ID both times; VM forwards both calls (dedup is the service's responsibility, not the VM's).
2. **Self-conversation rejection surfaces error**: service throws on self-ID → VM returns null + sets error + `isLoading==false`. Proves the VM does not silently swallow the error.
3. **Clears prior error on successful retry**: a failed attempt sets error; a successful retry clears it. Guards `executeAsync` error-clearing semantics.

**Decision record — what was deferred:**
- GRP-12 `ChatViewFacade` is view-layer (flagged as per original brief).
- GRP-09 poll-creation path is service-level (`MessagingService.sendPoll`); VM only resolves metadata + delegates — already covered.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-431 deferred-bootstrap coverage assessment [Pattern — CLEAN verdict]

**Pattern: static-method orchestrators with no injection seam are deliberately untestable in isolation — document the constraint instead of manufacturing a seam.**

`runDeferredBootstrap()` in `app_modules.dart` calls two static methods (`PerformanceModule.runInitSideEffects` + `ContentStage.enrichIngredientRegistry`) inside separate `try/catch` blocks. Both callees reach into `GetIt.instance` and Firebase — no injection seam exists. Testing `runDeferredBootstrap()` directly would require either a real DI+Firebase setup (too heavy for a unit test) or adding a callable/typedef seam purely for testability (production-code complexity in service of a trivially readable 12-line function).

**Verdict: CLEAN.** The 22 existing bootstrap tests cover the orchestrator contract (`application_bootstrap_test.dart`). The deferred-bootstrap change adds:
- Two static boolean flags gating `if (!defer...) return` paths — visible-by-reading, not worth a test.
- `try/catch` wrappers in `runDeferredBootstrap()` — each callee already has internal guards (`tryGet` returns null safely; `PerformanceMonitoringService` resolves from GetIt); verifying that `try/catch` catches exceptions is tautological.

**Decision rule for similar cases:** A static-method orchestrator that (a) is a thin sequence of ≤3 calls, (b) has no injection seam, (c) wraps each call in its own `try/catch`, and (d) has no branching logic other than the catch clauses — does NOT need a test. The "would a future refactor silently drop one call?" risk is real but cheaply addressed by a code review, not a test that would require Firebase scaffolding to be meaningful.

### 2026-06-21 — RecipeShareRequestModule: idempotency + accept contract review [Pattern — reviewed]

`request_recipe_share_test.dart` (6 tests) + `social_recipe_service_test.dart` (52 tests) — both pass 6/6 and 52/52.

**Idempotency proof is correct but uses a manual toggle, not a second call-count proof.** The test seeds `requestRepo.existingPending = true` AFTER the first call and verifies `created` stays length 1 and `sendCount` stays 1. This is behaviorally sound: it proves the module reads the repo's dedup query before writing. The alternative ("call it twice with real in-memory state") would also work but requires the Fake to auto-flip its `existingPending` on first write — the explicit-toggle approach is simpler and equally rigorous.

**`acceptRecipeShareRequest` proves all three critical properties:** (1) `recipeId` forwarded to `shareRecipe`, (2) `fromUserId` used as `memberIds` (requester identity), (3) status NOT flipped when `shareResult == null` (atomic guard). These are the exact invariants the docstring promises.

**NotificationService is resolved via `ServiceLocator.tryGet<>()` at call-time** (not constructor-injected). The test handles this correctly: it registers `notifier` into `GetIt.instance` in `setUp` and unregisters it in `tearDown`. This is the right approach for a `tryGet` pattern — no fake risk of the production code bypassing the injected dep.

**No false-confidence risks found.** All fakes are `extends Fake` (not `extends Mock` with `@override` bodies). The `_FakeSocialRecipeOperations` records calls in a `List<Map>` not a Mock — clean. The `_SpyNotificationService` records in-band data correctly.

**Sibling `social_recipe_service_test.dart` update is minimal and correct:** adds `_FakeSocialRequestRepository extends Fake implements FirebaseSocialRequestRepository` (a bare stub satisfying the new ctor param) + wires it at the SUT construction site. No existing test coverage is affected.

**One minor observation (not a gap):** the idempotency test's first call relies on `existingPending = false` (the Fake's default). This is fine but slightly implicit — adding a `reason: 'existingPending is false by default'` comment on the Fake field would make the assumption self-documenting. Not required for approval.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1334 AccountDeletionService: production-ServiceLocator bridge for post-CF assertions [Pattern]

**Problem:** `AccountDeletionService` resolves `NotificationService` via `prod.ServiceLocator.get<NotificationService>()` (the production ServiceLocator backed by `DIContainer → GetIt.instance`). Registering a mock into `GetIt.instance` directly is NOT enough if the production `ServiceLocator._container` is null — the lookup throws and the wrapping try-catch silently swallows it, so the mock is never called.

**Fix:** For tests that must verify a `ServiceLocator.get`-resolved dep: call `BaseUnitTest.setupUnitWithProductionLocator()` in `setUpAll` instead of plain `setupUnit()`. This calls `prod.ServiceLocator.initialize(DIContainer())`, and since `DIContainer._container` IS `GetIt.instance`, mocks registered in `GetIt.instance` are then reachable from the production `ServiceLocator.get<T>()` call.

**Decision rule:** If the SUT resolves a dependency via `ServiceLocator.get` (not constructor injection) and you need to verify that call, use `setupUnitWithProductionLocator()`. If the call is fire-and-forget inside a try-catch (non-fatal), the test is still worth writing — the try-catch only protects production from crashes; the test verifies the intent.

**Split into a second `group`** when the extra `setUpAll`/`tearDownAll` lifecycle would conflict with the primary group (which uses plain `setupUnit()`). The two groups can coexist in the same file.

**Tests added to `account_deletion_service_test.dart` (BUT-1334 criteria):**
- crit-1: `errors` list is exactly `['No authenticated user']` (length 1, exact string) — stricter than the pre-existing Test 5 (`contains('No authenticated')`).
- crit-2: CF `unauthenticated` exception → `requiresReauth == true` — the second re-auth branch in `_handleCfException`, previously untested.
- crit-3: BOTH search-index AND offline-cache cleanup run before the CF call — `verifyInOrder([search.removeUser, offline.clearUserData, callable.call])`.
- crit-4 (separate group with `setupUnitWithProductionLocator`): CF success → `notifService.resetForLogout()` called AND `auth.signOut()` called.

### 2026-06-21 — feed_tab_navigation Task 9: dialog+verify+snackbar pattern [Pattern — reviewed]

**All three tests pass (3/3 green). Task 9 null-branch is behaviourally correct and approvable.**

Key findings from this review:

1. **`MockSocialRecipeService` has NO concrete `@override` body for `requestRecipeShare`** — grep confirms it. The `when()` stub in the test will take effect cleanly; mocktail is not silently blocked.

2. **`find.text('Be om receptet').last` ambiguity is a known, documented, acceptable workaround** — `feedRequestRecipeTitle` and `feedRequestRecipeConfirm` are the SAME Swedish string ("Be om receptet") by deliberate product design. The `.last` targets the second match which is the actions-row button. If the title/confirm strings ever diverge (or the l10n key changes), the `find.text` call will break with a clear error — self-healing fragility, not silent false-confidence.

3. **`verify(...).called(1)` + `find.byType(SnackBar)` together prove both halves of the contract.** `called(1)` proves the service was invoked exactly once with the exact ownerId/recipeId/recipeTitle from the event. `find.byType(SnackBar)` proves the success snackbar rendered. Neither assertion is redundant.

4. **`find.byType(SnackBar)` is slightly broad** — it would also pass if an ERROR snackbar appeared. The strictest form would be `find.text(context.l10n.feedRecipeRequestSent)` but that requires a `Builder`/context capture. The current form is acceptable because `requestRecipeShare` is stubbed to return `true`, making the error path unreachable; the test intent is still clearly stated in the reason string.

5. **Pre-existing tests (happy path + own-recipe) are unaffected.** `_navigateToRecipe` was already updated to accept `ActivityEvent` in Task 5; the tests tap `find.text(_recipeTitle.toLowerCase())` which is a render-level assertion requiring no change when the method signature changed — proving the test was written at the right abstraction level.

**Pattern to remember**: when a dialog title and confirm button share the same l10n string, use `.last` to target the button; document this in an inline comment (the test does). The alternative of `find.byKey(...)` or `find.widgetWithText(TextButton, ...)` are more precise but require production-code key injection — not worth it when the root cause is deliberate l10n design.

**One gap (non-blocking):** the `requestRecipeShare returns false` branch (shows error snackbar via `SnackBarUtils.showError`) has no test. This is an optional follow-up; the happy-path coverage is complete.

### 2026-06-20 — WS5 UnifiedShoppingItem.copyWith regression tests: one gap identified [Review]
**Two regression tests correctly prove their stated contracts.** "editing a bought item preserves purchase attribution" exercises the exact pre-fix bug path (`bought` param is `null` → `effectiveBought = this.bought = true`, `becameBought = false` → `purchasedByUserId` preserved); would fail against the bug. "unmarking clears attribution" exercises `effectiveBought = false` → all three fields null; would fail against an inverted `else null` branch. Neither is fragile to harmless refactors. The `togglePurchased` group (lines 212–252) covers the "marking a fresh item stamps the buyer" contract at the canonical call site (production always uses `togglePurchased`, not `copyWith(bought:true)`, for the purchase action).

**One meaningful gap: idempotent re-mark must not re-stamp.** `copyWith(bought: true)` on an item that is ALREADY `bought: true` should preserve `purchasedAt` and `purchasedByUserId` (the original buyer, not a new one). The correct production behaviour is `becameBought = bought == true && !this.bought = true && !true = false` → attributions preserved. If that condition regresses to `bought == true`, every no-op "re-mark" silently re-stamps `purchasedAt` and overwrites the buyer — user-visible in collaborative lists. No existing test catches it. **Recommended addition (one test, three assertions):**
```dart
test('re-marking an already-bought item does not re-stamp purchasedAt or overwrite buyer', () {
  final first = testItem.copyWith(
    bought: true, lastModifiedByUserId: 'buyer_1', lastModifiedByDisplayName: 'Buyer One');
  final firstAt = first.purchasedAt;
  // Re-mark as bought (no-op transition) with a DIFFERENT caller identity.
  final remarked = first.copyWith(
    bought: true, lastModifiedByUserId: 'buyer_2', lastModifiedByDisplayName: 'Buyer Two');
  expect(remarked.purchasedByUserId, equals('buyer_1'), reason: 're-mark must not overwrite original buyer');
  expect(remarked.purchasedByDisplayName, equals('Buyer One'));
  expect(remarked.purchasedAt, equals(firstAt), reason: 're-mark must not re-stamp the purchase time');
});
```
**UI-only WS5 fixes (error snackbar, empty-state):** no widget test required. Both are simple rendering conditionals with no state machine or ViewModel path to test — the DO-NOT-WRITE "assert a static widget shows static text" pattern applies.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1347 web-only services: stub-contract vs state-machine untestability [Pattern]

**PwaInstallService / RecipePrintService (ENG-25/26):** Both services use the `export 'stub' if (dart.library.js_interop) 'web'` pattern. The web implementations import `dart:js_interop` + `package:web`, which means they **only compile under a browser target**. A regular `flutter test` (VM/native) resolves to the stub. Therefore:

- **The stub no-op contract IS testable** and is worth testing: proves the service is safe to call cross-platform without crashes. Tests: `canPromptInstall` is always false, `onInitialize`/`promptInstall`/`dismissInstall` complete without throwing, state stays consistent after dismiss on non-web.
- **The web state machine (session counter, dismiss flag) and the HTML generator are NOT testable** in a standard unit test environment without either (a) targeting the web compilation or (b) extracting pure logic into a platform-agnostic Dart file. Do not write fake test doubles that reproduce the production logic — that tests the double, not the production code.
- **The `_buildRecipeHtml` / `_esc` helpers in the web file are pure Dart** (no `dart:js_interop` usage) but are private top-level functions. They cannot be accessed from tests without either `@visibleForTesting` or extraction. Flag in the test file so the next dev knows this is a refactor gap, not a test gap.
- **Decision rule for `export … if (dart.library.js_interop)` services:** always test the stub (no-op safety), then note clearly what the web path would need to be testable. Don't add empty tests pretending to cover logic you can't reach.

Files: `test/unit/services/pwa_install_service_test.dart`, `test/unit/services/recipe_print_service_test.dart`.

### 2026-06-19 — MetricRenderer empty-mount suite review [Pattern — reviewed]
**The every-kind-empty-mount pattern is correct for sealed-type renderers where the main risk is crash-on-zero-data.** `tester.takeException()` on each kind loop is the right idiom — it catches thrown exceptions during build and layout. `createLocalizedTestApp` is the right boundary because `MetricRenderer` calls `context.l10n.adminMetricNoData` on the empty path; without real l10n the test would throw before reaching the assert.
**One structural gap (acceptable today, watch if diverges):** `SeriesMetric([])` and `FunnelMetric([])` have their no-crash asserted but their placeholder text is NOT independently checked. Both share `_LabelValueTable` (same `rows.isEmpty` branch as `BreakdownMetric`, which has an explicit `find.text('Ingen data än')` in test 3). Safe today because the branch is shared; becomes a real hole if the three kinds ever get separate empty-state paths. **If SeriesMetric or FunnelMetric is ever given its own render widget, add a dedicated empty-text assertion at that point.**
**ScalarMetric omitted from the loop is correct by design** — `ScalarMetric.isEmpty` is always `false` (model doc-comment explicit); a zero-value scalar is still data, not an empty state. No action needed.
**MatrixMetric empty path uses a distinct widget (`_MatrixTable`) from the other three (`_LabelValueTable`) — the no-crash assert covers it, but placeholder-text visibility is not independently pinned.** Low priority since the `isEmpty` guard is a 4-line branch that clearly renders `adminMetricNoData`; add a dedicated matrix-empty-text test if `_MatrixTable` grows complexity.

### 2026-06-21 — permission-denied branch in cross-user Firestore reads: acceptable FakeFirebaseFirestore omission [Pattern — reviewed]

**Context:** `readSharedRecipe` (and any cross-user read that catches `FirebaseException(code:'permission-denied') → null`) has three outcome branches: doc-present→Recipe, doc-absent→null, permission-denied→null. FakeFirebaseFirestore cannot fire `permission-denied`; the emulator can.

**Decision rule:** when the permission-denied branch is (a) a single `if (e.code == 'permission-denied') return null;` above a `rethrow`, (b) the happy-path and absent-doc paths are covered by FakeFirebaseFirestore tests, and (c) no additional logic (side-effects, notifications, error state) executes in the denied branch — the omission in the unit test is **acceptable and should be documented in the library doc**, NOT papered over with a brittle Firestore-level mock. The correct closure is a separate emulator-lane integration test (`skip: emulatorOnlySkip`) exercising real Firestore rules, filed as a follow-up. Seed via real `.toFirestore()` (not a hand-rolled map) to exercise the deserialization round-trip.

**Contrasted with:** `FirebaseShoppingRepository` delete permission bypass (BUT-369) — that test was worth writing because the guard was a NON-TRIVIAL multi-condition check on `ownerId`. A trivial single-string-equality catch is the dividing line.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-918 "What we log" static disclosure — CONSCIOUS SKIP [Pattern]
**A static GDPR disclosure built from compile-time constant references needs NO test — record it as a conscious skip, don't manufacture a smoke test.** `consent_management_view.dart._buildAnalyticsTransparency` adds an `ExpansionTile` listing 6 l10n category labels each followed by `[AnalyticsEvents.appOpened, ...].join(', ')`. No branches, no state, no VM interaction. The only "contract" — disclosure stays in sync with `analytics_events.dart` — is **compiler-enforced** (a renamed constant fails `flutter analyze`, the test never even runs), and l10n-key existence is covered by `flutter gen-l10n`. The three things a widget test here could assert are all negative-value:
- `find.byType(ExpansionTile)` → topology assertion (BUT-368 anti-pattern); breaks on a harmless swap to a custom disclosure widget showing identical content.
- a literal event-string (`'app_opened'`) renders → hardcodes the constant's VALUE, duplicating the compile guarantee with a brittle copy that breaks on a harmless value change.
- "the static widget shows static text" → the exact BUT-368 anti-pattern verbatim.
Harness cost is real and buys nothing: mocked `ConsentViewModel` + production-ServiceLocator bridge + `createLocalizedTestApp` + pumping past the post-frame `loadConsent()` loading gate, all to drive UI with zero coupling to any of it. **Decision rule reaffirmed (cf. 2026-05-04 BUT-582): test changes where a regression SILENTLY changes user-visible behaviour AND the seam is cheap. A static disclosure whose sync-contract is compile-enforced fails BOTH halves.** Visual/expanded-layout protection, if ever wanted, belongs in a consent-flow journey test (`test/views/`, Phase 6 territory), not a per-widget mechanical test. The right design move was already made in production: referencing `AnalyticsEvents.*` constants instead of string literals is exactly WHY no test is needed.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-977 single-IconButton nav affordance = NO TEST [Pattern]
Assessed an uncommitted `lib/views/mina_recept_view.dart` change: ONE `IconButton` added to the header `actions: [...]`, `onPressed: () => Navigator.of(context).pushNamed(Routes.ingredientSearch)`. No VM/logic/branch change; `Routes.ingredientSearch` pre-existing + registered in 3 lists. **Verdict: nothing blocks commit, no new test warranted.** Reasoning chain (reusable for any pure-nav affordance on a heavy view):
- A button whose body is `pushNamed(<const route>)` = framework navigation wiring, same "is it subscribed / does it forward to an already-tested target" class as BUT-754 — needs no test of its own. The destination view is already reachable/exercised via another entry point (here Cmd+K).
- The route constant is **compile-time checked** — a missing/renamed `Routes.X` fails `flutter analyze`, not at runtime, so there's no silent-regression seam to guard.
- A `find.byIcon(...)` + NavigatorObserver test would require full view scaffolding (VM + UserService + multi-Provider + ServiceLocator + router) to assert one `pushNamed` → cost/value inverted, and it's exactly the BUT-368 topology-assert anti-pattern (breaks on icon swap / header restructure, proves no user behavior).
- ui-conventions.md line 84 is explicit: **skip heavy-ViewModel-scaffolding widget tests; covered by `tools/audit_unwrapped_tap_targets.dart` + manual passes.** `IconButton` is in the self-labeling a11y allow-list (line 34) and carried a `tooltip` → audit script satisfied, no `Semantics` wrapper needed.
- Localized `tooltip` (`context.l10n.ingredientSearchTitle`) is compile-checked too — no hardcoded-Swedish (BUT-1088/1115 family) risk. **General rule: a view-layer change that adds a const-route nav entry point with no new logic does not earn a widget test; cite ui-conventions.md line 84 and the compile-time route guard.**

### 2026-06-14 — VeckomenyCookingSessionCard wiring-test (BUT-1302) review [Pattern]
Reviewed `test/widget/widgets/menu/veckomeny_cooking_session_card_test.dart` (new with the `8b91c165b` veckomeny-widgets extract). 5/5 green. **Exemplary scope decision and worth pinning as the template for "a presence widget that merges streams + owns a holder":** the file tests ONLY what the widget itself adds — the three short-circuit guards (`module==null`, `userId==null`, `groups.isEmpty` → `SizedBox.shrink`) plus the two StreamBuilder outcomes (own-session-only collapses the inner card; a friend's session flows through to a rendered `CookingSessionCard` with the name surfacing). It correctly does NOT re-test the merge/filter/sort/dedup logic (owned by `test/unit/widgets/cooking/cooking_session_stream_test.dart` — 8 tests incl. self-filter) or the compose-line cases (owned by `test/widget/cooking/cooking_session_card_test.dart` — single/merge/step-suffix). **Before flagging "the merge path is under-tested in the widget test," grep `test/` for the collaborator class names — the deep logic usually lives in a sibling and duplicating it here would be rot.** Fakes are `extends Fake` (not Mock+@override bodies — BUT-368-correct), stubbing only the two data sources (`UnifiedFriendsService.currentUserId`/`categoriesList`, `CookingSessionModule.watchGroupSessions`), not the widget's own logic — no mocked-away-subject. Uses the production-ServiceLocator-bridge pattern (`GetIt.registerSingleton` + `production.ServiceLocator.initialize(DIContainer())`). Avoids `pumpAndSettle` on the populated path (the rendered card's `PulseDot` loops forever) — two bare `pump()`s drain the single-value stream; documented inline. **Genuine thin-coverage gaps this file owns but doesn't cover (all Medium/Low, none High — no untested NEW behavior, the deeper logic is in tested siblings):**
- **No rebuild test** — the whole reason `CookingSessionStreamHolder` exists is no-re-subscribe-on-rebuild (per its header comment). The holder's idempotent-`refresh` is unit-tested, but nothing proves the WIDGET feeds identical inputs across `build` calls (i.e. the holder + widget integrate correctly). A `tester.pumpWidget(... same child ...)` second pump asserting `find.byType(StreamBuilder)` stays the same instance / no snapshot blank would close it. Medium.
- **No multi-group merge through the widget** — every populated test wires a single group `g1` owned by the user. The production `groups` filter has a SECOND branch `friendUserIds.contains(userId)` (user is a MEMBER not owner) that is untested at ANY level for this widget; a two-group fixture where the user is a member of group 2 would exercise it + the holder's cross-group dedup end-to-end. Medium.
- **No dispose/unmount test** — the widget's only added lifecycle line is `_sessionsHolder.dispose()`; pumping then removing the widget to confirm no throw is low value (holder dispose is unit-tested) but it's the one line the widget owns. Low.
- `find.textContaining('Erik')` is a name-substring assert (not weakened — the full line is pinned in `cooking_session_card_test.dart`); acceptable division of labor, just note it would survive an eyebrow/recipe-render break. Info.
- Standard BUT-1180 scaffolding reused: tall surface (1200x2600) + scoped `FlutterError.onError` ignoring ONLY `'A RenderFlex overflowed'`; `MockOfflineService` (isOnline→true, addListener/removeListener stubbed) for `offlineIndicator()`; the auto-registered `FakePermissionService` already reports `currentUserId == 'test-user-123'` (no override needed — match your fixtures' currentUser to it). This completes BUT-1180 (6/6 files green).

### 2026-06-20 — WS8 GDPR Art 8 age-gate compliance tests review [Review — Pattern]
**The compliance contract (gate locked until explicit pick) is directly and robustly proven at both the ViewModel and widget levels.**

VM test ("does not auto-select a birth year on init"): constructs `OnboardingViewModel()` with no prior `setBirthYear` call and immediately asserts `selectedBirthYear isNull` + `isAgeGatePassed isFalse`. If someone re-added `_selectedBirthYear = DateTime.now().year - 30` in the ctor (or in any `_init`/`postFrameCallback`), the `isNull` line fails before reaching the second assertion.

Widget test ("does not auto-select a year on render"): pumps `OnboardingAgeGatePage` with a fresh VM, runs full `pumpAndSettle` (exercises `initState`, `didChangeDependencies`, first `build`), then asserts the SAME two fields. A page-level auto-seed in `initState` would fail here even if the VM ctor was clean.

**Three supporting cases are sound:**
- Adult-pick (VM line 221): `setBirthYear(now.year - 30)` → `isAgeGatePassed isTrue`. Clear causal chain.
- Under-15 (VM line 226): uses `now.year - 10` (age 10), well inside the under-15 band — no off-by-one risk of a false pass on the threshold itself.
- Clear-re-locks (VM line 234): sets an adult year, asserts open, `setBirthYear(null)`, asserts re-locked. Covers "change your mind" UX.

**One meaningful gap: exact age threshold (15) is not pinned.** The compliance tests use age 10 and age 30/16 — neither exercises `age == 15` (should pass) or `age == 14` (should fail). If `minAgeYears` changed from 15 → 16 or `>=` flipped to `>`, all existing tests stay green. If the exact threshold is a hard legal requirement, add:
```dart
test('exactly age 15 passes; exactly age 14 does not (GDPR Art 8 threshold)', () {
  viewModel.setBirthYear(DateTime.now().year - 15);
  expect(viewModel.isAgeGatePassed, isTrue, reason: 'age 15 is the legal minimum');
  viewModel.setBirthYear(DateTime.now().year - 14);
  expect(viewModel.isAgeGatePassed, isFalse, reason: 'age 14 is under the threshold');
});
```
Not a blocker — the gate is definitively locked on null — but the boundary should be pinned if the 15-year cutoff is itself a compliance requirement rather than a design choice that could drift.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-925/1244 parse_confidence_review widget test assessment [Pattern + Gap]
14/14 green. Five design behaviours under review:
- **Bar colour per confidence**: real — reads `Container.color` from a `ValueKey` test hook, compares to static `ButleryColors.light.success/warning/neutral`. Would catch wrong-token, wrong-widget regressions. Fragile point: the `ValueKey` is embedded in production code; removing it breaks the finder before any colour assertion runs (document as known fragile spot).
- **No pill labels**: real — `find.text('HÖG')` etc. + English variants, covers CI locale.
- **Subtitle counts / hidden when all-high**: real, but the "all-high" assertion checks `findTextContaining(subtitle(0))` which is always-absent even when the guard is correct. More explicit form: assert no subtitle string at ALL (e.g. `findsNothing` on `find.textContaining('ingrediens')`). Add a comment explaining the indirect logic.
- **Whitespace-only suppression vs genuine difference**: real — exercises `_stripped()` boundary on both sides.
- **A11y semantics**: real — uses `ensureSemantics()` + live l10n. **GAP: only `high` and `low` tested; `medium` and `failed` arms of the production switch are uncovered.** Two cheap additions close it.
**Action taken**: none (assessment-only task). Flag to add medium+failed a11y tests in same file.

### 2026-06-19 — metric-registry key-lock + l10n-pure-function resolver pattern [Pattern reviewed]
**Key-lock test (`registry_keys_test.dart`):** each registry is compared against `MetricKey.values.toSet()` INDEPENDENTLY — not cross-compared. This catches all three drift directions (add to enum only; add to one registry only; add to two registries only). The enum is the single source of truth. `MetricKey.category` exhaustiveness is enforced by the Dart compiler's exhaustive-switch check, not by a test — correct division of responsibility.
**`AppLocalizations.delegate.load(Locale('sv'))` in `setUpAll` for l10n-pure tests:** the right pattern when the SUT is a pure function `(InsightsData, AppLocalizations) → MetricValue`. `WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()` is mandatory before the `delegate.load()` call (the delegate touches platform channels). Do NOT use `createLocalizedTestApp` here — pumping a widget tree to get an `AppLocalizations` reference when the function under test has no widget tree is noise. Reserve `createLocalizedTestApp` for widget tests that need `BuildContext.l10n`.
**Zero-fill invariant via row-count assertion:** `expect(rows.length, 5)` is a stronger guard than checking each zero individually — a missing zero-fill row lowers the count AND surfaces as a crash (null passed to `BreakdownRow`). Pair with an explicit `social.value == 0` spot-check on ONE absent key as a readability canopy.
**Display-order as a user-visible contract:** `rows.first` + `rows.last` labels pin the `_recipeMethodOrder` constant end-to-end through the real resolver — if the order is rewritten, exactly these two assertions fail. This IS a behavioral assertion (first/last in the rendered breakdown), not a topology assertion.

### 2026-06-19 — ParsingDetailsViewModel 6-test suite review [Pattern]
**Approved 6/6. Suite is exemplary for a read-only admin ViewModel with a cost-cap branch.** Key findings:
- `_FakeParsingRepo extends ParsingCorrectionRepository` with concrete `@override` bodies = correct Fake pattern (not Mock), consistent with BUT-368/369 guidance.
- The `_maxDetailDomains = 30` cost-cap is the ONE production branch not covered. A test with 31 domains where the 31st has corrections would prove that `topIssue` stays `none` beyond the cap while its count still accumulates into `totalCorrections`. Low risk pre-launch but is the gap to add if the cap logic ever changes.
- **`_dominantIssue` tie-breaking is non-deterministic**: uses `>=` in `reduce`, so a tie falls to the first key in map-iteration order (Dart `Map` iteration order is insertion order in practice but not guaranteed by the language spec for `HashMap`). The tests wisely avoid a tie — note this if you add a dominant-issue test with equal counts.
- **`_FakeParsingRepo` overrides only `getDomainStats` + `getByDomain`**; the rest of the repo is silent (no emulator, no `FakeFirebaseFirestore` reads exercised). This is correct — the ViewModel under test only calls those two methods.
- Pattern for future cost-cap tests: seed `entries.length > _maxDetailDomains` by building a map with `_maxDetailDomains + 1` keys, assert `.last.topIssue == ParsingIssue.none` and `totalCorrections` still equals the sum of all 31 counts.

### CPI→LoadingIndicator migration (BUT-885/1066/1168/1173, iter-113 + waves 2-4) [Pattern]
Canonical rule for mechanical indeterminate `CircularProgressIndicator → LoadingIndicator` swaps inside static builders (subsumes the 2026-05-30, 2026-06-01 iter-113, and 2026-06-01 wave-2 entries; covers `lib/widgets/` social/common/image files + the `lib/views/` BUT-861 `StateWidget.loading()` siblings):

- **A pure indeterminate swap is behavior-preserving and needs NO new widget test.** `LoadingIndicator → AdaptiveActivityIndicator` stays on the indeterminate `CircularProgressIndicator` path on non-iOS hosts (the `flutter test` default) IFF `value == null && backgroundColor == null`. `valueColor → color` is the equivalent indeterminate swap. The regression contract is the arch guard (`architecture_test.dart` "no raw CircularProgressIndicator in lib/widgets|views/", BUT-885/1066) — **de-allowlisting the migrated file + confirming the guard still passes IS the proof**, plus `loading_indicator_test.dart` (pins value!=null → determinate CPI carrying value/strokeWidth/backgroundColor; value==null/.small → AdaptiveActivityIndicator). A `find.byType(LoadingIndicator)` re-assert = a low-value getter-identity test.
- The swap adds a net-new `Semantics(liveRegion: true)` node (a11y improvement, not a behavior change).
- **`find.byType(CircularProgressIndicator)` SURVIVES the swap on the default non-iOS host** — it only changes under `Platform.isIOS` (→ `CupertinoActivityIndicator`); a CPI finder breaks ONLY if a test both renders a migrated widget AND forces iOS — verify neither. Remaining CPI finders are in `test/widget/common/indicators/` (wrappers — correct) + tests rendering their OWN spinners; view tests use the wrapper finder or an OR fallback (`LoadingIndicator || CircularProgressIndicator`, BUT-891).
- **The ONE swap that DOES need an extra test: any `value:` fed by a ternary/conditional/nullable-cast is a BEHAVIOR BRANCH, not a swap** — grep the diff for `value:` on every migrated call site. Straight pass-throughs (`value: progress`) are covered by the widget's own null/non-null tests. But `value: status.progress > 0 ? status.progress : null` is a real domain branch (progress==0 must render INDETERMINATE, not a stuck 0%-full arc) — assess as new logic needing a boundary test (the widget's hardcoded-literal tests never exercise the `>0 ? : null` boundary). **Spinner type alone never deserves a behavioral test; a determinate/boundary `value:` clamp does.**

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-912 online-status privacy gate [Pattern]
Assessed coverage for a privacy opt-out (`showOnlineStatus` bool). Verdict: model round-trip + backward-compat-default + service gate cover the actual enforcement — adequate, not blocked. Reusable rules:
- **The load-bearing privacy-gate test pairs `verify(write false)` with `verifyNever(write true)`.** The gate is `effective = isOnline && showOnlineStatus` (service reads the *fetched* profile). The `false`-write leg alone passes even with the gate dropped UNLESS something else writes; the `verifyNever(true)` directly kills the dropped-gate regression. `user_service_test.dart:418` is the reference — this is the highest-value test of the feature and was done right.
- **Default-true bool round-trip must cover BOTH legs.** A serializer hard-coding `false` (or dropping the key → default-true read path) passes a `false`-only round-trip. Loop `[true,false]`. The backward-compat absent→true test covers the default but NOT present-`true`→true.
- **A `copyWith(x:false)` test asserting ONLY the flipped field is vacuous** — passes if some *other* field is collaterally clobbered. Add one "unchanged" assert (uid / a sibling bool) per the file's existing copyWith convention (`user_profile_test.dart:145`). These are strengthenings, not corrections; current tests aren't wrong, just loose.
- **"View-state convenience" ≠ trivial wiring when the setter branches asymmetrically.** `updateShowOnlineStatus(false)` ALSO clears `isOnline` (immediate dot removal: `copyWith(showOnlineStatus:false, isOnline:false)`), while the `true` branch only sets the flag. The service gate canNOT catch a regression that drops the `isOnline:false` clause (gate reads the fetched profile, not edited VM state) → genuine untested invariant. Pure-sync ~2-test block (off clears isOnline; on doesn't resurrect it) — cheap enough to add, but a "should" not a blocker. The view SwitchListTile IS trivial wiring (Phase 6: no view test).
- **Grep tool artifact**: some `//` comment lines render with a leading `\` in Grep output (saw it on `user_service.dart:291` + `user_profile_viewmodel.dart:104`) — VERIFY via Read before flagging a "syntax error"; the source files were clean.

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-948 pantry multi-select: bulk-bar delete+undo wiring gap [Pattern + Gap]
**The classic stubbed-both-ends blind spot — here without the symmetry.** `pantry_viewmodel_bulk_test.dart` proves the VM's `bulkRemoveItems`/`restoreItems` contract (correct ids, no collateral delete, fresh ids on restore). `pantry_item_card_selection_test.dart` proves the card-level long-press/toggle/swipe-suppression contract. But `_PantryViewContent._deleteSelected` — the view method that (1) snapshots the removed items BEFORE the await, (2) calls `selection.clearSelection()` then `viewModel.bulkRemoveItems(ids)` in that order, (3) shows a `SnackBar` whose `onPressed` calls `viewModel.restoreItems(removed)` — is entirely untested at the widget level. Four distinct regressions are invisible to the current suite: (a) snapshot taken AFTER the await (items already gone, undo restores nothing); (b) `ScaffoldMessenger.maybeOf(context)` returns null in a host without a Scaffold (snackbar silently absent); (c) `onPressed` capturing a mutable reference instead of a snapshot (restores []); (d) `clearSelection()` not called before the delete (bar stays up during the async operation). This is P2 — the parallel with the existing swipe-undo widget test (`pantry_item_card_undo_test.dart`) is exact and that precedent exists precisely to catch wiring bugs like these. Closing test shape: pump `_PantryViewContent` (or a minimal `Stack(children:[...sections..., _PantryBulkBar(...)])`) with a mocked `PantryViewModel` and a real `PantrySelectionManager` pre-loaded with items; tap the delete button; assert `SnackBar` appears with "Ångra"; tap "Ångra"; `verify(() => mockVm.restoreItems(removed)).called(1)` where `removed` is the pre-seeded item list. **Note**: `_PantryBulkBar` is a private class — the test should either drive it through the `_PantryViewContent` integration or pump the bar widget directly via `find.byType` in a `tester.widget<_PantryBulkBar>(...)` call (not possible for a private class — drive at the `_PantryViewContent` seam). Separate observable: the single-item swipe undo test in `pantry_item_card_undo_test.dart` is fully behavioural and a good template. **Secondary observation**: `pantry_selection_manager_test.dart`'s `selectedIds is an unmodifiable view` test asserts the returned `Set`'s mutation throws `UnsupportedError` — an internal-representation detail; the observable safety property (callers can't corrupt state) would also be proven by asserting `selectedIds` after a caller-side mutate attempt shows no change in `selectedCount`/`isSelected`. Low priority, not wrong.

### 2026-06-20 — WS5 markdown renderer + heirloom-form mixin review [Review — Pattern]
Assessed `test/widget/views/legal/markdown_body_test.dart` (3 tests, new) and the new `hasHeirloomContent` group (3 tests) in `test/unit/viewmodels/photo_import/photo_import_heirloom_form_mixin_test.dart`.

**Both files are intention-driven and would fail on real regressions.**

Markdown tests: (1) "strips heading/bold markers" — a passthrough `SelectableText(data)` regression makes `find.textContaining('# ')` match, failing it; (2) "renders bullets with '•'" — leaking `- ` source fails `findsNothing`; (3) "link span carries TapGestureRecognizer" — correct behavioural probe (interactivity, not colour). The bare `MaterialApp` (no `AppTheme`) is safe because these tests assert only text presence and gesture recognizer, not colour. The TapGestureRecognizer walk is slightly broad (passes if ANY unrelated span happens to have a recognizer) but in the narrow test context there are no other tappable spans — acceptable.

hasHeirloomContent tests: (1) "false when toggle alone (no typed fields)" — pins that the UI confirmation dialog does NOT show for an accidental toggle; (2) "true for each single field independently with clearHeirloomForm between" — exercises all three `||` arms; a missing arm goes red; (3) "false again after clear" — closes the round-trip and pins that `clearHeirloomForm` resets the predicate, not only the display state.

**Skipped items verdict:**
- `SmartImportView` initialUrl prefill and deep-link route change: correctly skipped — both are post-frame/post-DI-init wiring with no cheap seam.
- Notification tap-nav: the static `onNotificationTapped` seam is potentially testable as a PURE routing-switchboard unit test (`data map → expected route string`) with no DI at all, IF the static method is a simple switch on `notification.data['type']`. Worth reading before permanently skipping — if the method reaches `ServiceLocator` before routing, skip is correct; if it is a pure data-map → route-string function, a 5-line unit test closes a real regression gap (a mistyped case key silently does nothing, DI-backed tests can't catch it).

**Decision rule added:** a static notification-tap router is cheaply tested as a pure function (input: a `RemoteMessage` data map; output: route string pushed or `null`) only if it does not call ServiceLocator before routing. Read the production method body before concluding either way. If the body is DI-entangled, the skip is correct per the recipe_selection_dialogs precedent.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1041 source-icon host-sniff helper [Pattern]
**`@visibleForTesting static IconData sourceIcon(String url)` is the RIGHT seam for a host→icon affordance** (`recipe_detail_shared_widgets.dart`). The host-sniff is a pure function; the Row/Flexible wrapping around it is layout that `buildTitleSection` only assembles after pulling UserService+ViewModel+Provider — exactly the heavy view-scaffolding ui-conventions.md says to skip. 8 unit tests pinning the host→icon contract (yt watch, youtu.be, m.youtube subdomain, tiktok, vm.tiktok, generic, hostless fallback, case) are adequate; do NOT demand a widget test of the Row. **Two cosmetic mislabels, NOT gaps (verified in Dart):** (1) `Uri.host` already lowercases per RFC 3986 normalization (`YouTube.com`→`youtube.com`), so production's `.toLowerCase()` is dead-defensive and the "case-insensitive" test passes with or without it — still a valid pin of user-visible behaviour, just not testing what its name implies. (2) `'not a url'` does NOT make `tryParse` return null — it parses to a relative-ref with empty host; the actual `tryParse→null` branch needs `'http://[invalid'`. Both the empty-host path and the null path land on `open_in_new` (identical user-visible result), and empty-host is already covered, so the unexercised `?? ''` null branch is a non-gap. **Verdict template for "pure helper extracted from a DI-heavy build method": cover the helper's contract at the helper, skip the widget assembly — and before claiming a missing edge case, run the input through `Uri.tryParse` in a scratch Dart file rather than assuming what parses to null vs empty.**

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-987 filter-panel manage-prefs deep-link [Pattern]
**Callback-gated contextual deep-link in a leaf StatelessWidget = exactly the right seam to test directly, NOT through the view.** `FiltersPanelWidget` is a pure `StatelessWidget` (no Provider/ServiceLocator); the new `if (onManageFoodPreferences != null) TextButton.icon(...)` render-gate + tap-wiring is the only logic the feature adds. 3 widget tests (renders-when-provided / tap-fires-callback / absent-when-null) via `createLocalizedTestApp(wrapInScrollView: true)` are adequate. The "absent-when-null" negative is load-bearing — pins the gate so a future refactor can't render a dead no-op button. **The MinaReceptView→SearchFilterWidget→FiltersPanelWidget threading is BUT-754 pass-through plumbing** (`onManageFoodPreferences: widget.onManageFoodPreferences`, no transform/conditional) — compile-time-checked named-param wiring, no own test earns its keep; the only unasserted bit is the `Routes.settingsAllergens` literal in the view closure (journey-test territory, skip per ui-conventions.md). **Latent brittleness flagged (NOT commit-blocking): the tests finder-key on `find.byIcon(Icons.tune)`, and `Icons.tune` is ALSO used by `FilterToggleButton`** — unique here ONLY because `FiltersPanelWidget` doesn't contain that toggle (it lives in the parent `SearchFilterWidget`). The robust finder is the localized label (`filterManageFoodPrefs` → "Hantera allergener & kost"), which keys on the feature's user-visible contract rather than a generic Material icon shared across the widget tree. **Pre-approve rule: when a widget test targets `find.byIcon(<genericMaterialIcon>)`, grep the package dir for other uses of that icon and confirm none co-render in the subject; prefer the localized label finder when the icon isn't feature-unique.**

### 2026-06-14 — RealtimeSyncService review: conflictStream gap CLOSED + dead-duplicate resolveConflict [Review + Gaps]
Re-reviewed `test/unit/services/realtime_sync_service_test.dart` against `lib/services/realtime_sync_service.dart` after iter commits 27c61e7aa (cache-coherence on conflict-loss) + d4ee51b0f (recoverLocalVersion gap-close). **The 2026-06-14 conflictStream entry above (line ~241, BUT-1031, "stubbed-both-ends blind spot") is now SUPERSEDED — the gap is CLOSED.** The `conflictStream (BUT-1265 live delivery end-to-end)` group (test file ~line 722) drives a REAL losing-local conflict through `updateResource → shouldResolveConflict → _conflictModule.resolveConflict → _emitConflict → onConflict → _conflictController.add` and asserts exactly one `ConflictEvent(chosenStrategy: remoteWon, docId, remoteValue.editCount: 9)` reaches a pre-subscribed `service.conflictStream` listener. That is the exact one-liner the prior entry prescribed; the wire-up is now intent-gated. The file is exemplary: real conflict resolution through `FakeFirebaseFirestore`, side-effects (cache + disk) asserted not just returns, correct local `_MockAuthRepository extends Mock` (shared one's concrete `currentUserId` blocks `when()`), recoverLocalVersion BUT-1263 (stamps `lastEditedBy`+`lastEditedAt`) + BUT-1264 (false-branch of the `remote.editCount > base` guard). Subject is NOT mocked away — only the auth repo (external collaborator) is.
Two REMAINING gaps (both Medium-or-below, neither commit-blocking — file is committed green):
- **`shouldResolveConflict` FALSE branches are untested at the service level** (`conflict_resolution_module.dart:40-58`). All conflict-path service tests drive the TRUE branch (recordLocalUpdate within 5s + remote `lastEditedAt.isAfter(lastUpdate)`). The three falses — (a) no prior `recordLocalUpdate` → `lastUpdate==null` → false; (b) >5000ms since the local update (`conflictResolutionWindowMs`) → false; (c) remote `lastEditedAt` NOT after `lastUpdate` → false — gate whether ANY write enters conflict resolution. A `<`→`<=` window slip, an `isAfter`→`isBefore` flip, or dropping the window check would make every back-to-back owner write spuriously conflict-resolve (or never), and no test in the file goes red. The module is constructor-pure (inject `getLatestResource` closure + `onConflict: emitted.add`) — cheapest to pin at the MODULE level via `withClock` per-call (hit 4999ms then 5000ms, pinning the strict `<`), mirroring the import_rate_limiter window pattern. Medium.
- **`RealtimeSyncService.resolveConflict<T>(T local, T remote)` (lib lines 377-409) is a DEAD DUPLICATE of `ConflictResolutionModule.resolveConflict` that OMITS the `_emitConflict` calls.** Grepped all `resolveConflict` callers: `updateResource` uses `_conflictModule.resolveConflict` (line 260); the viewmodel path (`realtime_recipe_viewmodel.dart:382` → `realtime_recipe_operations.dart:273`) is a DIFFERENT method (`resolveConflict({recipeId, localVersion, remoteVersion, resolution})` on `_editingModule`). Nothing calls the service's public `resolveConflict(local, remote)`. The 4 `resolveConflict (last-write-wins...)` tests (file lines 665-720) pin winner-selection on this dead public method — not WRONG (it's public API, `identical(winner, ...)` is a sound contract pin) but they create a FALSE impression that "conflict resolution is covered" while the actually-used `_conflictModule.resolveConflict` is exercised end-to-end only for the `remoteWon` branch (the single BUT-1265 test). localWon / tie-break-local / tie-break-remote on the EVENT-EMITTING module path have no end-to-end coverage. **Reusable lesson: when a service hand-copies a module's algorithm into a same-named public method, grep for callers before trusting same-named tests — winner-selection tests on the dead copy don't cover the live (event-emitting) copy, and the divergence (no `_emitConflict` on the service copy) is a latent production smell the tests can't surface.** Recommend deleting the dead duplicate (per the 2026-05-25 BUT-1072 "prefer a deletion-PR over a pinning-test" rule) and, if the localWon/tie branches matter to the banner, adding emit-path tests through `updateResource`. Info→Medium.
Did not modify the test file (review-only task). `flutter test` on this file was still in the Windows ~12-min compile floor at report time; the file is committed green in d4ee51b0f.

Reviewed a 4-test draft for long-press multi-select removal of group members (GroupMemberCard selection params + GroupMembersList stateful selection + inline "Ta bort markerade (N)" bar). The `_StubPermissionService extends Fake implements PermissionService` (overriding only `currentUserId`/`isGroupAdmin`/`canInviteToGroup`/`isOwner`) + register-in-GetIt + `production.ServiceLocator.initialize(DIContainer())` harness from `group_detail_report_tiles_test.dart` is the right seam — the card/list resolve `PermissionService` via `ServiceLocator.get` only, so NO Firebase Core mock/pigeon handlers needed. **Two meaningful gaps a happy-path-only draft missed (both cheap, pure widget-level, no `removeMultipleMembers`/UnifiedFriendsService stub):** (1) **the `selectable = canRemoveMember` guard** — in selection mode a non-removable member (owner/self) must keep its avatar and ignore taps; if `&& selectable` were dropped from `onTap`/`leading`, the owner could be toggled into the set and fed to bulk-remove, and an all-`'other-uid'` test stays green. Pin with the owner's OWN tile (`uid == currentUserId == ownerId`): `circle_outlined`/`check_circle` findsNothing + tap → toggle callback fires 0×. (2) **clear-on-cancel** — the "bar reveals with count" test stops short; tap `Icons.close` then re-enter selection and assert the count is back to **1, not 2** (proves `_selectedIds.clear()` ran, not just `_selectionMode=false`). Added both → file is 6 tests, all green, analyze clean. **Stopping before the final Remove CTA is an ACCEPTABLE boundary** — that path is `removeMultipleMembers` → confirm dialog → `UnifiedFriendsService`, and the LEAF (`removeFriendFromCategory`) is unit-tested in `friend_categories_operations_test.dart`. **Honest scope note: the `removeMultipleMembers` ORCHESTRATION itself (loop + partial-fail snackbar + `GroupEventBus.memberRemoved` + count return) has NO test — `GroupDetailActions` has zero test files in `test/`** — but that's a pre-existing gap predating this diff (the method shipped under BUT-997), not introduced by the UI change, so it's a backlog item, not a commit blocker.
