### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1080 notifications inbox bulk-dismiss [Pattern + Bug-class]
Reviewed the BUT-1080 boundary (long-press multi-select + bulk dismiss). Repo `deleteByIds(docIds,userId)` is well-covered (4 new cases mirroring `deleteAllByUser`: deletes-only-requested / refuses-cross-user / ignores-foreign-id / empty→0). **The repo-layer coverage was NOT sufficient on its own** — the VM `dismissSelected` carries an invariant the repo can't prove and a VM test was both cheap and warranted, so I ADDED `test/unit/viewmodels/notifications_viewmodel_test.dart` (6 tests, green). Why cheap: `NotificationsViewModel({NotificationService? notificationService})` is constructor-injectable, `MockNotificationService extends Mock` already exists in `production_mocks.dart` as a CLEAN Mock (no concrete `@override` bodies on the methods → `when()` works), the model has a trivial const ctor, and the VM resolves its dep ONLY via the ctor arg → **no DI bridge, no `TestServiceLocator`, no Firebase Core mock needed**. Just `MockNotificationService()` + `when(getNotificationHistory).thenAnswer` to seed `loadHistory()`, then drive `dismissSelected`.
**The load-bearing invariant the repo test can't reach: `dismissSelected` filters `_entries` on `e.id`, while the sibling `markAsOpened` one method up filters on `e.notificationId`.** A copy-paste regression to `e.notificationId` stays green at the repo layer and against any naive happy-path VM test that uses the same string for both. Pin it with entries whose `id` ('a') DIFFERS from `notificationId` ('notif-a'), then assert `dismissSelected({'notif-a'})` is a no-op (0 removed, no service call). Companion tests: selective-removal-leaves-others, forwards-exact-ids (`verify(captureAny).captured.single as List<String>` → `.toSet()`), notifies-listeners, empty-set no-op.
**Bug-class / mocktail trap for testing a fire-and-forget `.catchError` swallow on a `Future<int>` method:** the production code is `service.deleteHistoryNotifications(ids).catchError((e){log; return 0;})`. Modeling the failure with `when(...).thenThrow(...)` makes the mock throw SYNCHRONOUSLY at the call site → `.catchError` is never attached → throw escapes `unawaited()` and the test "fails" misleadingly (this distinguishes a sync-throw from the async rejection production actually faces — the method is `async` so real failures are rejected futures). Next attempt `thenAnswer((_) async => throw ...)` ALSO fails: mocktail erases the type arg to `Future<dynamic>`, tripping `Future.catchError`'s "handler must return the future's type" runtime check (the `return 0` int handler no longer matches dynamic). **Correct model: `thenAnswer((_) => Future<int>.error(Exception(...)))`** — a typed rejected future matching production's `Future<int>`, which `.catchError` swallows cleanly. Assert the optimistic removal STANDS after the failure (entries stay gone; the VM does NOT rollback by design — fire-and-forget, a refresh would resurrect). **General rule: to test a `.catchError`-swallow on a typed-future method, stub with `Future<T>.error(...)`, never `thenThrow` (sync) and never `async => throw` (type-erased).**
**View selection-mode in `notifications_view.dart` left untested — acceptable boundary** per the BUT-1038 pattern + ui-conventions.md (Stateful selection-mode UI is heavy Provider+VM scaffolding for low regression value; the logic worth pinning — selective removal, no-op guards, failure-survival — all lives in the now-tested VM). Service pass-through (`deleteHistoryNotifications` → `_historyRepository.deleteByIds(docIds, _userId)`) is a one-line forward with the security scoping in the repo (tested) — no own test earns its keep.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1194..1198 sprint audit (6 test/lib pairs) [Pattern — all adequate]
Audited a 6-pair sprint; verdict ADEQUATE, nothing blocking. The high-value reusable judgements:
- **`ConfidenceIndicator` (BUT-1196) — the icon-color assertion is correct precisely because production splits alpha.** Production sets `badgeBackgroundColor`/`badgeBorderColor` via `token.withValues(alpha: ...)` but `badgeIconColor = context.butleryColors.success` at FULL opacity. The test captures `tokens.success = context.butleryColors.success` and asserts `icon.color == tokens.success` — matches because it targets the un-alpha'd icon token, NOT the alpha'd background. **If a future review wants to also pin the background it must capture `token.withValues(alpha: AppDimensions.opacityVeryLight)`, not the bare token.** Threshold boundaries pinned at the exact `>=` edges (0.8 / 0.6 / 0.59) — that's the load-bearing part (a `>` vs `>=` regression flips a bucket). `floors rather than rounds (0.999→99%)` pins the `.toInt()` truncation contract a future `.round()` swap would break. Captures tokens via `Builder` — theme-safe. Adequate.
- **`getCommentLikers` (BUT-1194) — correctly on the FAKE lane, not the emulator lane, despite the repo file using `FieldValue.increment`/`serverTimestamp` elsewhere.** Decision rule confirmed: lane choice keys on what the METHOD UNDER TEST executes, not what sibling methods in the same file use. `getCommentLikers` is a plain `orderBy('likedAt', descending:true).limit().get()` — fake-runnable. The test SIDESTEPS the fake's serverTimestamp-ordering weakness by seeding explicit `Timestamp.fromDate(...)` values (documented in the header) — the right call, since `toggleCommentLike`'s production write stamps `serverTimestamp()` which a fake can't order across rapid writes. desc-order + limit-keeps-most-recent + default-100 + empty→[] is the full contract. Adequate; emulator lane would be over-engineering here.
- **`smart_import_view_smoke_test` (BUT-1197) — a smoke/mount test that EARNS its keep because the risk it guards is real-and-narrow: "does the 467-line `State.build` split still resolve its self-built `ChangeNotifierProvider(create: ServiceLocator.get<ImportManager>())`?"** The `find.byType(TextField), findsWidgets` assert is load-bearing, not topology-rot: it proves `build()` reached the real body (line 624 TextField) rather than an ErrorWidget — i.e. the DI graph resolved AND the post-frame clipboard-read initState didn't throw. This is the BUT-368-permitted exception: a mount guard for a freshly-split large view with a non-trivial DI `create:`, not a `findsOneWidget` on `ChangeNotifierProvider<VM>`. The clipboard stub returning `{'text':''}` is necessary (empty → no URL → no suggestion dialog → clean settle). Adequate.
- **`OcrErrorMessageBuilder` (BUT-1195) — the empty-list-vs-absent-key distinction is the real win.** The `recommendations: <String>[]` case (non-null but `.isNotEmpty==false`) is a branch the transitive VM-level `contains` suite never isolated; pairing `contains(ocrQualityTips)` WITH `isNot(contains(labelImprovementSuggestions))` pins mutual-exclusivity so a refactor collapsing the if/else can't pass. `_result` using the FULL ctor (not `.failure`, which hard-codes confidence 0.0) so the `confidence: 0.42` field assert isn't vacuous — documented and correct. Adequate.
- **`AllergenMismatch` (BUT-1198) — exemplary pure-detector coverage.** FREE-ignored / UNKNOWN-ignored / tracked-not-flagged / no-tagResult-empty are the four safety-relevant negative-space cases (a regression that flagged FREE or UNKNOWN would spam the banner; one that ignored an untracked CONTAINS would hide a real allergen). multi-allergen confirms set-builder accumulation. Nothing to add.
- **`persistence_service` scroll-offset (BUT-1028) — default-0.0 / round-trip / overwrite is the complete contract** for a get/set pair over SharedPreferences. (Sidebar: the file's `TestMockSharedPreferences` is unused dead scaffolding by these 3 tests — they use `SharedPreferences.setMockInitialValues` — but that's pre-existing file cruft, not introduced here, so not a finding.)
**Two NON-gaps confirmed acceptable as follow-ups, NOT blockers:** (1) `mina_recept_view.dart` ScrollController wiring (BUT-1028 view layer) — the persistable LOGIC (offset get/set/default) is unit-tested; the ScrollController→`setRecipeListScrollOffset(controller.offset)` debounce + `jumpTo(savedOffset)` restore is view-layer plumbing covered by journey/manual passes per ui-conventions.md, and a multi-scrollable view's controller wiring is exactly the fragile-finder territory the 2026-06-03 BUT-1180 entry warns against testing structurally. (2) `allergen_setup_banner.dart` MaterialBanner — `ScaffoldMessenger.showMaterialBanner` fire-and-forget is the same untestable-in-isolation shape as a SnackBar; the testable unit (the allergen-set computation) IS tested via `AllergenMismatch`. **General rule reaffirmed: when a feature splits into a pure detector + a ScaffoldMessenger/ScrollController presentation shell, test the detector exhaustively and let the shell ride the journey/manual lane — do NOT manufacture a structural widget test to chase the line.** No weak/proves-nothing tests found; no subject-mocked-away tests found; commit is clear from the testing-specialist's side.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1199 SwipeHintBanner generalization audit [Pattern — adequate, one strengthening]
A self-contained once-per-device hint widget was parameterized (`seenKey`/`icon`/`message`; static `seenKey`→`recipeSwipeSeenKey`, two new keys `cookingStepSeenKey`/`shoppingClaimSeenKey`). Verdict ADEQUATE, not blocking. Reusable judgements:
- **The dismiss-side isolation test is the load-bearing one and it's correct: `expect(prefs.getBool(shoppingClaimSeenKey), isTrue)` paired with `expect(prefs.getBool(recipeSwipeSeenKey), isNull)`.** `isNull` not `isFalse` is the right matcher — it proves the recipe key was NEVER WRITTEN, not merely "is false." This is the flip-detection that fails if `_dismiss` ever wrote the static key instead of `widget.seenKey`. Pattern: **for a parameterized-key persistence widget, the dismiss test must assert the OTHER keys are `isNull` (untouched), not just that the own key is `isTrue`.**
- **WEAKNESS caught (read-side isolation, test c "honours its own seenKey"): asserting only `findsOneWidget` on the cooking icon when the recipe key is flagged is the POSITIVE half only.** It catches the primary regression (recipe key bleeding into the cooking banner → cooking goes `findsNothing`) but NOT "ignores the flag entirely / always shows." **Strengthening rule: an isolation test needs BOTH halves in the SAME prefs state — the default banner under the recipe key must go `findsNothing` AND the parameterized banner must still `findsOneWidget`.** Recommended as polish, not a gate, because the worst regression is still caught by the positive half.
- **Test-message literal `'Long-press a step'` (not a real l10n key) in the unit test is CORRECT** — decouples the widget-unit test from ARB copy; the real `context.l10n.cookingStepHintText`/`shoppingClaimHintText` wiring rides the call sites. Verified both view insertions (`cooking_mode_view.dart`, `collaborative_shopping_items.dart`) pass distinct correct `seenKey`/icon/`context.l10n.*`, and the recipe default site stayed `const SwipeHintBanner()` so the backward-compat default path the original 3 tests cover is real.
- **View-placement of an already-tested widget is manual/journey territory (ui-conventions), not a widget-unit gap** — same ruling as the 2026-06-04 BUT-1194 sprint. Confirmed l10n keys resolve (no missing-key runtime risk). The icon+message render test (a) asserting BOTH `find.byIcon` AND `find.text` (not just that it builds) is the right depth — proves the params reach the rendered Row.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1037 recipe-likeness gate audit [Pattern — adequate, no blocker]
A pure `RecipeTextHeuristic.looksLikeRecipe(String)` LLM-cost gate (`measures>=1 && verbs>=1` OR `measures+verbs>=3`, with `\b`-anchored unit regex + word-prefix verb regex) wired into a view dialog + a thin analytics method. 9-case test. Verdict ADEQUATE, not blocking. Reusable judgements:
- **The load-bearing false-positive guard is "does not match units inside other words."** Verified by tracing the regex: `\b(...g...|...l...)\b` does NOT fire on the `g` in "good"/"great" or `l` in "loaf" (no word boundary inside the word), so measures=0, only the `bake` verb hits → false. **This assertion genuinely fails if someone drops the `\b` anchors** — it's the real guard, not topology rot. For any heuristic with single-letter/short tokens (`g`/`l`/`dl`/`st`), the "substring-inside-word must NOT count" negative case is the highest-value test and must be present.
- **The verb-less count-branch boundary case is the other must-have.** `'200 g\n3 dl\n... 5 dl'` → measures=3, verbs=0, hits `measures+verbs>=3` at the exact `>=` edge. It's the ONLY branch that admits an ingredient-only paste; without it that path is untested. Pin it at exactly 3 hits (the boundary), as the test does.
- **Don't flag a passing-via-the-other-branch test as vacuous if its INTENT still flips on a real regression.** The "EN metric (g not cups)" case passes via the AND-branch (`g`+`ml`+`bake`); `g` isn't strictly load-bearing (drop it, `ml`+`bake` still passes), but the test still flips if the EN verb list or `ml` unit is removed — intent (EN recipes survive) is real. Keep.
- **A thin analytics passthrough (`logWarnDialogCancelled` → `logEvent(name, {'source': source})`, no branching) and a `showDialog`-driven view gate (`_parseAndNavigate`/`_confirmNonRecipeImport`) are NOT unit gaps** — journey/manual territory per BUT-387 Phase 6 + ui-conventions. The pure function is the entire testable surface and it's covered. Confirmed `looksLikeRecipe` is the only seam with branching/regression risk; the dialog + emitter ride the journey lane.
- **Optional strengthening (P3, not a gate): a CHARACTERIZATION test pinning the gate's intended LENIENCY** — non-recipe prose with one standalone unit + one verb-prefix word (e.g. `'I weigh 80 kg and I cook dinner most evenings.'`) returns `true`. This is inherent to a cheap heuristic (user can back out of the warn dialog), so pin it as a documented-limitation so a future "tighten the gate" change has to consciously update it rather than silently breaking the override-UX assumption. Same `CHARACTERIZATION:`-prefix pattern as the 2026-05-25 YouTubeTranscriptService entry.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-905 SemanticsService.announce — conscious skip [Pattern — no test]
Two `SemanticsService.announce(...)` calls added inline: favorite-toggle in `recipe_detail_view.dart` (`isFavorite ? a11yRecipeFavorited : a11yRecipeUnfavorited`) and comment-post in `recipe_detail/handlers/recipe_social_handler.dart` (`a11yCommentPosted`). **Verdict: conscious skip, no widget test.** Reusable ruling for `SemanticsService.announce` side effects:
- **`announce` is fire-and-forget with NO observable widget-tree effect** — the only way to assert it fired is intercepting `SystemChannels.accessibility`, which is brittle + high-harness-cost (needs mocked self-loading VM + recipe + l10n scaffold + finding an `IconButton` in a `SliverAppBar`). This is exactly the "skip tests needing heavy ViewModel scaffolding — covered by audit script + manual screen-reader passes" carve-out in `ui-conventions.md`.
- **The announce lives inside an inline `onPressed`/handler closure** → a channel-interception test pins call-site topology, not contract. The *correct* refactor (extracting the favorite handler the way the comment announce already lives in a handler) would break the test with zero behaviour change = BUT-368 topology-rot anti-pattern.
- **The `favorited/unfavorited` ternary is not a domain branch worth pinning** — it reads VM state the toggle already mutated; the sole failure mode is "two l10n keys swapped," which a screen-reader pass catches instantly. No aggregation/chunking/permission/locale invariant in play.
- **Real (free) regression guard = the l10n keys.** `a11yRecipeFavorited` / `a11yRecipeUnfavorited` / `a11yCommentPosted` must exist in BOTH `app_sv.arb` + `app_en.arb` with `flutter gen-l10n` run; a missing key is a compile error `flutter analyze` already gates. The existing per-chunk `find.bySemanticsLabel` tests cover *labels*; announcement interception is a deliberate, documented convention gap, not an oversight.
- **General rule going forward: a `SemanticsService.announce(...)` added to a view/handler is manual/a11y-pass territory, not a widget-unit gap** — same family as the BUT-1194/BUT-1037 view-placement rulings above.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-1245 pure-threshold extraction pattern [Pattern discovered]
**When a static method mixes pure threshold decisions with DI/dialog calls, extract the decisions as `@visibleForTesting` statics rather than building a DI bridge or widget pump.** `ImportResultHandler.checkForDuplicates` owned two boundaries — `score >= 0.6` (fingerprint match gate) and `score < 0.8 ? 0.8 : score` (display-score floor) — that needed zero services, no `BuildContext`, no dialog. Extracting them as `meetsContentDuplicateThreshold(double)` and `clampToExactMatchFloor(double)` and having the parent call them is a pure refactor (the inlined expressions are word-for-word identical), but it opens a one-import test file with 9 tests and no DI scaffolding at all. **When to use this pattern**: the logic is a predicate or transformation over primitives (doubles, ints, strings), the threshold constants are `static const`, and the decision step is surrounded by side-effect code that makes the method untestable whole. **Test file pattern**: two `group()` blocks — one per helper — each with exact-boundary, just-below-boundary, well-above, and zero/sentinel cases. Each test has a comment-sentence explaining which regression it guards against (`>= vs >`, floor removal, over-aggressive clamping). No imports beyond `flutter_test` and the production file. File lives at `test/unit/views/<subdirectory>/<helper>_test.dart` (not `test/views/`). **Gotcha: `@visibleForTesting` on an `abstract final class` static is available from `package:flutter/material.dart`** (re-exports `foundation.dart`); no extra import needed as long as the production file already imports Material. Analyze confirmed clean, arch test confirmed unaffected, 9/9 new + 73 existing tests green.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1079 pt1 read-only SourceArtefact sheet — CONSCIOUS SKIP [Pattern — no test]
`recipe_detail_view.dart` adds an overflow item → `showModalBottomSheet` displaying a persisted `SourceArtefact`: a type label via a private exhaustive `_sourceTypeLabel` `switch` over 6 `SourceArtefactType` values, a captured-at via `ContextualTimeFormatter.standard`, and a scrollable `SelectableText` of the raw payload. No mutation, no service call, no branch beyond the exhaustive switch. **Verdict: conscious skip — same family as BUT-918 / BUT-946 static-disclosure rulings.** Reusable judgements:
- **An exhaustive `switch` over an enum is its OWN completeness guarantee** — a missing arm is a `flutter analyze` error (the test never runs). A widget test pinning "`url` → the SV URL string" only hardcodes the l10n VALUE, duplicating `gen-l10n` with a brittle copy that breaks on a harmless wording tweak = the BUT-918 "literal string renders" anti-pattern. A *wrong* mapping is a copy defect for a screen-reader/manual pass, not a behavioural regression.
- **The "read-only sheet does no mutation/re-extract" safety test is a BUT-946 tautology** — the re-extract path DOESN'T EXIST YET (prod comment: "Re-extract … is a follow-up"). Bypass/no-mutation tests are only worth writing once the guarded path exists; it'll arrive with its own intent + tests.
- **Both null-guards are already correct (item gated on `sourceArtefact != null` AND handler re-checks)** — a code-review observation, not a test. No null-deref surface.
- **Harness cost fails the second half of the decision rule**: `RecipeDetailView` self-loads via VM through the production-ServiceLocator bridge; driving the sheet needs mocked VM + bridge + `createLocalizedTestApp` + a seeded recipe with a non-null artefact + pumping past the self-load gate + opening the overflow `PopupMenu` (which overflows its Row on the 800px default surface → needs the scoped `FlutterError.onError` 'A RenderFlex overflowed' ignore from the BUT-1180 files). High cost, zero coupling to any of it.
- **If a recipe-detail JOURNEY test ever lands in `test/views/`, the sheet is one beat there** (open overflow → View captured source → type+timestamp+payload visible), alongside the future re-extract flow. Not a standalone mechanical widget test now.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-904 AutoSaveManager<T> primitive extraction — ADEQUATE [Pattern — no blocker]
Shared draft-persistence primitive (`lib/services/persistence/auto_save_manager.dart`) extracted from 5 per-surface `_load/_save/_clearDraft` try/catch triads; 3 surfaces migrated (URL import, text import, comment composer). Deliberately NOT a BaseService/DI singleton — widget/VM-owned, single storage key, disposed with owner. 13-test unit suite + the EXISTING comment widget test passing UNMODIFIED. Verdict ADEQUATE. Reusable judgements:
- **For a `<T>` over `SharedPreferences`, the `@visibleForTesting Future<SharedPreferences> Function()? prefsProvider` ctor seam is the right best-effort-error harness** — pass `() => Future.error(StateError(...))` to prove `load` returns null / `save`/`clear`/`flush` `completes` without propagating. This is exactly the contract the extraction EXISTS to centralize (each removed copy had its own swallow-to-`AppLogger.warning`); without an injected-throw test the "best-effort" promise is unproven. Pin it.
- **The load-bearing domain invariant is "encode-to-empty / encode-null ⇒ REMOVE the key, never write \"\"."** All 3 surfaces rely on it (delete-everything-in-the-field must drop the draft, not persist an empty string that re-seeds a blank draft on next mount). Two tests cover it (empty-string-encodes-empty, encoder-returns-null) — both genuinely flip if `_write` drops the `encoded.isEmpty` branch. Highest-value tests in the suite; an LLM-draft "save persists the value" alone would miss this.
- **An EXISTING widget test passing UNMODIFIED across a primitive swap IS the migration's behaviour-preservation gate** — `comment_form_widget_test.dart` drives the real `comment_draft_v1_<recipeId>` keys (load-on-mount, save-on-keystroke, clear-on-post, per-recipe isolation) through the new manager. No new widget test needed for the comment surface; the green unmodified test proves the refactor is contract-preserving. Don't add a duplicate manager-level test for what the widget test already exercises end-to-end.
- **debounce/flush are tested but NOT exercised by any current surface** (all 3 migrate with `debounce: Duration.zero` eager). Acceptable forward-looking coverage, not impl-coupling: they're published contract of the primitive and BUT-910 (photo import) is the named intended adopter. The generic `<T>` JSON-map round-trip likewise justifies the abstraction existing over a String-only helper — keep it.
- **FOLLOW-UP GAP (non-blocking, file as ticket): the two import views have NO draft-persistence widget test.** Grepped `test/` for `url_import_draft|text_import_draft|ImportViaUrl|FranSocialaMedier` → zero hits. The comment surface got its end-to-end gate (BUT-1058); the URL + text import surfaces rely on the manager unit tests + manual verification only. A `tester.enterText → prefs key written` + `seed prefs → field restored on mount` widget pair per view would mirror the comment gate. Not a commit blocker (primitive is unit-proven, migration is mechanical, no behaviour change in the diff), but a real coverage gap for those two surfaces' load/save/clear wiring.

### 2026-06-04 — BUT-1208 franSocialaMedier allergen-banner wiring — NO TEST OWED, journey gap folds [Pattern — no blocker]
3rd-and-final import surface wired to `AllergenSetupBanner` (after `smart_import_view` and the BUT-1200 photo path). Staged diff = ONLY `lib/views/fran_sociala_medier_view.dart` (+ todo.md); `allergen_mismatch.dart` is NOT in the diff → no new pure function. The wired predicate `AllergenMismatch.unconfiguredContainedAllergens(parsed, prefs).isNotEmpty` already has **6 unit tests** (`test/unit/services/tagging/allergen_mismatch_test.dart` lines 35-82) and is now called from 2 prod sites. Verdict: NO new unit test warranted, journey gap does NOT block. Reusable judgements:
- **The "Nth import surface adopts an already-built banner at an already-proven predicate" is the canonical mechanical-wiring NO-TEST case.** Confirm by `git diff --staged --name-only`: if the pure-logic file (here `allergen_mismatch.dart`) is absent from the diff, there is by definition no new test surface — the body is `read-state (null-guard) → call-already-tested-method → .isNotEmpty → fire-already-built-widget`. Unlike BUT-1200 (which DID add `anyUnconfigured` and correctly got 4 tests), this diff adds zero pure logic. Don't manufacture a smoke test.
- **When the SAME banner/predicate is now wired at 3+ surfaces, fold the journey coverage into ONE parameterized journey ticket, don't file per-surface.** Here: smart-import + photo (BUT-1209) + franSocialaMedier all answer "import surface with a CONTAINS-unconfigured-allergen recipe → banner shows." Note franSocialaMedier as an additional surface on the existing photo-path journey ticket (BUT-1209); a new ticket would fragment one parameterized journey test across three IDs. (Sibling missing-journey families already tracked: URL/text import draft on BUT-1204.)
- **Only thing to eyeball (code-review, not a test): `ServiceLocator.get<UserService>().allergenPreferences` null/throw at the nav point** — identical to how `smart_import_view` already does it, so it's a shipped exercised path, not a risk this diff introduces. No test owed for parity with an already-live call.

### 2026-06-05 — BUT-914 comment-visibility audience review [Pattern — no blocker]
New pure `commentVisibilityAudience(recipe, currentUserId)` in `lib/views/recipe_detail/comment_visibility.dart` (owner + collaborative members − self; empty for non-collaborative) + 4 unit tests. Pure-helper-only + untested `_buildVisibilityLine` UI glue is the CORRECT codec-gate call (BUT-1203/1207 family). Reusable judgements:
- **When a pure helper claims to "mirror" a server-side resolver, diff the resolver and pin the SHARED fallback branch, not just the happy path.** The helper's `socialData?.ownerId` empty → `recipe.createdBy.orEmpty()` fallback (lines 18-20) mirrors `firebase_recipe_ownership_resolver._resolveOwnerId`'s `return recipe.createdBy` (lines 80-88) — and was the ONE privacy-relevant uncovered case: a regression dropping the `createdBy` fallback silently under-states the audience (owner vanishes from the "visible to" label) with zero failing tests. The 4 shipped tests all run through `RecipeFactory.buildCollaborative` which ALWAYS sets `socialData.ownerId='test_user'` → the null-socialData-on-collaborative branch is never reached. Recommended (cheap): `RecipeFactory.build(type: RecipeType.collaborative, createdBy: 'legacy_owner')` (no socialData) → audience `['legacy_owner']` viewed as outsider, `[]` viewed as the owner. owner-as-current-user is ALREADY covered (the "owner not in own audience" test IS that case — don't double-file it).
- **The truncation "+N" arithmetic is the non-brittle UI gap worth a follow-up ticket.** `_buildVisibilityLine` computes `overflow = audienceIds.length - shown.length` where `shown` derives from the RESOLVED-names subset, not the full audience → when names don't all resolve, `+N` counts unresolvable members (possible "+N people you can't name" leak; intended-or-bug is a product call). Fix shape: extract `(shown, "+N")` into a pure helper beside `commentVisibilityAudience` and unit-test the count against resolved-vs-audience. This is pure-extractable and survives theme/UI refactor; the hide-when-empty / hide-when-unresolved early-returns fold into the same ticket. Do NOT drive `_buildVisibilityLine` end-to-end (needs VM+DI+localized-app for cosmetic output = the BUT-387-Phase-6 brittle lane).
- **Set-union `{owner, ...members}..remove(self)` is equivalent to the resolver's `recipeOwnerId + sharedIds.where(uid != owner)`** — both dedup the owner-also-a-member case; the helper's dedup test correctly pins it. Empty-id filtering (`members.where(isNotEmpty)` + `if (ownerId.isNotEmpty)`) is defence-in-depth, one assert if you touch the file, not its own ticket.

### 2026-06-08 — BUT-1211 audience name-resolution extracted (closes the 2026-06-05 follow-up) [Pattern — extraction + tests]
BUT-1211 adds `_resolveCommentAudience` (returns `({List<String> resolved, int total})`) shared by `_buildVisibilityLine` + a new `_showAudienceDialog` (full audience: all resolved names + a trailing unresolved-count line, count-only body when zero names resolve) on `recipe_detail_comments.dart`. This is the EXACT "+N arithmetic / name-resolution" gap the 2026-06-05 entry flagged as the worth-a-follow-up pure-extractable case. Verdict + what I did:
- **The new logic was trapped behind two private `State` methods** needing the full `RecipeDetailComments` widget pumped with a live `SocialRecipeViewModel` Provider + a ServiceLocator-registered `UnifiedFriendsService` + the VM's post-frame `initialize()`/`refreshComments()` Firebase stack. Driving that end-to-end for cosmetic output = the BUT-387-Phase-6 brittle lane + ui-conventions.md:84 heavy-VM exemption. **Don't pump it.**
- **But "exempt from a widget test" ≠ "no coverage owed" when there's genuinely-NEW untested logic.** The name-merge (friends-list names + owner-name `putIfAbsent` fallback, `audienceIds.where(containsKey).map(...)`) and the dialog's `unresolved = total - resolved.length` are NOT covered by the existing `commentVisibilityAudience` (pure id-set) or `formatCommentAudience` (pure formatter) tests — both predate the name lookup. Min-viable seam (cf. 2026-05-02 BUT-573/434): **extract the merge into a pure `resolveCommentAudienceNames(audienceIds, friendNames, {ownerId, ownerName})` in the sibling `comment_visibility.dart`** (the module that already owns the clean unit-test lane). The State method becomes thin wiring: gather `friendNames` from `ServiceLocator.get<UnifiedFriendsService>().friendsList` inside the try/catch, then call the pure helper. The untestable-by-design part (ServiceLocator + post-frame) stays trivial; the privacy logic gets VM-free coverage.
- **6 new tests** (`test/unit/views/recipe_detail/comment_visibility_test.dart`, now 17/17 green) pin the privacy contract, NOT topology: `total` always = `audienceIds.length` not the resolved subset (unresolved counted, never dropped); **owner name resolves via the fallback when the friends map is empty** (the try/catch friends-unavailable path — a collaborator must still identify the owner); blank owner name does NOT register; **friends map wins over owner fallback for the same uid** (`putIfAbsent` = fallback-not-override, guards stale denormalized names); order preserved; and the dialog's `unresolved` arithmetic pinned ON the record (`total - resolved.length == 3`) so a resolved/total desync fails.
- **Testability verdict on the lib change: sound, and improved by the extraction.** As originally written it duplicated the `audienceIds.isEmpty` early-return and inlined the merge in the widget (untestable). The extraction removes the duplication (early-return now lives once, in the pure helper) and is behaviour-identical (`flutter analyze` clean on both files). Reusable rule: **a private `State` helper returning a Dart record of pure-computable data is a near-zero-cost extraction candidate — move it to the sibling pure module, leave the impure gathering (DI/ServiceLocator/post-frame) in the widget, and the record's fields become directly assertable without any Provider/Firebase scaffolding.** Did NOT commit (assessment task).

### 2026-06-08 — BUT-944 AdaptiveIcons semantic concept aliases (icon codemod) [Pattern — codemod test-impact + convention pinning]
A pure icon-constant codemod swapped 9 call sites' raw `Icons.favorite|star|bookmark` (+ `_border`/`_outline`) for new `AdaptiveIcons.{favouriteFilled,favouriteOutline,primaryFilled,primaryOutline,savedTemplate,savedTemplateOutline}` getters, each a thin alias of the existing `_isIOS ? Cupertino… : Material…` shape getter. Verdict + reusable rules:
- **Test-impact of an `AdaptiveIcons.*`-aliasing codemod is structurally ZERO even when `find.byIcon(Icons.favorite)` exists at a migrated site.** Because the getters resolve to the SAME Material glyph on the test platform (`_isIOS => !kIsWeb && defaultTargetPlatform == iOS`, false in the default harness), a `find.byIcon(Icons.favorite)` finder still matches the migrated `Icon(AdaptiveIcons.favouriteFilled)`. The user's grep flagged hits only in unrelated fixture tests; I found one they missed at a MIGRATED site (`test/widget/recipe/recipe_card_test.dart` lines 129/232/233/293/294) — but all five are `findsNothing` (favorite button absent in those render modes), so doubly safe. **Rule: for an adaptive-getter swap, a `byIcon` hit at the migrated widget is NOT automatically breakage — check (a) is the assertion `findsNothing`, and (b) does the new getter resolve to the same glyph on the non-iOS test platform. If both, no edit owed.** (Contrast the BUT-755..758 color-token entry, where `cs.X` on a bare MaterialApp DID change the value — icon getters have no such theme dependency.)
- **The codemod's own contract = a convention getter unit test, added where the platform-flip helpers already live.** `test/widget/common/icons/adaptive_icon_test.dart` already had an `AdaptiveIcons static helpers` group using `onPlatformSync(TargetPlatform.android|iOS, () => expect(getter, glyph))` (sets/resets `debugDefaultTargetPlatformOverride` in try/finally — the canonical platform-flip pattern for these). Appended a `semantic concept aliases (BUT-944)` group: (1) Material-side concept→glyph map, (2) iOS-side `_fill` variants (CAUTION: bookmark's iOS glyph is `CupertinoIcons.bookmark_fill`, NOT `bookmark` — verify each against source, the outline getters use the non-fill variant), (3) **alias ≡ underlying shape getter** equivalence (proves glyph-preserving, the whole point of the codemod), (4) **distinctness** — `{favouriteFilled, primaryFilled, savedTemplate}` has length 3, which is the test that actually fails if someone re-points `primaryFilled => bookmark`. This pins concept→glyph so the convention can't silently drift, and survives any refactor that keeps the mapping. 22/22 green.
- **Runner note (recurs every `flutter test` here):** the native-PATH `cmd.bat` MUST include `C:\Windows\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0` on PATH — flutter's wrapper shells out to PowerShell and dies with "PowerShell executable not found" on the bare `System32;flutter\bin;Git\cmd` PATH. Also: pipe flutter's stdout to a dedicated `> .out.txt 2>&1` file inside the bat and tail THAT — the agent harness buffers a background bat's combined stream until the wrapper fully exits (saw a run sit at 0 bytes for the whole ~12-min compile floor), whereas flutter writes the redirected file incrementally. Did NOT commit (assessment task).
- **`/c/tools/flutter/bin/flutter test <path>` via the Bash tool ran fine this session WITHOUT the cmd.bat workaround** (git-PATH error did not trip) — try the direct invocation first, fall back to the native-PATH bat only if it errors on git/PowerShell.

### 2026-06-08 — BUT-923 Settings-hub food-tile subtitle + relabel [Pattern — label/subtitle change, test added]
A label relabel (`allergenSettingsTitle` "Allergeninställningar" → "Allergener & kostpreferenser") + a new optional `String? subtitle` on the PRIVATE `_SettingsTile` (rendered via `ListTile subtitle:`), wired only onto the food/allergen entry with new key `allergenSettingsHubSubtitle`. Verdict: no existing test broke, and a cheap full-hub widget test WAS warranted. Reusable judgements:
- **Pre-clearing a string relabel = grep `test/` for the OLD value in BOTH locales AND the l10n KEY name AND the private widget name.** Here: `Allergen Settings` / `Allergeninställningar` / `allergenSettingsTitle` / `_SettingsTile` → zero hits asserting the old copy. The only hub-adjacent test (`language_tile_test.dart`) renders `LanguageTile` in isolation and never touches the food tile, so the relabel can't break it. No settings smoke/journey test pins the old string. **Confirmed-safe relabel.**
- **A subtitle on a PRIVATE sub-tile is only reachable by pumping the WHOLE view — but that's affordable here and worth it.** Unlike the `LanguageTile` (deliberately made public to avoid the hub's dep graph), `_SettingsTile` forces a full `SettingsHubView` pump. Cost is modest: register a `Mock extends ReportService` (no existing mock — its 3 ctor deps are concrete, so mock the class not the interface) stubbing `watchIsAdmin() → Stream.value(false)` (admin tile collapses, no Firestore), PLUS register `LocaleProvider()` (the embedded `LanguageTile.initState` resolves it from DI — forgetting it throws `GetIt … LocaleProvider is not registered` and cascades 12 exceptions through the ListView). `SharedPreferences.setMockInitialValues({})` for the LocaleProvider. Pass `wrapInScaffold: false` to `createLocalizedTestApp` (the view supplies its own Scaffold/AppBar — nested Scaffold otherwise).
- **3 tests pin the user-visible contract, not topology:** (1) food tile shows relabelled title + subtitle, and `find.ancestor(of: title, matching: ListTile)` then `find.descendant` proves the subtitle is on the SAME tile (not floating); (2) the null-branch — a subtitle-LESS tile (`personalTagsViewTitle`) has `ListTile.subtitle == null` (guards a refactor that drops the `subtitle != null` guard or duplicates the title); (3) **the domain invariant the autopilot draft missed — English locale renders the English relabel + English subtitle**, proving both ARB tables were updated in lockstep (the BUT-748-family cross-table risk: a key added to one locale but not the other). Test (3) initially false-failed finding 0 English text, but that was a CASCADE from the missing-LocaleProvider crash — once DI was wired, `MaterialApp(locale: Locale('en'))` resolved correctly. `test/widget/views/settings/settings_hub_food_tile_test.dart`, 3/3 green, analyze clean. Did NOT commit (assessment task).

### 2026-06-08 — BUT-906 activity-broadcast opt-out (privacy `bool` enforcement at a service emit gate) [Pattern + reusable harness]
A new `shareActivityToFeed` privacy `bool` (default true) on `UserProfile`, enforced by one line in `ActivityFeedService.emitEvent`: `if (userService.currentUserProfile?.shareActivityToFeed == false) return;`. Added 5 service tests + 6 model tests, both files green (5/5 and the model file 58/58), analyze clean. Mutation-verified. Reusable rulings:
- **A service that injects ONE dep via ctor but resolves the rest (`PermissionService`, `UserService`) via `ServiceLocator.get` INSIDE the method is fully unit-testable via the production-locator bridge** — no need to declare it "untestable." Recipe: `await BaseUnitTest.setupUnit(); await TestServiceLocator.initialize(); prod.ServiceLocator.initialize(DIContainer());` then `TestServiceLocator.registerMock<PermissionService>(fakePerm)` + `registerMock<user_svc.UserService>(mockUser)` (import `user_service.dart as user_svc` — the interface type is what `ServiceLocator.get<UserService>()` looks up). Inject the ctor dep (here the repo) as a recording **Fake**. tearDown: `BaseUnitTest.resetMocks()` + `await TestServiceLocator.reset()`.
- **`MockUserService` (production_mocks:1628) is `extends Mock` and does NOT override `currentUserProfile`** → stub it with `when(() => mock.currentUserProfile).thenReturn(profile)`. (Its `setUserState`/`currentUser` getter is a SEPARATE test-only accessor, NOT the `currentUserProfile` the service reads — don't confuse them.) `FakePermissionService` (`extends Fake`) has `setPermissionState(currentUserId: ...)` for the auth gate; no `when()` (it's a Fake).
- **The `== false` (not `!= true` / not `== true`-then-negate) guard is the load-bearing detail and the test trio MUST cover all three truth-states of a NULLABLE bool gate:** opted-out (`false`) → suppressed; opted-in (`true`) → emitted; **null profile → STILL emitted** (default-broadcast preserved for pre-field / unloaded accounts). The null case is the one an autopilot draft drops, and it's the actual privacy-vs-data-loss tradeoff: `== false` correctly treats null as "broadcast," whereas a naive `!profile.shareActivityToFeed` would NPE or mute every legacy account. Pin it explicitly with a `reason:`.
- **Mutation-check a privacy gate before claiming done:** `sed 's/== false) return;/== true) return;/'` flipped the guard → exactly the opted-out (leak) + opted-in (over-suppress) tests went red (`+3 -2`), the null/auth/throws tests stayed green. That `-2` is the proof the two privacy assertions are load-bearing, not topology. Restore from a `/tmp` backup + `git diff --stat` to confirm byte-identical.
- **Model side: mirror the sibling privacy `bool`'s test group (here BUT-912 `showOnlineStatus`) but add the Firestore lane the sibling omitted.** `UserService` persists through `toFirestore`/`fromMap` (NOT just `toJson`/`fromJson`), so the round-trip test must cover BOTH paths, and the backward-compat "absent key → true" must be asserted on BOTH `fromJson` AND `fromMap` (two decode paths, two `SerializationUtils.safeBool(..., defaultValue: true)` call sites — a regression dropping the default in one but not the other is the BUT-748-family cross-path risk). Did NOT commit (assessment task).

### 2026-06-09 — BUT-910 photo-import draft persistence review [Pattern — draft-persistence test architecture + the a11y-fake-VM seam]
Reviewed `test/unit/viewmodels/photo_import/photo_import_draft_test.dart` (9 tests). Verdict ACCEPTABLE. Reusable rulings:
- **Three-layer draft-persistence test shape is the template for future AutoSaveManager adopters:** (1) codec contract incl. the nothing-worth-keeping rule (`encode → null` removes the key) and `decode` of a blob missing the load-bearing field → null; (2) byte store as real-FS round-trip via a `debug*DirOverride` seam (`getTemporaryDirectory` needs a platform channel) incl. gone-file → null; (3) VM round-trip with the SUBJECT real (real `ImportManager.withStrategies([TextImportStrategy()])` so restore's re-parse is genuine; `SharedPreferences.setMockInitialValues({})` per-test doubles as draft-key isolation). Corrupt-blob resilience belongs to `auto_save_manager_test.dart` ("returns null (no throw) when decode fails") — do NOT re-test it per adopter.
- **The headline test must be `persist → dispose() → restore into a FRESH VM instance`** — that's the user-visible feature (draft survives nav-away which disposes the VM). A restore on the same instance would mock away the very death the feature rescues from. Pair with degraded-mode (delete the staged file out-of-band → restore returns true, text-only) and explicit-clear discard (assert BOTH SharedPreferences key gone AND staged file gone; `pumpEventQueue()` after the `unawaited` discard).
- **"Persist fires after OCR success" wiring is untestable-by-design** (`OCRExtractionService.instance` singleton, no seam — same family as 2026-05-02 BUT-573/434) and is one `unawaited(persistPhotoDraft(...))` line = fan-out-listener-shaped (BUT-754). Testing `persistPhotoDraft` directly is the right call; don't manufacture an OCR-pipeline test for it.
- **ui-conventions.md:84 "skip heavy-VM widget tests" does NOT apply once an a11y/fake-VM seam already exists.** `photo_import_announce_test.dart` already has `_FakePhotoImportViewModel extends ChangeNotifier implements PhotoImportViewModel` resolved via ServiceLocator — extending it with a non-null `loadPersistedDraft()` + recorded `restoreDraftCalled`/`discardCalled` flags makes the restore-dialog choice wiring (~accept → restoreDraft; decline → discardPersistedDraft so it never re-prompts; skip-when-`hasImage||hasOcrResult`) a cheap widget test, not a heavy one. The decline-discards path is the strongest candidate: a regression re-prompts the user forever. Filed as follow-up, not a blocker.

### 2026-06-09 — BUT-1213 favourite-heart colour token, theme-resolved pattern [Pattern]
A one-token change (`cs.error` → `cs.primary` on the active favourite heart in `RecipeCard._buildFavoriteButton`) is above the testing threshold when the token encodes a documented domain invariant (green = personal favourite, red = social like — BUT-1213 comment added in production code). The regression risk is real: a future merge could flip the constant back silently and none of the 77 existing tests would catch it.

**Pattern for asserting a theme-resolved colour on a specific Icon:**
```dart
late ColorScheme cs;
await tester.pumpWidget(
  createLocalizedTestApp(
    child: Builder(builder: (context) {
      cs = Theme.of(context).colorScheme;
      return Scaffold(body: MyWidget(...));
    }),
  ),
);
final icon = tester.widget<Icon>(
  find.byWidgetPredicate((w) => w is Icon && w.icon == AdaptiveIcons.favouriteFilled),
);
expect(icon.color, cs.primary);
expect(icon.color, isNot(cs.error));
```
The `Builder` captures the live `ColorScheme` from `AppTheme.lightTheme` (installed by `createLocalizedTestApp`), so the assertion is theme-resolved and survives any future token-value change. The `isNot(cs.error)` assertion is load-bearing: it would catch exactly the class of regression this change fixed.

**`RecipeFactory.build(...)` doesn't expose `isFavorite`** — use `.copyWith(isFavorite: true)` on the result. `isFavorite` defaults to `false` in `RecipeCore`, so the inactive-heart test needs no copyWith.

Added 2 tests under group `'Favourite heart colour (BUT-1213)'` in `test/widget/recipe/recipe_card_test.dart`. Import `adaptive_icon.dart` to reference `AdaptiveIcons.favouriteFilled` / `AdaptiveIcons.favouriteOutline`. `dart analyze` clean.

### 2026-06-09 — BUT-1212 announce sites 2-of-3 closed; full-shell announce pump UNMASKED a ProviderNotFoundException [Bug found + Pattern — supersedes parts of 2026-06-04 BUT-905 + 2026-06-08 BUT-1210 deferrals]
BUT-1212 covered the collaborative-shopping bought/unbought announce (`test/views/social/collaborative_shopping_view_test.dart`, full-shell group) and the comment-posted announce (`test/widget/a11y/comment_posted_announce_test.dart`, host-widget over the REAL static `RecipeSocialHandler.postComment`). Verdict ACCEPTABLE. Updates to prior rulings:
- **PRODUCTION BUG UNMASKED (the philosophy proven again — add to the bugs-caught list):** `_CollaborativeShoppingViewState._toggleItem`/`_addItem` did `context.read<CollaborativeShoppingViewModel>()` on the **State's own context, which sits ABOVE the `ChangeNotifierProvider` its `build()` creates** → `ProviderNotFoundException` on every real add/toggle in production. Invisible to the old sub-widget-only harness (it injected a VM and bypassed the view's handlers); the first full-shell tap surfaced it instantly. Fix: pass the VM from the Consumer scope into the handler (`onToggleItem: (id) => _toggleItem(id, viewModel)`). **Rule: a State whose `build()` creates its own Provider can NEVER `context.read` that type from its own context — and only a full-shell gesture test exercises that wiring. When a view self-provides, at least one test must tap THROUGH the shell, not drive the sub-widget.**
- **The BUT-1210 deferral routes were wrong about the needed seam:** no VM-injection ctor was required. `ChangeNotifierProvider(create: () => VM(shoppingService: ServiceLocator.get()))` is already seam-ed via the prod↔test locator bridge — register `MockUnifiedShoppingService`, the REAL VM resolves it. For a static handler (ServiceLocator + Provider deps), a ~25-line host widget (`ChangeNotifierProvider<VM>.value` + `Builder` + button invoking the handler) runs it end to end. Neither is the "brittle heavy-VM" case BUT-905 feared.
- **The BUT-905 "channel test pins call-site topology" concern does NOT apply when the assertion is gesture→message:** tap checkbox → `announces.messages contains l10n.a11yItemBought`. Extracting/renaming/moving the handler can't break it; only the user-audible behaviour can. The 2026-06-04 "announce = manual-territory" general rule is SUPERSEDED for sites reachable via locator-bridge or host-widget; it survives only for the genuinely scaffold-heavy third site (recipe_detail favorite, 610-line self-loading view) → deferred to **BUT-1225** with rationale.
- **Stub-mutates-seeded-state pattern for "re-read after refresh" announce branches:** the view computes `nowBought` by RE-reading `viewModel.completedItemsList` after the toggle's `_loadList()` refresh — so the mock's `toggleItemBought` stub must MUTATE `setShoppingState` inside its `thenAnswer` before returning true. That makes the production re-read path (service → _loadList → getter → ternary) genuinely observed; a stub returning bare `true` would leave the ternary untestable/false-green. Pair each direction positively AND negatively (`contains(bought)` + `isNot(contains(unbought))`) plus a failed-toggle `count == 0` (which doubles as "the full shell pump announces nothing spurious").
- **Known residual gap (non-blocking follow-up):** `_addItem` got the same Provider fix but NO test taps the add path — a regression re-introducing `context.read` in `_addItem` alone would go green. One full-shell test (enterText → tap 'Lägg till' → item appears / `addItem` verified) closes it; harness already exists post-BUT-1184.

### 2026-06-10 — BUT-1225 RecipeDetailView full-shell scaffolding + favorite announce (BUT-1212 site 3/3 closed) [Pattern]
The "genuinely scaffold-heavy" deferral from 2026-06-08/09 is now CLOSED — the 1100-line self-providing `RecipeDetailView` pumps as a full shell with ~9 locator registrations and zero lib/ seams. File: `test/views/recipe_detail_view_test.dart` (4 tests, green; mirrors the collaborative_shopping_view_test full-shell template). Recipe for the next monster view:
- **Registration set on top of `ViewTestHelpers.setupViewTestEnvironment()` defaults:** `UnifiedRecipeService` (own instance — seed `setRecipeState(recipes:[recipe], isInitialized:true)` + `when(toggleFavorite)`), `RecipeCookingService` (NOT in the default registry — `RecipeDetailViewModel`'s third ctor dep, throws "not registered" without it), `UserService` (stub `allergenPreferences → UserAllergenPreferences.defaults` — two `Selector<UserService, UserAllergenPreferences>`s read it during build; plus `addListener`/`removeListener` since the view exposes it via `ChangeNotifierProvider.value`), `SocialRecipeViewModel` (the State resolves+disposes it; use `MockSocialRecipeViewModel` from widget_mocks — concrete listener mgmt makes it Provider-safe; stub `initialize()`, `refreshComments(any())`, `topLevelComments → const []` for RecipeDetailComments' post-frame init + collapsed header), `OfflineService` (online, collab-test stubs), and a local `_FakeCookSnapService extends Fake implements CookSnapService` overriding `watchCookSnaps(String, {int limit = 20})` → `Stream.value(const [])` (no mock exists in test/; the override MUST keep the named `limit` or `invalid_override`). Defaults already cover PermissionService/AnalyticsService (concrete `.recipe` tracker via `_NoOpFutureMock`)/UnifiedFriendsService.
- **Seed recipe traps:** `createdBy: 'test-user-123'` (owner → fork stays in overflow, sharing-status short-circuits); NO imageUrls (hero takes the VegetableIllustration branch — no CachedNetworkImage network); **≥3 ingredients + ≥2 instructions** or `completenessScore < 0.7` renders the improve-banner whose Row OVERFLOWS horizontally at 420px (production layout smell at narrow widths, surfaced by this harness — banner Row at recipe_detail_shared_widgets.dart:166 has no Flexible). `RecipeFactory.build` defaults are BELOW the threshold.
- **Pump/tap mechanics:** 420x900 dpr 1.0 (mobile path, avoids RecipeDetailTabletContent); never `pumpAndSettle` (cook-snap gallery shows a CPI for one frame); two pumps + 50ms drains post-frame portion-init + comments-init + cook-snap stream. Tap the SliverAppBar hero favorite via `find.descendant(of: find.byKey(ValueKey('test-recipe-detail-favorite')), matching: find.byType(InkWell))` — pinned app bar, no scrolling/hit-test issues. Title asserts must be `.toLowerCase()`'d (design rule).
- **Domain-invariant test the autopilot draft misses:** failed toggle (`toggleFavorite → false`) must announce `a11yRecipeUnfavorited` — the announce reads `viewModel.recipe.isFavorite` AFTER the await, so on service failure the screen reader hears the reverted truth that matches the visibly-reverted heart. Pins against "compute the message before the await / from the pre-toggle state" regressions.
- BUT-1214's cook-snap visibility dialog lives behind `_showAddSnapSheet` (gesture-gated) — invisible to these pumps, no extra mocking needed.

### 2026-06-10 — BUT-1214 visibility write-chain pin (CookSnapService) [Pattern]
Coverage-verdict follow-up: read-side privacy coverage (model fromWire + repo onlyMe-exclusion + rules suite) does NOT pin the WRITE chain when every link is a pass-through with a safe-looking default. `dialog → vm.addSnap(visibility:) → service.addCookSnap(visibility:) → CookSnap.create(visibility:)` all default to `sameAsRecipe` — dropping any forward compiles clean, all read-side tests stay green, and "Bara jag" photos get silently shared. **Rule: for a privacy override threaded through N layers with defaults, pin at least the service→persisted-doc link with a capturing fake, both directions (override persists + omitted stays inherited).** File: `test/unit/services/cook_snap_service_visibility_test.dart` (2 tests, green).
- **Scaffolding recipe for `CookSnapService.addCookSnap` (full real flow, hermetic):** prod-bridge `setUpAll` + `BaseUnitTest.setupUnit()` + `TestServiceLocator.initialize()`, then override FOUR registrations: `AuthRepository` (**the default registry is UNAUTHENTICATED — `executeServiceOperation`'s pre-flight reads `AuthRepository.currentUserId` directly and silently returns null/defaultValue**, the classic false-"flow never ran" trap), `ConnectivityMonitoringService` (`isConnectedToInternet → true`; pure Mock, unstubbed getter throws), `ImagePickerService` (`pickImage(any()) → File('snap.jpg')` — returns `dart:io File`, no fallback needed since `ImageSource` fallback is registered in setUpAll), `ImageUploadService` (no shared mock exists — local `extends Mock implements`, stub `uploadImage(file: any(named:), userId: any(named:)) → UploadResult.success(url)`), `UserService` (`currentUserProfile → null` is a valid path, '?' fallback). `ActivityFeedService` needs NO mock — resolved inside `try{}catch(_){}`, the locator throw is swallowed. Keep `recipeAuthorId == currentUserId` to skip the NotificationService branch; `caption: null` skips ContentFilterService.
- Repo capture: `_CapturingCookSnapRepository extends Fake implements CookSnapRepository` — note `addCookSnap` returns `Future<CookSnap>` (echo the snap), and `FriendsRepository` ctor dep is a bare Fake (only read paths touch it).
- Dialog→VM wiring (RadioGroup "Bara jag" → `addSnap(visibility: onlyMe)`) deliberately NOT tested here — filed as a follow-up by the user; the BUT-1225 full-shell harness is the ready-made home for it.

### 2026-06-10 — BUT-1231 cook-snap dialog→service wiring closed via the BUT-1225 harness [Pattern]
The dialog half of the BUT-1214 chain is now pinned: `test/views/recipe_detail_view_test.dart` group "cook-snap visibility wiring" (4 tests, green) drives the REAL view's add-snap flow end to end — gallery `Icons.add_a_photo` IconButton → `ImagePickerDialogs.showImageSourceDialog` → BUT-1214 visibility dialog → recording `_FakeCookSnapService.addCookSnap`. Reusable mechanics:
- **The existing `_FakeCookSnapService` (stream-only) extends naturally into the capture seam**: add a recording `addCookSnap` override (callCount + lastRecipeId/lastSource/lastVisibility, return `CookSnap.create(...)`). The view creates `CookSnapViewModel` internally via `ChangeNotifierProvider(create:)`, so the service IS the practical seam — no VM injection needed, consistent with the BUT-1212 locator-bridge ruling. The override must mirror the FULL signature incl. `visibility = CookSnapVisibility.sameAsRecipe` default or `invalid_override`.
- **`ImagePickerDialogs.showImageSourceDialog` is fully drivable in widget tests** — plain `showModalBottomSheet` with l10n ListTiles (`commonSelectFromGallery` / `commonTakePhoto`), no platform channel until AFTER a source is returned (and the fake service means the picker itself never runs). Two pumps + 300ms cover sheet slide-in; same again covers sheet-pop → dialog fade-in.
- **The dialog only fires for a non-private audience** (`cookSnapAudience`: `isPublic` → public; personal non-collaborative → private skips). Seed `RecipeFactory.build(..., isPublic: true, createdBy: testUser)` in a nested-group `setUp` reassigning the outer `recipe` + re-seeding `setRecipeState` — `pumpDetailView` reads the outer variable at call time, so no helper changes. Public+personal keeps `RecipeDetailSharingStatus` short-circuited (it gates on `isCollaborative || isShared`, not `isPublic`).
- **Off-screen gallery affordance**: `tester.ensureVisible(find.byIcon(Icons.add_a_photo))` works (the whole mobile Column lives in one box-sliver, so it's built while off-screen). Scope dialog-button taps via `find.descendant(of: find.byType(AlertDialog), ...)` — `commonCancel` can exist elsewhere in the tree under the barrier.

### 2026-06-20 — WS9 crash-safety deserialization tests: review verdict [Pattern reviewed]
Three test files reviewed for WS9 (data-model crash-safety). All three pass the intent gate.

**`serialization_utils_safe_enum_index_test.dart`** — all five failure modes (valid, past-end, negative, String, null, double) are distinct code paths and all are covered. Would fail on reversion to bare `.values[index]`. Not fragile to theme/schema changes (uses a local private `_Sample` enum). One minor fence-post gap: no exact-boundary test (`values.length - 1` must succeed; `values.length` must fall back). A `safeEnumByIndex(values.length - 1, ...)` and `safeEnumByIndex(values.length, ...)` pair would make a `< vs <=` regression visible. Low severity — the past-end crash is still caught; add when the file is next touched.

**`recipe_serialization_safety_test.dart`** — correctly tests `fromJson` for the out-of-range case and `fromMap` for the non-int case (matching the two distinct call sites in production). Round-trip test locks the happy path. One pre-commit check worth confirming: the `defaultValue` arg in production's `safeEnumByIndex` call for `RecipeType` must be `RecipeType.personal` (index 0) for the `expect(..., RecipeType.personal)` assertions to be intent-gated rather than accidentally correct.

**`quantity_parser_test.dart`** — all three digit-run positions (numerator-only, denominator-only, mixed) are covered. Would fail if `int.parse` (throws `FormatException`) replaced `int.tryParse`. The division-by-zero guard is also covered. No missing position.

**`ResourcePermission.memberPermissions` path** — mechanism-level coverage via `safeEnumByIndex` unit tests is sufficient; a separate heavyweight integration test is not warranted.

**Reusable rule: for a `safeEnumByIndex` helper, the minimum test matrix is (valid, past-end, negative, non-int-type, null). Always include a String test because Firestore occasionally deserializes enum fields as strings when documents were written by older schema-less writers.**
- **Dismissal asserts need the pop animation drained**: `findsNothing` on the dialog's radio key fails at pump+50ms (dialog still fading); pump+300ms after the cancel tap. The upload-count assert alone would have passed — don't weaken to it, the "dialog actually left" half catches a pop-swallowing regression.
- **Domain-invariant test the autopilot draft misses**: private recipe → NO dialog AND the upload still happens with `sameAsRecipe` (a regression gating the upload on the never-shown dialog would break adding snaps to personal recipes — the "no friction" half of BUT-1214 is two-sided). Deliberately excluded: a source-sheet-dismiss test (`source == null` early return) — pure framework behaviour, proves nothing of ours.
- Transient gotcha: a parallel session mid-refactor on lib/ can fail `flutter test` compilation on files you never touched (here `input_components.dart`/`RecipeIngredient`); re-run before investigating, per the parallel-sessions rule.

### 2026-06-10 — BUT-444 structured-first scaling gap-check: routing links were the gaps, logic was clean [Pattern + gaps closed]
Iter-137 gap-check on the BUT-444 diff (scaleEntries + optional `structuredIngredients` threaded recipe_detail_content → InputComponents → PortionScaler, plus the cooking-mode VM swap). The 8 logic tests + alignment-getter tests + BUT-1228 producer tests were all sound — but BOTH consuming surfaces' WIRING was unpinned: reverting `PortionScaler._scale` (or the VM line) to the string path kept all 242 tests green. This is the BUT-1214 "defaults-threaded chain" pattern verbatim: every layer's `null → legacy path` fallback makes a dropped forward compile-clean and silently user-visible. Closed with 3 tests:
- **Widget routing pin** (`portion_scaler_orchestration_test.dart`, group "Structured-first routing (BUT-444)"): drive through `InputComponents.portionScaler` (pins facade forward + widget `_scale` branch in ONE test) with a string-parser-defeating fixture (`ca 2,5 dl vispgrädde`, amount 2.5), tap `Icons.add` 1→2 → callback delivers `'5 dl vispgrädde'`. Paired with an omitted-param test pinning the legacy output.
- **VM pin** (`cooking_mode_viewmodel_test.dart`): `Recipe.copyWith` does NOT expose `structuredIngredients` (only `RecipeCore.copyWith` does — preserve-only by design) → construct `Recipe(core: RecipeCore(..., structuredIngredients: [...]))` directly. `updatePortions(8)` from 4 → `'5 dl vispgrädde'`.
- **Probe-then-pin correction:** the diff's comments claimed the string path returns `ca 2,5 dl …` "unscaled" — FALSE. It MANGLES it: quantity coerced to 1.0, "ca" dropped → `'2 dl vispgrädde'` at factor 2. Pinned that actual output as the documented-wrong legacy contract (tripwire comment: update if the string path ever learns "ca"). Rule: when a test comment asserts what the OTHER path would do, run that path before trusting it — the discriminator may be stronger (or weaker) than claimed.
- **Residual non-blocking gap:** the `recipe_detail_content.dart:591` call-site named-arg itself (one dropped `structuredIngredients:` forward = detail view silently legacy). Cheapest pin if wanted: one test in the BUT-1225 full-shell harness — seed recipe with the "ca" structured entry + portions, ensureVisible + tap the scaler's `Icons.add`, assert `'5 dl vispgrädde'` text renders. Same family as the BUT-1212 `_addItem` residual. **CLOSED by BUT-1233 (2026-06-11, entry below).**

### 2026-06-11 — BUT-1233: detail-view `structuredIngredients:` call-site pinned in the full-shell harness [Pattern — closes the BUT-444 residual]
The last unpinned BUT-444 link (recipe_detail_content `structuredIngredients: viewModel.recipe.structuredIngredients`) is now covered: `test/views/recipe_detail_view_test.dart` group "structured-first portion scaling (BUT-444/BUT-1233)", 1 test, green (suite 9/9). Mechanics worth keeping:
- **Seeding structured entries through the test factory:** `test/infrastructure/factories/recipe_factory.dart` does NOT expose `structuredIngredients` (the PRODUCTION `lib/models/recipe/recipe_factory.dart` does, but importing it collides on the class name). Build the base via the test factory, then `Recipe(core: base.core.copyWith(structuredIngredients: [...]), type: ...)` — `RecipeCore.copyWith` preserves `id` (line ~469) and exposes the field via the sentinel pattern.
- **Alignment is all-or-nothing:** `Recipe.structuredIngredients` (recipe_unified.dart:1214) serves the stored list ONLY when length matches AND every `stored[i].raw == ingredients[i]`; one mismatch degrades the WHOLE list to rawOnly → the test silently re-routes through the string path and false-greens its own premise. With ≥3 ingredient lines (completeness banner) the unstructured companions must be `RecipeIngredient.rawOnly(lines[i])`, not omitted.
- **The detail view does NOT render the scaled string verbatim** — `_buildIngredientRow` re-parses each line via `IngredientParser.parseIngredient` into a quantity cell (`'5 dl'`) and a name cell (`'vispgrädde'`). Assert `find.text('5 dl')` EXACT, never `textContaining('5 dl')` — the unscaled raw line `'ca 2,5 dl vispgrädde'` CONTAINS `'5 dl'`, so a contains-match would false-pass on a scaling no-op. Negative half: `find.text('2 dl'), findsNothing` (the pinned legacy mangle at factor 2).
- **Driving the stepper in the full shell:** scope the plus via `find.descendant(of: find.byType(PortionScaler), matching: find.byIcon(Icons.add))` (the gallery's `add_a_photo` is a different icon but scoping is cheap insurance), `ensureVisible` once (whole mobile column is one box-sliver), then 4 taps 4→8 with `pump() + pump(100ms)` between each (bounce animation; harness rule: no pumpAndSettle). Pick non-`dl` companion lines (`'3 ägg'`, `'4 msk socker'`) so their scaled quantity cells can't collide with the asserted texts.

### 2026-06-11 — BUT-956 MenuShoppingListGenerator orchestration contract (uncommitted SUT moved mid-task) [Pattern + User correction-equivalent]
`test/unit/services/shopping/menu_shopping_list_generator_test.dart` (8 tests, green) pins the generator on top of the already-pinned aggregator. Three lessons:
- **An UNCOMMITTED SUT can change between your first Read and your test run — when a logically-impossible failure appears ("empty plan returned a full result"), re-Read the lib file BEFORE debugging the harness.** Here a parallel/coordinating session had evolved the contract mid-task: empty-plan/all-unresolved now return the `MenuShoppingGenerationResult.nothingToGenerate` const sentinel (`isEmptyPlan == true`; check it, don't `same()` — the view-facing getter is the contract) and NULL strictly means FAILURE via the service error path. The brief's "empty plan → null" was stale; current code-read trumps the brief (Step-0 premise-verification rule applies to briefs too). Bonus: the bought-key had ALSO been upgraded from `toLowerCase` to `SwedishCharacterNormalizer.normalize(name) + unit.toLowerCase().trim()` with a comment defending it — that comment is a test REQUEST: pin existing `'Mjöl'` bought:true + fresh aggregation display `'mjol'` → bought survives (a toLowerCase regression produces `'mjöl|dl' != 'mjol|dl'` → red).
- **Harness recipe for a locator-resolving `BaseService` orchestrator over `MockUnifiedShoppingService`:** prod-bridge setUp (BaseUnitTest.setupUnit + TestServiceLocator.initialize + authenticated `MockFactory.createAuthRepository` override per the BUT-1214 trap) + `registerMock` for WeeklyMenuPlanService (local `extends Mock`)/UnifiedRecipeService (seed `setRecipeState(recipes:, isInitialized: true)` — `getRecipeById` is CONCRETE, don't `when()` it)/UnifiedShoppingService. **`createPersonalList`'s stub must MUTATE `setShoppingState` inside its `thenAnswer` to add the created list** (the generator re-reads `service.lists.firstWhere` after creating — production-mirror, same stub-mutates-seeded-state shape as BUT-1212). `personalLists`/`lists` are concrete state-backed getters; `createPersonalList`/`updateList` are clean stubs.
- **When null doubles as the service-layer failure signal, an un-stubbed write stub IS a false test failure**: forgetting `when(updateList).thenAnswer((_) async => true)` in one test made mocktail's missing-stub throw → `safeExecute` swallowed → null → "Expected not null". Every path that reaches a write needs the stub; the loud `reason:` on each `isNotNull` ("null is the failure path / auth pre-flight") made the diagnosis instant. Sentinel tests pair `isEmptyPlan == true` with `verifyNever(createPersonalList)` + `verifyNever(updateList)`. ISO-week name pinned as a LITERAL (`'Inköpslista v.24'` for 2026-06-10) — no tautological re-derivation via IsoWeekUtils in the assertion.

### 2026-06-11 — BUT-1234 marker-lookup review [Pattern discovered]
**Marker-equality lookup (`l.generatedForWeek == weekKey`) needs THREE pins, not two**: (1) marked-same-key → reused (rename test), (2) unmarked name-collision → untouched, AND (3) **marked-for-a-DIFFERENT-key → untouched, new list created**. Without (3), a regression to `generatedForWeek != null` ("any generated list") passes the whole suite — last week's W23 list gets hijacked and overwritten on W24 generation. General rule: every `field == expected` selector test set needs a wrong-value negative, not just a null/absent negative.

### 2026-06-11 — BUT-838/694 review: ordering pins + same-repo fixture mirrors [Pattern discovered]
- **"No local +1 compensation" contracts need a `verifyInOrder` pin.** When a VM reads an aggregate that is only correct BECAUSE the write committed first (cook event in the same batch → `countSince` already includes this cook), a canned mock count goes green regardless of call order. Pin `verifyInOrder([write, count])` or a count-moved-before-write refactor silently reintroduces the off-by-one (habitual one cook late — the exact BUT-830 bug class).
- **Cross-port fixture "byte-identical copy" claims should be mechanically enforced when both files live in the SAME repo.** A Dart test can `File('functions/src/__tests__/fixtures/x.json').readAsBytesSync()` from the package root and compare bytes against its local copy — turning the comment-only sync convention into a failing test. `hasLength(N)` only catches count drift, not content drift (TS updates a vector's expected, Dart copy goes stale, both suites stay green while ports diverge).

### 2026-06-11 — BUT-1214 square-restyle of a radio dialog [Pattern discovered]
Replacing `RadioListTile` with a hand-rolled selected-tile (InkWell + `Semantics(selected:)`) keeps key-based end-to-end tests green but opens ONE new gap: RadioListTile rendered selection from `groupValue` for free; the hand-rolled tile re-renders only if the tap handler calls `setDialogState`. A regression mutating the local `selected` var WITHOUT setState still passes the service-level pin (pop value correct) while the user gets zero visual feedback. Pin it behaviorally via semantics, not colors: `tester.ensureSemantics()` + `tester.getSemantics(find.byKey(...))` + `containsSemantics(isSelected: true/false)` before/after the tap, asserting BOTH tiles (single-choice exclusivity). Theme-proof, and doubles as the radio-picker a11y rule check (`.claude/rules/ui-conventions.md` rule 4). Don't add tests for the styling itself (square shape, cream bg, lowercase title) — pure restyle.

### 2026-06-12 — iter-141 review: PantryViewModel.restoreItem VM gap + PantryItemCard undo-flow gap [Pattern discovered]
Reviewing 7 iter-141 test files. All 6 new/extended test files are sound. Two follow-up gaps identified:
- **PantryViewModel.restoreItem has NO VM test.** The service test covers `PantryService.restoreItem` (round-trip id, validation rejection). The VM layer's "append restored item with NEW id to `_items`" invariant is uncovered — a regression using the old item (wrong id in the list) or not appending at all is silent. This is the BUT-1068 list-state-mismatch family. File as a P2 ticket; VM test shape: prod-bridge setUp + `registerMock<PantryService>` + seed `_items` with one item via `loadPantry` stub + call `vm.restoreItem(item)` + assert `vm.items.last.id == newId` + `verifyNever(repo.add)` (repo is behind the service mock).
- **PantryItemCard swipe→undo flow has no widget test.** `_removeWithUndo` wires `context.read<PantryViewModel>()` + `SnackBarUtils.showSuccessWithAction`. A full-shell Dismissible swipe test (pump the card inside a `ChangeNotifierProvider<PantryViewModel>.value` + `Scaffold`) would prove (a) `removeItem` called, (b) snackbar with "Ångra" label, (c) tapping Ångra calls `restoreItem(item)`. The `context.read` in `build()` is the BUT-1212 ProviderNotFoundException risk class — it's correctly scoped here (card IS inside the provider), but a test proves it stays correct. P2 follow-up.
- **Weak throw-type assertion** in pantry_service_test restoreItem: `throwsA(isA<Object>())` — should be `isA<ValidationException>()`. P3 in-file fix.
- **Pattern reconfirmed: `throwsA(isA<Object>())` is always a smell** — it passes on any throw including test harness errors. Every new service test's rejection branch should specify the concrete exception type or at minimum `isA<Exception>()`.
- **Theme factory tests** (dialog_theme_square_test) correctly call the pure factory function directly without pumping a widget — this is the right approach for testing theme component factories. No widget pump needed; the contract is "given a ColorScheme, produce the correct ThemeData." Confirmed pattern for future theme-component tests.

### 2026-06-12 — BUT-1241 review: diff doc-header claims against the test list [Pattern discovered]
When a test file's library doc enumerates the contract ("baseEntriesOverride replaces entries for the original week **but NOT for other weeks**"), diff that claim list against the actual `test(...)` blocks — the unpinned half is where silent-regression risk hides. Found: `MenuPlacementViewModel._loadWeek`'s `normalized == _originalWeekStart` guard had no test; deleting the guard passes all 14 tests yet clobbers a NEIGHBOR week's saved entries with the redo base, which `confirm()` would then persist (data loss). The week-navigation test used no override, so it couldn't catch it. Also good in that suite: mock-the-repo-facing-methods + delegate-pure-methods-to-a-REAL-WeeklyMenuPlanService keeps placement semantics genuine without Firestore scaffolding — reusable for any VM orchestrating a pure-function-heavy service.

### 2026-06-26 — BUT-1371 Paprika multi-recipe import: bomb-guard integration gap added [Bug found + Pattern]

The three original tests for BUT-1371 (`file_import_paprika_test.dart`) proved:
1. `importMultipleFromContent` collects every recipe (regression for the dropped-bulk-path bug).
2. A non-gzip entry is skipped, not fatal.
3. `importFromContent` single-path still returns the first recipe.

All three are sound and would fail on real regressions. However, none of them exercised the `DecompressionGuard` interaction at the `_parsePaprikaRecipes` level.

**Gap found: an entry whose gunzipped JSON exceeds `DecompressionGuard.defaultMaxEntryBytes` was skipped rather than imported, but no test verified this.** `guard.accept(...)` throws `DecompressionBombException`, caught by the per-entry `catch(e)` block — but if someone removed the `guard.accept()` call, dropped the `catch`, or changed the catch to re-throw, the bomb-path would OOM the app and no test would go red.

**Fourth test added:** builds a zip with one oversized gzip entry (payload `defaultMaxEntryBytes + 1` bytes of spaces, a valid GZip of valid UTF-8 that inflates too large) and one valid recipe entry. Asserts `importMultipleFromContent` returns length 1 with title `'Fruktsoppa'` — the oversized entry is skipped, the valid sibling is collected, no exception escapes. The `DecompressionGuard` import was added to the test file.

**Why the empty-archive case is NOT a gap:** a valid empty zip (no entries) → empty loop → `[]` returned safely by `importMultipleFromContent`'s outer `try/catch`. A garbage/non-zip byte sequence → `ZipDecoder` throws → caught by `importMultipleFromContent`'s outer `catch (e) { return []; }`. Both paths are safe due to existing outer catches, and the `DecompressionGuard` unit tests in `decompression_guard_test.dart` already cover the guard in isolation.

**Pattern: when a multi-entry loop has a per-entry try/catch that absorbs a bomb-guard exception, add a test proving the absorb happens rather than re-throwing.** The `DecompressionGuard` unit tests prove the guard THROWS; the integration test proves the CALLER survives. Both layers are needed.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-925/1243/1154 sprint review [Pattern discovered]
- **Theme-component factory test shape confirmed (BUT-1243):** calling `FeedbackThemes.snackBarTheme(cs)` directly and asserting `shape is RoundedRectangleBorder + borderRadius == BorderRadius.zero` is the correct approach for theme-factory contract tests. Passes without widget pump. Pattern already noted in BUT-1241/pantry entry — this confirms it. The background test asserting `cs.inverseSurface` is the right "theme-resolved, not hardcoded" idiom (captures the live scheme, not `Color(0xFF...)`).
- **`AppColors.neutralMedium` hardcode in production widget creates a matching hardcode in test** — when production resolves a color from `AppColors.xxx` rather than `context.butleryColors.xxx`, the test legitimately mirrors the production constant. This is not a test smell; it's a production design-language smell (BUT-1243 sibling: `low/failed` confidence grey should be a `ButleryColors` slot). Flag in the production widget's `// TODO`, not in the test.
- **`ConfidencePill.confidenceColorFor()` static helper exposed as `@visibleForTesting`** is the correct seam pattern: the test asserts the static-method output equals the live `ButleryColors.light` constant AND that the rendered pill border matches that same value. This two-way check (static-function path + render path) is stronger than either alone — a refactor diverging the two paths fails at least one assertion.
- **BUT-1154 pure extraction: smoke test + viewmodel test are the right regression gate.** Both passed unmodified. The `ImportResultHandler` static-method class (extracted from SmartImportView) owns real branching logic (4 `DuplicateMergeChoice` cases + threshold arithmetic) but is deeply coupled to `ServiceLocator.get<UnifiedRecipeService>()` in its body, making unit-isolation costly. A follow-up ticket warranted IF any branch is edited; pure extraction is adequately covered by the smoke test proving DI resolves. The `PendingImportBanner` extracted widget (pure presentational, two callbacks) and all `user_profile_edit/` extracted widgets (pure presentational, VM passed as argument) have no behavior to test beyond "callbacks reach the parent" — the BUT-754 fan-out pattern: no standalone test owed, covered by the parent view's smoke test.
- **`_ConfidencePill` widget uses a `ValueKey('confidence-pill-${confidence.name}')` seam** — this is a well-designed test seam: stable, domain-meaningful, doesn't encode visual structure. When a widget has an enum-per-instance render variation, expose `ValueKey('domain-noun-${enumValue.name}')` for the container so tests can find each variant without `find.byType().at(N)` fragility.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-1245/1244 iter-143 quality audit [Pattern — adequate, one follow-up gap]

**BUT-1245 (`import_result_handler_test.dart`) — STRONG, NOT TAUTOLOGICAL.**
Production exposes two `@visibleForTesting` static methods: `meetsContentDuplicateThreshold(score) => score >= 0.6` and `clampToExactMatchFloor(score) => score < 0.8 ? 0.8 : score`. Both are called in the real `checkForDuplicates` body (confirmed in production). The 9 tests pin ALL load-bearing boundary shapes: `0.5999→false` / `0.6→true` (catches `>=`→`>` flip); `0.7999→0.8` / `0.8→0.8` / `0.95→0.95` (catches `<`→`<=` on either side of the floor). The 2026-05-29 "TierResult.success overrides quality" trap does NOT apply here — both helpers are pure one-liners with no intermediate recomputation. Follow-up gap: no test exercises the CALLSITE CONTRACT — i.e. that `checkForDuplicates` actually invokes these two methods in the correct order (content-threshold gate before clamp). A callsite regression (e.g. applying the clamp BEFORE the threshold check, or skipping the threshold for URL matches) would pass all 9 tests.
