This is a genuine but LOW-PRIORITY gap because (a) `checkForDuplicates` body is covered by the existing 521 parsing tests passing unchanged, and (b) the static methods are only two calls in a 40-line method with no other branching arithmetic. Worth a follow-up ticket, not a commit blocker.

**BUT-1244 (`parse_confidence_review_test.dart`) — STRONG AFTER REFACTOR. l10n did NOT weaken the assertions.**
The BUT-1244 change replaced hardcoded `AppColors.neutralMedium` with `ButleryColors.neutral` and hardcoded Swedish strings with `context.l10n.*` keys. **Key finding: the color assertions now compare `_pillBorderColor` (extracted from the live rendered Container's BoxDecoration) against `ButleryColors.light.neutral/success/warning` — these are `static const` values baked into the `ButleryColors` extension. The test is NOT doing `colors.neutral == colors.neutral` (trivially true); it is asserting the RENDERED widget's color equals the semantic token.** If production's `_colorFor` switch were changed to use `colors.warning` for low/failed instead of `colors.neutral`, the test goes red. The `confidenceColorFor()` unit test (non-widget, line 138) plus the 4 widget tests create a TWO-PATH check: static-function path + render path must agree. One gap: the color-comparison approach compares against `ButleryColors.light` directly rather than resolving the extension from the live `BuildContext` via a `Builder`. This means if a dark-mode host somehow supplies a different `ButleryColors` instance, the assertion could give a false green. In practice `_wrap()` hardwires `theme: AppTheme.lightTheme` so the live resolved extension IS `ButleryColors.light` — the const comparison is equivalent for the test's target scenario. The l10n-resolved-label test (line 114) resolves `AppLocalizations.of(tester.element(...))` from the pumped tree and compares `find.text(entry.value)` — survives ARB copy edits because it reads the live resolved string, not a hardcoded literal. Overall: the refactor genuinely improved the tests; no assertions were weakened.

**BUT-862 (isolate offload) — the 521 existing parsing tests are NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT as the only regression evidence.** They prove the parsing logic is intact at the service level, but they do not pin the serialization round-trip: that `toIsolateMap()` and `fromIsolateMap()` (or equivalent encode/decode helpers) are lossless for every field. A `toIsolateMap→fromIsolateMap` round-trip test is warranted as a follow-up: if a new field is added to the parse output model but its serializer is omitted, the parse tests stay green while the field silently disappears across the isolate boundary. **Recommend a follow-up ticket.**

**Run results: 9/9 green (BUT-1245), 10/10 green (BUT-1244). Both suites clean, no failures, no skips.**

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-648/BUT-1057 iter-144 quality audit [Pattern — mostly strong, one meaningful gap]

**BUT-648 (`schema_version_test.dart`) — STRONG. Tests prove the lazy-default-1 contract.**
All 18 tests (3×6 models) are green. Assertion quality:
- **(a) default-1:** Trivial but NOT tautological — confirms the ctor default is wired. A developer removing `this.schemaVersion = 1` from any model's initializer list would fail this.
- **(b) round-trip:** Strong — calls real `toFirestore()` then real `fromMap()`. Every model's `toFirestore()` explicitly writes `'schemaVersion': schemaVersion` (confirmed in production) and `fromMap()` reads `data['schemaVersion'] as int? ?? 1`. Removing the `?? 1` from the READ path → (b) would still pass (value is written and read back as 1). But (c) catches that: removing `?? 1` from `fromMap` while (c) passes a map WITHOUT the key → throws a null cast → red. So (b) and (c) are complementary: (b) catches "serializer drops the field", (c) catches "deserializer drops the default". Together they are sufficient. No tautology.
- **(c) legacy-absent:** The strongest of the three. Each legacy map is manually constructed WITHOUT the `schemaVersion` key, confirming that old Firestore documents get `1` on read. This is the exact regression a `as int` (no `?? 1`) refactor would break.
- **UserProfile edge note:** Test (b) calls `profile.toFirestore()` → `UserProfile.fromMap()`. `toFirestore()` writes to the `public_profiles` shape, which IS the same data structure `fromMap()` reads — the round-trip is coherent.
- **No gap in the 6-model scope.** The chosen models (UserProfile, RecipeCore, WeeklyMenuPlan, UnifiedShoppingList, PersonalTag, SharedMenu) are the ones BUT-648 added the field to. If additional models gain `schemaVersion` later, tests should be added at that point.

**BUT-1057 (`related_recipes_test.dart`) — STRONG. All critical contracts pinned. One meaningful gap.**

10/10 green. Assertion quality per concern:
- **Editor chips (tests 1-2):** Test 1 confirms BOTH chips render with service-resolved titles. Test 2 captures `unlinkedId` and asserts it equals the target id (`'r2'`) — not just "callback fired." Strong.
- **RelatedRecipesSection hide/show (tests 3-6):** BOTH empty cases pinned separately (`null` and `[]`). The populated case asserts BOTH section title AND recipe title (two-signal). The ghost-id case (all resolved to null) is a meaningful edge: the `whereType<Recipe>()` filter causes `linked.isEmpty → SizedBox.shrink()` even with a non-empty id list — pinned by test 6.
- **UsedInSection hide/show (tests 7-8):** Both cases pinned. Hidden test uses a recipe with `relatedIds: null` (not an empty list), which exercises the `?.contains(...) ?? false` path. Shown test uses a recipe with `relatedIds: ['r1']` and confirms both section title AND linked-recipe title render.
- **Navigation (test 9):** Uses semantics label `RegExp(r'Öppna relaterat recept: Pastasås')` to find the thumbnail — tests the actual a11y label wired in production (`a11yRelatedRecipeThumbnail(recipe.title)` → Swedish `'Öppna relaterat recept: {title}'`). Captures both route name AND the `Recipe` object argument. Asserts `navigatedRecipe?.id == 'r2'` (correct recipe) AND `find.text('Pastasås')` in the pushed screen (navigation succeeded). This is the right level of pin — it would fail if `Routes.recipeDetail` were wrong OR if `arguments` carried the wrong type/id.
- **Meaningful gap — link-via-picker flow (onLink callback path):** `_openPicker` calls `RelatedRecipesPickerDialog.show(context, ...)`. The tests stub `onLink: (_) async => true` but never trigger the picker flow. The editor's "link button" test only confirms the button renders — it does NOT tap the button and exercise the dialog → onLink chain. A regression making the button non-functional (e.g. `onPressed: null`) would not be caught. This is the "callback-stubbed but not end-to-end" gap the audit request anticipated. Worth a follow-up ticket. Mitigation: `RelatedRecipesPickerDialog.show` is itself a `showDialog` call and would require separate scaffolding; the gap is real but the picker dialog is a separate widget with its own planned tests.
- **No "unlink snackbar fires on success/failure" coverage.** `_handleUnlink` shows `recipeRelatedUnlinkSuccess` or `recipeRelatedUnlinkError`. The test confirms the callback fires with the correct id but does NOT assert the snackbar text. Low-priority gap (the snackbar is a `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar` one-liner).

**General pattern reconfirmed:** semantics-label-based navigation assertions (via `tester.ensureSemantics()` + `find.bySemanticsLabel`) are the correct approach for "did the tap navigate correctly?" — theme-proof, survives visual refactors, pinned against the real localized label key.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-1251 resetForLogout index-leak gap [Pattern + Bug-prevention]
**`resetForLogout` bypasses `notifyListeners`, so any auxiliary state rebuilt by `notifyListeners` (like an O(1) `_recipeById` index) must be cleared EXPLICITLY in `resetForLogout` too.** Without `_recipeById.clear()`, the index retained the previous user's recipes after logout — `getRecipeById` would leak stale data into a new session. The production fix was a one-liner (BUT-1251); the regression guard is a test that (a) seeds a recipe into the service index via `createCollaborativeRecipe`, (b) calls `resetForLogout`, (c) asserts `getRecipeById(thatId) == null`. Two pre-condition asserts (`recipeId != null`, `getRecipeById` is non-null BEFORE logout) make a false-green impossible. **General rule: whenever a production path (logout, reset, clear) intentionally skips `notifyListeners`, audit every field rebuilt by `notifyListeners` — each one is a potential stale-index regression that needs an explicit clear + a matching test.** Test location: `test/unit/services/unified/unified_recipe_service_test.dart`, "Recipe Retrieval" group.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-1247/BUT-1250 callsite-order contract + picker end-to-end flow [Pattern + unawaited-future bottom-sheet pump]

**BUT-1247: gate-before-clamp ordering test via unawaited-future + bottom sheet interaction.**
`ImportResultHandler.checkForDuplicates` combines async service calls (`findBySourceUrl`, `findByTitle`) with `showModalBottomSheet`. Two patterns needed:

- **Sub-threshold test (a) — use `tester.runAsync`**: when the method returns `true` without opening a sheet (gate blocks), `tester.runAsync(() async => checkForDuplicates(...))` is safe because the method's awaited chain resolves and returns before any dialog is shown. No deadlock.
- **Above-threshold test (b) — unawaited-future pattern**: `tester.runAsync` deadlocks here because the callback awaits `showModalBottomSheet` which itself awaits user interaction, and we can't pump the widget tree from inside `runAsync`. **Fix: start the future WITHOUT await (`// ignore: unawaited_futures`), then drain the mock async chain with sequential `pump()` calls** (one per `await findBySourceUrl` / `await findByTitle` / showModalBottomSheet entrance), then interact with the rendered sheet, then `await resultFuture`. The testWidgets fake-async zone advances mocked Futures through `pump()` while the sheet is visible.
- **Ordering proof**: the regression is concrete — if `clampToExactMatchFloor` ran before `meetsContentDuplicateThreshold`, a Jaccard=0.0 score would be clamped to 0.8, which passes the 0.6 gate, and the sheet would appear. Test (a) asserts `find.text('Spara som nytt'), findsNothing` (sheet absent = gate blocked it). If clamp-before-gate were introduced, test (a) would deadlock (sheet appears, `runAsync` never returns) or fail the `findsNothing` assertion.
- **`showDuplicateMergeSheet` uses `showModalBottomSheet`, NOT `showDialog`/`AlertDialog`**. Always check which `show*` function production code uses before writing finders. Bottom sheet buttons are found by `find.text(...)` on the button label; there is no `find.byType(AlertDialog)` for bottom sheets.
- **Pumping sequence for a bottom sheet**: `pump()` × N to drain each `await` point in the method, then `pump()` once more for `showModalBottomSheet` to push its route, then `pump(Duration(milliseconds: 300))` for the entrance animation. Exact count depends on how many `await` points precede the `show*` call.

**BUT-1250: `RelatedRecipesPickerDialog` DI wiring — register the configured VM instance, not just the type.**
`RelatedRecipesPickerDialog.initState` calls `ServiceLocator.get<RecipeListViewModel>()` directly on the production locator. The default `TestServiceLocator._registerViewModels` registers a factory (new `MockRecipeListViewModel` per call) — that works for most cases but NOT when you need a specific pre-configured instance. **Fix: call `TestServiceLocator.registerMock<RecipeListViewModel>(mockVm)` AFTER calling `setRecipeListState(...)` on the mock.** This unregisters the factory and registers the specific configured singleton, so `ServiceLocator.get<RecipeListViewModel>()` from inside `initState` returns your pre-seeded mock. Both `production.ServiceLocator.initialize(DIContainer())` (setUpAll) and `TestServiceLocator.initialize()` (setUp) are required to wire the prod↔test bridge.
- **`MockRecipeListViewModel.dispose()` is safe to call from `RelatedRecipesPickerDialog.dispose()`** because `MockBaseViewModel` (the parent) has a concrete `dispose()` implementation that calls the real `ChangeNotifier.dispose()` — `ChangeNotifierProvider.value` inside the dialog does NOT call dispose on it (only `ChangeNotifierProvider(create:)` does), so there is no double-dispose trap.
- **Confirm button label in `RelatedRecipesPickerDialog`**: `'${l10n.commonAdd} (${_selectedIds.length})'` = "Lägg till (1)" when one item is selected. Find it with `find.text('Lägg till (1)')` or `find.textContaining('Lägg till')` — either works, but the parenthetical count guards against showing the button with 0 items.
- **Picker dialog tests live at `test/widget/recipe/related_recipes_picker_test.dart`**, not inside the existing `related_recipes_test.dart`, because the picker has its own DI setup (prod bridge + `RecipeListViewModel` singleton registration) that would pollute the existing file's simpler setUp.

### 2026-06-13 — iter-146 quality review (BUT-1053/1247/1250) [Review]
All 13 tests green in one run (9 unit + 2 widget callsite + 2 widget picker). Key findings:

**BUT-1053 (OCR locale helpers):** Unknown-locale assertions for `ocrSpaceLanguage` and `tesseractLanguage` are LOOSE — they assert only that both languages are PRESENT (`contains`/`containsAll`) without pinning ORDER. For `ocrSpaceLanguage` this is actually fine (single-code return, no ordering). For `tesseractLanguage('de')` the production code returns `'swe+eng'` (Swedish-first, matches the comment "app default audience") but the test allows `eng+swe` equally — if an implementer flips to English-first for unknown locales, the test goes green when it should fail. Acceptable residual only IF the ordering contract for unknown locales is explicitly not committed-to; pin order if it matters. `googleVisionLanguageHints('de')` returns `['sv','en']` in production; `containsAll(['sv','en'])` doesn't require sv-first — identical loose-order concern (though a reorder bug on the UNKNOWN branch is extremely low risk).

**BUT-1247 (callsite gate/clamp ordering):** Test (a) — gate before clamp — is genuinely intent-gated: 0.0 similarity with completely disjoint ingredient sets, asserting `result==true` AND `find.text('Spara som nytt')` absent. If clamp ran first (0.0→0.8, above the 0.6 gate), the future would never complete synchronously and either hang or produce `false` — the test fails on both regression vectors. This is the correct shape. Test (b) — content path above gate — drives the real bottom sheet with a 0.70 score (identical ingredients, different titles) and asserts the sheet appears then resolves `true` on "Spara som nytt" tap. Intent solid. The implementer's noted gap (clamp-only-on-URL-match not fully driven by its OWN dedicated test) is acceptable: the clamp path is a display calculation for `matchScore` shown in the sheet's similarity percentage — it runs on line 118 of `import_result_handler.dart` only when `matchScore==1.0 && matches.first.ingredients.isNotEmpty`. A display regression (0% shown instead of 80%) is user-visible but low-risk and not a save-correctness issue; the separate BUT-1245 pure-helper tests pin the 0.8 boundary itself without any context.

**BUT-1250 (picker flow):** Test 1 drives `link → picker opens → select Pastasås → confirm → onLink('r-pasta')`. Test 2 seeds two recipes, selects only Pastasås, and asserts `linkedIds == ['r-pasta']` (not `['r-pasta','r-tacos']`). Both tests pin the EXACT id — not just "not null". The single-selection exclusivity test (test 2) is the higher-value assertion and its `reason:` makes the failure message self-documenting. Optimistic-chip gap: correctly deferred to ViewModel layer (recipe_form_viewmodel_test.dart BUT-1057). This is an acceptable residual — the editor widget is purely presentational, chip appearance is controlled by `relatedRecipes` list from the parent, and that invariant is tested at the level that actually controls it.

### 2026-06-13 — iter salvage (BUT-1252/1253) [Review + Pattern]
Reviewed + ran two hand-finished sprint tests; all green (229 in the combined OCR+recipe run, 4 in the BUT-1252 group incl. one I added).

**BUT-1253 (`clearCacheForTesting` clears the service-owned LruMaps):** The right way to PROVE two extra LruMaps (`_cache`, `_rawHashToPreprocessedHash`) were cleared — not just the BaseService cache — is a **behavioral re-hit assertion**: extract bytes (populates both maps), `clearCacheForTesting()`, extract the SAME bytes again, then `verify(send).called(2)`. If only the BaseService cache had cleared, the raw-hash fast-path index would short-circuit to a stale result and `send` would stay at 1. Asserting `cache_size==0` alone is necessary but NOT sufficient (it only sees the result cache, not the raw-hash index). New hook: `createForTesting(registerAsInstance: true)` installs the mock-driven service as the static singleton so the static `clearCacheForTesting()` (operates on `instance`) acts on it.

**BUT-1252 (reindex gating):** The O(1) `_recipeById` index is rebuilt inside `notifyListeners()` (list-mutation path) but NOT inside `_notifyStateOnly()` (error/loading-only path via `clearError`/`_setError`). Pin the contract through the PUBLIC surface — `getRecipeById` after `optimisticRestore`/`optimisticRemove` (mutation must reindex: found / dropped) and after `clearError` (error-only must not stale the existing index: still findable). The literal web double-notify path (`_handleDirectRecipeUpdate/Removal`) is `kIsWeb`-gated → CANNOT run in a VM; the optimistic public methods exercise the same one-mutation/one-notify contract. **Added the cheap VM-testable gap the staged tests missed: a state-emission COUNT test** — `stateStream` is a BehaviorSubject, drain the replay event with `await Future.delayed(Duration.zero)`, reset counter, do ONE `optimisticRestore`, assert exactly 1 emission. Guards the inverse regression (re-adding a redundant native-path notify, the thing the cache_module fix removed for web). `Future.delayed(Duration.zero)` is the accepted drain idiom for these unawaited rx emissions (per Wave-14 entry).

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-1043 weekly-menu bulk-move multi-select [Pattern]
Reviewed the menu-ui sprint diff (`weekly_menu_plan_viewmodel.dart` + `calendar_cells.dart` + `veckomeny_view.dart` + new ARB keys). Untested new behaviour was the bulk-move/multi-select feature — added 15 VM tests + 3 widget tests, all green (52 VM, 10 widget).

**`bulkMoveSelectedTo` is the bug-prone surface, not the getters.** It folds the service's PURE `moveEntry` over the in-memory plan and saves ONCE at the end. The intent-gated assertions that catch real regressions: (a) **save fires exactly once with the FINAL folded plan** — `verify(save(afterB)).called(1)` + `verifyNever(save(afterA))` (pins the loop-then-save-once design vs a per-entry save); (b) **moved-count excludes self-moves** — stub one entry's `moveEntry` to return the SAME plan instance via `thenAnswer((inv) => inv.namedArguments[#plan] as WeeklyMenuPlan)` so the VM's `identical(next, working)` short-circuit folds it out, assert `moved==1` not 2; (c) **ghost-id skip** — an id in the selection but absent from `plan.entries` must NOT reach `service.moveEntry` (`verifyNever` on that id) and must not throw; (d) **save-failure path returns 0 + sets error + KEEPS the selection** — guards against a "1 recept flyttat" toast on an unpersisted week. The all-self-move case returns 0 and KEEPS selection (user picks a different target), distinct from a real move which clears it.

**Selection-clearing is a cross-cutting invariant worth its own tests.** `_selectedEntryIds.clear()` is called from THREE places (`loadWeek` success, `adoptPlan`, `bulkMoveSelectedTo` success). Stale selection pointing at a week that's no longer visible is the bug — pinned via "toggle on week 1 → nextWeek → isSelecting false".

**`copyWeekToNext` three-way return is the contract**: int count (success), 0 (nothing copied — "allt finns redan", distinct user message), null (failure → error snackbar). Same shape as `generateShoppingList`. The view branches on `copied == null` for the error toast, so 0-vs-null must stay distinct — pinned both.

**Widget-level selection-mode tests drive the REAL VM through `CalendarWeeklyMenuWidget`** (the existing host pattern in `calendar_weekly_menu_widget_test.dart`), calling `vm.toggleSelection(id)` to flip modes then asserting rendered behaviour: checkbox icon appears (`Icons.check_box` filled / `check_box_outline_blank` empty), and **a cell tap in selection mode toggles instead of navigating** — proven by `verifyNever(recipeService.getRecipeById(any()))` (navigation consults it; selection-toggle must not). Border-colour test captures `cs` live via a `Builder` and asserts the selected entry's accent border `contains(cs.secondary)` (only the `left` side carries the accent in `_accentedBorder`; `bottom` is always `AppColors.rustLight`) — survives a theme swap, per the no-hardcoded-theme rule. **`verifyNever` does NOT accept a `reason:` named arg** (only `expect`/`verify(...).called` do) — use a leading `//` comment instead.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-1252/1256 recipe_cache_module review [Review + Pattern]
Reviewed the cache-MODULE half of the BUT-1252 single-notify fix (the prior 2026-06-13 entry covered the recipe_INDEX VM half). Diff = a 2-branch guard (`if (_onRecipeUpdated == null) _notifyListeners();` in `_updateCachedRecipe`, mirror in `_removeCachedRecipe`) + two `@visibleForTesting` seams (`debugApplyRecipeUpdate`/`debugApplyRecipeRemoval`) that drive the exact private path FirebaseSyncManager invokes. All 26 tests green.

**The seam is the right call here, NOT kIsWeb gymnastics.** Unlike the recipe_index path which is `kIsWeb`-gated (untestable in a VM), the cache module's web/native fork is keyed on whether `onRecipeUpdated`/`onRecipeRemoved` callbacks were CONSTRUCTOR-INJECTED — so a VM test just constructs two modules (one with the callback, one without) and the `debugApply*` seams reach `_updateCachedRecipe`/`_removeCachedRecipe` directly. No platform shimming needed.

**The 4 new tests are correctly intent-gated** — each asserts notify COUNT (web=0, native=1) AND real cache state (load-after-update returns the recipe; load-after-remove returns null). Removing either guard flips the web count to 1 → web tests fail. The cache-state half rides `FakeJsonCacheHelper` (a real in-memory `extends Fake` with concrete `loadJson`/`saveJson`/`delete` — NOT mocked-away), so "callback fired" and "cache actually changed" are both proven, not just the one. This is the model for testing a web/native fork that's callback-keyed rather than platform-keyed.

**`Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero)` before asserting removal cache-state** is required because `_removeCachedRecipe` is `void` and calls `removeRecipeFromCache` fire-and-forget (unawaited) — the seam returns before the async delete lands. Accepted drain idiom (consistent with Wave-14 entry). The update path is `async`/awaited so its test needs no drain.

**Residual (not a gap from this diff): pre-existing "completes without error" tests** in the same file (`should start Firebase sync`, `should handle user login/logout`, `should dispose without error`) carry no behavioural assertion — they're smoke tests over methods that delegate to FirebaseSyncManager/CacheOptimization (tested in their own module suites). Not worth strengthening here; the delegation targets own the contract.

### 2026-06-13 — BUT-530 main.dart extraction (bootstrap area) [Pattern]

GDPR invariant: Phase A defaults all collection DISABLED, Phase B only flips on after consent confirmed — so deferring can't leak.
- **`AppInitializer.runPhaseA/runPhaseB` are architecturally untestable in isolation** — static methods over `Firebase/FirebaseCrashlytics/FirebasePerformance/FirebaseAppCheck.instance` with no injection seam (the "untestable-by-design" pattern, 2026-05-02). The phase-split ordering contract is only assertable via an e2e/integration test with full Firebase-core mock scaffolding, NOT a unit test. The consent GATE itself (`hasAnalyticsConsent`) IS already unit-tested in `consent_service_test.dart` (BUT-751), so the gate logic is covered — only the phase WIRING is uncovered. Documented as a gap, did not manufacture a smoke test.
- **The cheap NEW test surface the refactor created: `_ErrorApp` (private) → `ErrorApp` (public).** Visibility change = newly testable widget. It builds its own MaterialApp (must work before DI/theme), so pump it DIRECTLY (not `createLocalizedTestApp`). Genuinely new behaviour: `onRestart` callback — the old `_ErrorApp` had NO retry affordance. Wrote `test/widget/bootstrap/error_app_test.dart`: (1) message rendered, (2) Restart button invokes `onRestart` (load-bearing — `main()` passes its bootstrap closure), (3) error icon uses `cs.error` captured from the live theme (not hardcoded). 3 pass.
- **Doc drift caught (not a test gap): `ACCEPTED_LARGE_FILES.md` claims `butlery_app.dart` is "~850 lines"; actual is 1165** (and still over the 500 limit). The rationale entry undercounts.

### 2026-06-13 — realtime-ui review (BUT-1163 ConflictDiffView) [Pattern + Gap closed]
Sprint shipped `ConflictDiff` (pure value object, tested) + `ConflictBanner` (widget, tested) BUT left `ConflictDiffView` (259 lines, the only conflict-RECOVERY surface) with zero direct coverage. The banner test was misleading-as-coverage: it always passes `onViewChange` override or asserts the View button exists — so `ConflictDiffView.show`/`_keepMyVersion` were never rendered. **Lesson: a "View" button test that stubs navigation away does NOT cover the pushed screen — grep `test/` for the pushed widget's own type before trusting a banner/launcher test.**

Wrote `test/widget/realtime/conflict_diff_view_test.dart` (8 tests, all green). Key gotchas:
- **`updateResource<T extends RealtimeResource>` is GENERIC** → `when()`/`verify()` with bare `any()` fails `could_not_infer` (T→dynamic). Must write `service.updateResource<RealtimeResource>(any())` / `captureAny()` explicitly. `registerFallbackValue(_FakeResource(const {}))` needed for the captured-arg matcher.
- **`_FakeResource extends Fake implements RealtimeResource` overriding ONLY `toFirestore()`** lets the diff render controllable fields without the real model's Firebase ctor. `toFirestore()` is concrete (calls abstract `serializeContent()`), so overriding it bypasses the abstract member — Fake not Mock (concrete body would silently no-op a `when()`).
- **The load-bearing assertion** is that keep re-persists the LOCAL snapshot, not the remote winner: `verify(...).captured.single.toFirestore()['title'] == 'Min version'`. A regression passing `remoteValue` would keep the version the user was trying to overwrite — exactly the silent data-loss the feature exists to fix. Pinned both the captured value AND the route-pop.
- **`_localLost` getter** (`chosenStrategy == remoteWon`) gates the keep-bar — tested both branches (remoteWon shows bar, localWon hides it). Banner test only ever used `localWon`, so this branch was untested.
- Error paths (service absent → `tryGet` null; `updateResource` throws) both assert error toast present AND bar still enabled (not popped, not stuck on spinner) — a failed re-apply must never look successful.

### 2026-06-14 — realtime_sync_service updateResource cache-coherence + recoverLocalVersion review [Pattern + Verified gate]
Reviewed the two in-scope realtime files. Two distinct production changes:
1. **`updateResource` cache-coherence fix** — introduces a `final T persisted;` that is assigned the conflict WINNER (could be remote) in the resolve branch, then `_cachedResources[resource.id] = persisted` instead of the old `= resource`. New test "cache reflects the remote winner after a conflict the local lost" covers it.
2. **`recoverLocalVersion<T>` (BUT-1163)** from the prior commit — bumps a losing local snapshot to `max(local, remote).editCount + 1` then delegates to `updateResource`. Two tests (remote-beats-local 9→10; no-remote fallback 3→4).
- **VERIFIED the cache-coherence test is a real intent-gate, not a no-op**: temporarily reverted the single line (`persisted` → `resource`) and that one test failed `Expected <9> Actual <2>` ("cache held the local loser"). Restored. This is the gold-standard check for a one-line cache fix — flip the line, confirm red, restore. Always do this when the diff is "assign X instead of Y" and the test asserts on a getter reading that slot.
- **Scenario plumbing that makes the test bite**: `shouldResolveConflict` only returns true when `_lastLocalUpdate[id]` exists AND is within the 5s window AND `remote.lastEditedAt.isAfter(lastUpdate)`. The test correctly: (a) does a first `updateResource(initial)` to populate `_lastLocalUpdate` via `recordLocalUpdate`, (b) wraps everything in `withClock(Clock.fixed(...))` so the window math is deterministic, (c) seeds a collaborator with `lastEditedAt` strictly AFTER the fixed clock instant. Drop any of the three and `shouldResolveConflict` returns false → the resolve branch (the changed code) never runs → test goes green-for-the-wrong-reason. Pattern: **for a conflict-resolution test, assert the resolve branch was actually entered (here: disk editCount == 9 proves remote won), not just the final cache value.** The test does both.
- **Thin-coverage residual on `recoverLocalVersion` (Medium, not blocking)**: both recovery tests pin only `editCount`. The doc comment says the fix's purpose is that the recovered version "legitimately wins SUBSEQUENT resolveConflict comparisons" — which depends on `lastEditedAt: clock.now()` (fresh) and `lastEditedBy: _currentUserId ?? local.lastEditedBy`. Neither is asserted. A regression dropping the timestamp bump (keeping stale `lastEditedAt`) would pass both existing tests but reanimate the silent-discard bug on the NEXT edit when editCounts tie. Suggested add: a third recovery test that, after `recoverLocalVersion`, drives one more concurrent edit at equal editCount and asserts the recovered version wins on timestamp — OR minimally assert the persisted doc's `lastEditedBy == currentUserId` and `lastEditedAt == clock.now()` under a fixed clock. Not a High because the editCount bump alone defends the common (unequal-count) case.
- **Untested branch**: `recoverLocalVersion` when remote EXISTS but `remote.editCount <= local.editCount` (the `if (remote.editCount > baseEditCount)` is false) → uses local's own count. Both current tests have remote>local or no remote. Low — same `+1` arithmetic, low regression surface.
- No weakened assertions, no mocked-away subject: the suite uses a real `FirestoreRepository` over a LOCAL `FakeFirebaseFirestore` and a local `_MockAuthRepository extends Mock` (correctly NOT the shared concrete-override mock — matches the 2026-05-24 entry). updateResource/deleteResource/recover all hit the real conflict+parser modules end-to-end.

### 2026-06-14 — RealtimeSync conflict module: prior flags closed, two residual gaps [Pattern]
Re-reviewed `realtime_sync_service.dart` + `conflict_resolution_module.dart` and their two test files. **The two recovery gaps flagged on the cache-coherence entry above are now CLOSED**: BUT-1263 test (line ~495) pins `lastEditedBy == currentUserId` + `lastEditedAt == clock.now()` under a fixed clock; BUT-1264 test (line ~549) covers the `remote.editCount <= local.editCount` false-branch. BUT-1266 added the three `shouldResolveConflict` false-branches (null lastUpdate / window boundary / stale remote) WITH a `remoteFetched` recording-flag that proves each branch short-circuits BEFORE the Firestore read — the right way to test a guard that exists to AVOID needless reads. The 5000ms boundary test pins the strict-`<` off-by-one by asserting 5000ms is outside AND 4999ms is inside (fetch fired). BUT-1267 correctly DELETED the four service-level `resolveConflict` last-write-wins tests (the method was dead — `updateResource` routes through `_conflictModule.resolveConflict`); the algorithm is re-pinned at module level WITH the ConflictEvent emission the dead service copy omitted. All 34 tests pass.

No test forces the try body to throw, so this intentional "fallback-to-remote-WITHOUT-emit" contract is unpinned. Hard to reach cleanly (would need a resource whose getters throw); document rather than manufacture. A throwing `onConflict` sink is inside the try, so it'd be swallowed and silently flip to remote — but the service wires a non-throwing controller-add, so not a live risk.
**Residual gap 2 (Low):** the service constructs `ConflictResolutionModule` WITHOUT passing `collectionPath` (defaults to `''`). So nothing pins what the LIVE service emits for that field (empty). If a future `ConflictBanner` dedups/routes on `collectionPath`, the empty default is a latent surprise. Cheap add: assert `events.single.collectionPath` in the BUT-1265 end-to-end test.
No weakened assertions, no mocked-away subject. Module tests use a `_FakeFirestoreRepository extends Mock` but the SUT (resolver/guard logic) is real and never stubbed away.

### 2026-06-14 — BUT-947 multi-URL import: SSRF guard + all-failed-recovery gaps [Gap closed]
Sprint added list-aware URL import to `UrlImportViewModel` (paste several URLs → per-URL `UrlImportResult` rows, partial-success tolerated, per-URL retry). The staged tests were genuinely behavioral (parseUrls split/dedup, per-URL success, partial failure no-banner, all-failed banner, retry-one-row, edit-clears-batch) — NOT structural, good batch. Three real gaps remained, all in branches the LLM draft skipped:
- **`parseUrls` private/reserved-host filtering is an SSRF invariant, not a formatting detail.** The single-URL path rejects `localhost`/`127.*`/`192.168.*`/`10.*`/`169.254.*` with `errorUrlPrivateAddress`; the batch path's `_isFetchableUrl` silently DROPS them. No test pinned this — refactor `_isFetchableUrl` to drop the `_isPrivateOrReservedHost` check and the batch becomes an SSRF vector with zero red tests. Added a one-shot test mixing each private host with two public siblings (proves drop isn't over-broad). **Rule: when a security check exists on path A and is re-implemented on path B (here single-URL vs batch), the path-B test is mandatory — same-invariant-two-codepaths is exactly where guards silently diverge (cf. BUT-748 field-name canonicalisation, BUT-1069 dead pagination filters).**
- **all-failed → retry-recovers → `clearError()` branch was uncovered.** The existing retry test started from a PARTIAL batch (row already succeeded) so `error` was never `importUrlBatchAllFailed` → the clear-branch (VM lines 236-238) never ran. The comment literally says "A retry can clear an all-failed batch error" and nothing proved it. Added a test that fails ALL urls first (banner set), then retries one to green and asserts `hasError` flips to false. **Lesson: a retry test that starts from partial success does NOT cover the error-clearing transition — the precondition (error was set) has to be established first.**
- **`retryUrl` out-of-range guard** (index<0 / >=length) was unexercised; cheap no-crash + batch-untouched test added.
- File went 60→64 tests, all green, `dart analyze` clean. The view (`import_via_url_view.dart`, new `_UrlBatchResults`/`_UrlResultRow` private widgets using `AppColors.success/error` + `importUrlBatchProgress(success,total)` plural) correctly needs NO mechanical view test per BUT-387 Phase 6 — the branch logic lives in the VM where `_fetchPage` delegates on `isMultiUrl`. ARB keys (`importUrlBatchAllFailed`, `importMultipleUrlsLabel/Hint`, `importUrlBatchProgress`) verified present in BOTH sv+en with matching `{success}/{total}` int placeholders; no test lane for ARB additions.

### 2026-06-14 — BUT-1205 re-extract-from-source: zero coverage on new view behavior [Gap closed]
`recipe_detail_view.dart` gained a "re-extract from source" flow (private `_reextractFromSource` + `_StaleSourceBanner` widget) with NO test of any kind — the journey test `test/views/recipe_detail_view_test.dart` exercised the real shell but never touched the source sheet. Added a 5-test group through the real shell. Key learnings:
- **An import strategy constructed inline inside a view method is testable IFF the strategy is hermetic.** `_reextractFromSource` hard-wires `UrlImportStrategy()` / `TextImportStrategy()` (no injection seam). `TextImportStrategy.import` is fully hermetic (no DI, no network, pure text parse — confirmed via a throwaway probe), so a `textPaste`-type artefact drives the whole flow end-to-end; the persistence layer is already mocked via the locator's `UnifiedRecipeService.updateRecipe`. URL-type would hit the network and is NOT drivable here. **Probe a parser's actual output with a scratch test before asserting on it** — this parser extracted title="Recept: Morotssoppa", 4 ings, 6 instrs from a 4-ing/4-step payload, so I pinned the robust invariants (id/createdAt preserved, ingredients replaced & non-empty, original artefact re-passed) instead of literal parse output.
- **Owner popup-menu Rows overflow at the 420px test width** (addToMenu / generateShoppingList items exceed PopupMenu's intrinsic ~256px) — a pre-existing layout quirk unrelated to the subject that throws RenderFlex-overflow FlutterErrors and aborts ANY menu-driven test in this file (which is why no prior test opened the menu). Fix that doesn't mask real bugs: temporarily swap `FlutterError.onError` to swallow ONLY `'A RenderFlex overflowed'` while the menu is open, restore in `finally`. Don't widen the viewport — the menu's overflow is overlay-internal, not screen-driven, and ≥600px flips to the two-column path.
- **Stale-banner boundary needs BOTH sides pinned** (31 days → banner, 29 days → no banner) under `withClock(Clock.fixed(...))` so a flipped `>`/`<` can't pass — production reads `clock.now()` so `withClock` controls it without fakeAsync.
- **The transient error snackbar is queued behind the in-progress info toast** (`SnackBarUtils.showInfo` shows first, `showError` enqueues via ScaffoldMessenger) → asserting on the error text is timing-fragile. Pin the DURABLE state instead: on a failed `updateRecipe` (returns false) the detail view still shows the original title (lowercased per design rule), NOT the extracted one — proves no optimistic mutation, robust to toast ordering.
- **copyWith identity invariant (cf. BUT-369):** `Recipe.copyWith` / `RecipeCore.copyWith` have NO `id`/`createdAt` params, so re-extract keeps both; captured the recipe passed to `updateRecipe` via `captureAny()` (+ `registerFallbackValue(RecipeFactory.build(...))` in setUpAll) and asserted id + createdAt + re-passed `sourceArtefact.payload`. Cancel path uses `verifyNever(updateRecipe)`.
- All 5 new + 10 pre-existing pass; `dart analyze` clean. ARB keys (`recipeSourceReextract*`, `recipeSourceStaleBanner`) present in sv+en; no ARB test lane.

### 2026-06-14 — import area BUT-1040 multi-recipe paste + BUT-931 AI-provenance [Bug found + Gap closed]
Sprint rewired `TextImportViewModel.parseText` from `importManager.autoImport` to `autoParseMulti` (cookbook paste → N recipes via the shared `BatchImportPreview` picker) and added an AI-provenance chip to assisted import. Three findings:
- **Test-suite REGRESSION (High):** the existing `text_import_viewmodel_test.dart` stubbed `autoImport`/`mockTextStrategy.import`, but prod now calls `autoParseMulti` (NO default stub on `MockImportManager`) → 4 single-recipe tests went red (parseText returned false because mocktail's null default → `parsed.isEmpty`). The view change shipped without updating the test. Fixed by adding a default single-recipe `BatchImportResult` stub (helper `_singleResult`) + repointing the parse-error fault-injection to `autoParseMulti`. **Lesson: when a VM method is rewired to a new collaborator method, grep the VM's test for the OLD method name — a no-default-stub Mock fails silently to mocktail's null, not loudly.**
- **Production bug (Medium, pinned not weakened):** the new `parseText` calls `autoParseMulti` directly WITHOUT `setLoading(true)`/`executeAsync`, so `isParsing` (== isLoading) never flips. `fran_sociala_medier_view.dart:335/338` binds the parse button's spinner + disabled-state to `isParsing` → the "Tolkar..." spinner won't show and the button won't disable during the Cloud-Function parse (double-tap risk). Used the **documented-residual tripwire**: renamed the test `BUG(BUT-1040):...`, pinned the CURRENT (regressed) `wasParsing == false` with a "flip to isTrue when fixed" reason. Keeps CI green AND loud. `importAndSave` is UNAFFECTED — it still routes `completeImport → performImport → parseTextToRecipe → strategy.import` (executeAsync), so two divergent parse paths now coexist.

The VM test stubs `getWeek` to return the same plan and asserts `moved==2` + mode-exit, but does NOT `verify` a SECOND `getWeek` after the move — a regression that dropped the re-fetch (leaving the UI showing the pre-move layout) would still pass. ### 2026-06-14 — onboarding-seed (BUT-930 sample-menu seeding): single-slot day-skip + null-id filter were untested
Trigger: coverage review of `lib/viewmodels/onboarding_viewmodel.dart` + `analytics_events.dart` working-tree diff (BUT-930 added `_seedSampleMenu`, changed `_seedStarterRecipes` to return `List<String>` of persisted ids).

The existing BUT-930 test pair (happy-path "saves a non-empty plan" + "idempotent: don't overwrite") was real but THIN — it asserted only `entries.isNotEmpty`, which masks the two invariants BUT-930 actually exists to guarantee:
- **Single-slot day-skip.** `_seedSampleMenu` walks Mon→Sun and, for non-multi slots (`MealSlot.lunch`/`middag`, `isMulti==false`), advances `dayIndex` past days already occupied so two `middag` recipes don't overwrite each other on Monday. `entries.isNotEmpty` passes even if all recipes collapsed onto one day. Pin it with: place N middag recipes → assert `middagEntries.map((e)=>e.day).toSet().length == middagEntries.length` (each on a distinct day). Don't hardcode the count — `RecipeSeeds.allRecipes` drives it (currently 6, all routed via the catch-all `getRecipeById(any())` stub → assert the *relationship*, not a literal). I initially asserted `==2` and it failed `Actual: <6>` because the seed list has 6 entries, not 2 — good reminder that `RecipeSeeds` length is the real driver.
- **null-id exclusion.** `createPersonalRecipe` returns `String?`; `_seedStarterRecipes` only adds `if (id != null)`. A recipe that fails to persist (null id) must never reach the menu. Stub first-call→`'ok'`, rest→`null`, assert `savedPlan.entries.map((e)=>e.recipeId) == ['ok']`.

Also added: empty-seed early-return (`seededRecipeIds.isEmpty` → `verifyNever(getWeek)` + no `onboarding_menu_seeded` event) and best-effort save-failure tolerance (`save` throws → `completeOnboarding()` still returns `true`, and `generateForWeek` is `verifyNever`'d because it runs only after a successful save). 18/18 green, analyze clean.

Helper note: the BUT-930 group's "real adder" pattern (stub `mockMenuService.addEntry` to delegate to a live `WeeklyMenuPlanService(repository: _NoopMenuRepo(), userService: mock)`) is the right call for placement-invariant tests — `addEntry` is pure/in-memory and never touches the repo, so you get production slot/day semantics without a fake Firestore. I extracted it into a local `wireRealAdderOnEmptyWeek()` closure inside the group to avoid copy-paste across the 3 new placement tests. `_NoopMenuRepo` already exists at the bottom of the file.

**What the diff did:** `deleteUserRootDoc` was moved verbatim from `FirebaseUserRepository` into `UserRootDeletionMixin on BaseFirebaseRepository<UserProfile>`; repo now `with UserRootDeletionMixin`. No logic change — same `validateOwnership` + explicit `firestore.collection(users).doc(uid).delete()`.
**Coverage check:** The load-bearing invariant (delete `users/{uid}` while leaving `public_profiles/{uid}` untouched — the whole reason the bypass-mixin exists) IS asserted in `test/unit/repositories/firebase_user_repository_test.dart` "deleteUserRootDoc targets the users/ collection (not public_profiles)" (seeds BOTH collections, asserts one gone + one survives). Non-owner rejection covered in both that file and `firebase/firebase_user_repository_gaps_test.dart`. All 7 GDPR-cascade tests green after the refactor. A mixin-extraction refactor like this needs NO new test if the cross-collection isolation test already pins the behavior — don't add structural "is the mixin mixed in" asserts.
**Weak-assertion pattern spotted (pre-existing, not this diff):** `gaps_test.dart` line ~414 `expect(() => repo.deletePublicProfile(_alice), throwsA(anything))` — async callback passed to bare `expect` (not `await expectLater`) can pass spuriously and asserts nothing about the doc surviving. The sibling file does it right with `await expectLater(...)` + a survival assertion. When you see `expect(() => asyncFn(), throwsA(...))` without await, flag it Medium and point at the correct sibling pattern.

### 2026-06-14 — BUT-1242 cooking step-timer: notifications + multi-timer + active-strip [Pattern + Gap]

**Diff:** StepTimerService generalised single→multi-timer (id-keyed map, `startTimer/pauseTimer/resumeTimer/resetTimer`, `timers` snapshot stream), new `LocalTimerNotificationService` (OS local-notification per running timer for backgrounded expiry), `_ActiveTimersStrip`/`_TimerChip` in cooking_mode_view, `StepTimerWidget` gains a `timerId` param, content_module wires notifications into StepTimerService.

**Pre-existing test state was good for the SERVICE but thin elsewhere.** `step_timer_service_test.dart` already covered multi-timer countdown, snapshot sorting, resetTimer-removal, and notification schedule/pause/reset/resume via a `_RecordingNotifications extends LocalTimerNotificationService` recorder (concrete `@override` bodies). Note: that recorder extends the REAL service (not Mock), so `when()` isn't in play — it's a Fake-style recorder. Acceptable, but it pulls in the real `FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin()` via the inherited ctor; `extends Fake` + a stored plugin-less stub would be cleaner. Logged as Info, not a blocker.

**Two real gaps this diff shipped with no coverage:**
1. `LocalTimerNotificationService` (158 new lines) was entirely stubbed away by the recorder — its OWN logic had zero tests. On a VM `Platform.isAndroid/isIOS` are false so `schedule`/`cancel` take the non-mobile early-return: the meaningful pure-Dart surface is `notificationIdFor` (determinism / non-negative `& 0x7fffffff` mask / no-collision across step-0..9 — this is the contract that makes `cancel` target the right pending alert) plus the off-device safe-no-op guards (`isPrepared` stays false, no throw). Wrote `test/unit/services/notifications/local_timer_notification_service_test.dart` for exactly that. The on-device branch (zonedSchedule, label-vs-no-label body, channel create) is NOT VM-testable — would need a device/integration leg; left uncovered with a note in the test header.
2. `StepTimerWidget`'s new `timerId` (the core BUT-1242 wiring) — the existing widget test only drove the default slot. Added a two-widgets-one-service test proving independent step-0/step-1 countdowns render distinctly AND pausing one leaves the other running (`find.byTooltip('Pausa').last`). Reset both named timers + the default slot + swap to empty MaterialApp before dispose to clear the forever pulse + periodic tickers (same teardown idiom as the expired test).

**Still-open gap (flagged, not filled): `_ActiveTimersStrip`/`_TimerChip`** are private widgets inside cooking_mode_view with no test — renders-nothing-when-empty, filters idle/expired, `step-N`→index reverse-parse on tap, Swedish `cookingActiveTimersTitle` + `a11yActiveTimer(label,time)` semantics. Per the BUT-387 mapping a view is journey-tested, not view-tested; this strip's behaviour belongs in a cooking-mode journey test. Logged Medium.

**l10n:** 7 new keys × sv+en (timerNotification* ×5, cookingActiveTimersTitle, a11yActiveTimer) all present in both ARBs with matching placeholders — no missing-translation gap. `AppLocale.current` defaults to Swedish with no setup, so notification-body string selection is unit-testable without bootstrap.

### 2026-06-14 — toFirestoreEditable() merge-write subset: the hiddenAt clobber path had no guard
**Trigger:** Reviewing commit 22ab49ae9 (BUT-734 user-repo split + BUT-1285 merge-write). New `UserProfile.toFirestoreEditable()` removes three server-owned keys (`friendsCount`, `isHidden`, `hiddenAt`) so a stale owner save can't clobber them via `set(merge:true)`.
- **Gap caught:** The repository regression test (`saveProfile preserves server-owned ...`) seeded and asserted only `friendsCount` and `isHidden`. The third removed key, `hiddenAt`, had ZERO assertion anywhere — and `toFirestoreEditable()` had no direct model-level test at all. Dropping `data.remove('hiddenAt')` would silently merge a stale `null` over a live moderation timestamp and no test would fail. Mutation-verified: commenting out that one line leaves both old assertions green.
- **Fix applied:** (1) Added a model-level test in `user_profile_test.dart` Firestore-Serialization group asserting all three keys are absent AND every owner-editable key survives (not a blanket strip). (2) Extended the repo regression to seed `hiddenAt` (opaque sentinel string — don't couple to `AppTimestamp`'s serialized shape) and assert it round-trips byte-for-byte. Both fail under the dropped-remove mutation.
- **Pattern:** When a "subset" method removes N fields, the test must pin ALL N removals, not a sample — each `.remove()` is an independent regression surface. Pin the contract at the model level (cheap, no fake) AND keep the repo-level merge-semantics guard.
- **FakeFirebaseFirestore note (registry):** `set(merge:true)` on `public_profiles` DOES persist+merge correctly under the fake — the saveProfile merge test proves it. The 8 skipped tests in this file are merge writes to the `users/{uid}/settings` SUB-collection, which the fake+TestServiceLocator combo drops; those settings round-trips are only provable on the emulator lane. Don't assume "merge doesn't work in the fake" — it's sub-doc-path-specific.

### 2026-06-14 — Calendar weekly-menu (BUT-1280/1043): widget wiring of a VM action was untested though the VM action was unit-tested
**Trigger:** Reviewing `test/widget/menu/calendar_weekly_menu_widget_test.dart` after commit `1711d297c` (bulk-move + copy-week).
- **Verdict:** Strong file — drives a real `WeeklyMenuPlanViewModel` (subject not mocked), resolves locale/theme via a local `_host(...)` wrapper, and the bulk-move flow (BUT-1280) covers success/error/dismiss branches with `verify`/`verifyNever`. All 14 original tests pass.
- **Gap caught (High → fixed):** The copy-week affordance's WIDGET wiring (`_onCopyWeek`: confirm `AlertDialog` → 3-way result snackbar) had zero coverage. The VM's `copyWeekToNext()` is unit-tested in isolation and the widget test asserted only the copy icon's PRESENCE. The tap → confirm → branch-to-snackbar path — where a wrong l10n key or a missing plural branch hides — was untested.
- **Fix applied:** Added a `copy-week affordance` group (4 tests): tap-copy → confirm → `service.copyWeek(from, from+7d)` called + numbered plural snackbar; `count==0` shows the distinct "Inget kopierades – allt finns redan nästa vecka" branch; `thenThrow` shows the error snackbar with no success copy; cancel fires `verifyNever(copyWeek)`. File now 18 tests, all green.
- **Rule (generalizable):** A VM unit test does NOT cover the widget's dialog-gate + result-branching wiring. When a widget gates a VM action behind a confirm dialog and branches the result into different user-visible copy, add widget tests that (1) drive the dialog confirm AND cancel, (2) stub the service to return each plural-selecting count (0 vs N), (3) `thenThrow` for failure, asserting each branch by its DISTINCT l10n string. ICU `{count, plural, =0{...} =1{...} other{...}}` branches are separate code paths — pin `=0` separately from the numbered one.
- **Helper note:** `_host(...)` here wraps a real VM in `ChangeNotifierProvider` + `MaterialApp` (sv, full delegates, `AppTheme.lightTheme`) — deliberately NOT `createLocalizedTestApp` because the widget needs the provider in scope. Reuse `_host` for further tests on this widget.

**Trigger:** re-review of `test/widget/import/import_via_url_view_multi_test.dart` working-tree delta vs HEAD (commit 74825b1f2). The delta adds ONE test + makes the fake VM's `isMultiUrl`/`hasExtractedText` parameter-driven instead of hardcoded. All 9 tests pass.

- **Good (additive, no weakening):** making `_FakeUrlImportViewModel.isMultiUrl`/`hasExtractedText` constructor-driven is a strengthening — the fake previously lied (`isMultiUrl => true` always); now it can honestly model the legacy branch. The new test pins that a single-URL VM (`isMultiUrl:false`, empty `urlResults`, non-empty `extractedText`) renders the legacy editor (`Extraherad text:` + `Gå vidare till klistra-in`) AND the batch surface is wholly absent. Correct: the view branches batch on `urlResults.isNotEmpty`, editor on `urlResults.isEmpty && hasExtractedText` (import_via_url_view.dart:245/263).
