The author correctly asserted the two header/CTA strings (which only render on the legacy branch) instead. Good restraint.
- **Coverage gap (Medium, lives in VM unit file not the reviewed file):** `successfulBatchText` — the getter that joins ONLY successful rows' `extractedText` with `\n\n` (the separator the multi-recipe splitter treats as a recipe boundary) — has NO unit test in `test/unit/viewmodels/url_import_viewmodel_test.dart` (verified: file ends at line 1213, last test is "editing the URL clears prior batch results"). The widget test feeds a pre-baked `batchText` and only proves the view forwards it verbatim. A regression that joined failures too, or used the wrong separator, would pass BOTH files green while silently producing wrong/merged recipes downstream. This is the load-bearing batch→N-recipes piece. Recommend a VM unit test: batch with [success, failure, success] → `successfulBatchText` equals `text1\n\ntext3` (failure excluded, blank-line separated).
- **Low/Info:** the legacy test's negative assertion `find.textContaining('Importera')` would also catch the fetch button if its label contained "Importera" — safe in practice because `importFetchText` = "Hämta text", but `find.text('Importera 2 recept')`-style exactness would be marginally more robust.

### 2026-06-14 — dart-test-gaps review: duplicated SSRF guard in UrlImportViewModel only half-tested
Trigger: Coverage review of `url_import_viewmodel_test.dart`, `menu_shopping_list_generator_test.dart`, `shared_recipes_by_friend_view_test.dart` (all three current, all green: 68 / 13 / 6).

Key finding — there are TWO SSRF host guards in the import stack and they diverge in coverage:
- `HttpContentFetcher.isBlockedHost` (real network gate) — fully pinned in `test/unit/services/import/ssrf_protection_test.dart` + `url_import_strategy_test.dart` (10/8, 172.16-31/12, 192.168/16, 169.254/16, 0.0.0.0, IPv6 ::1 / fc00 / fe80, plus the public siblings 172.15/172.32).
- `UrlImportViewModel._isPrivateOrReservedHost` (a SECOND copy, used by `parseUrls` batch + `getUrlValidationErrors` single-URL UI validation) — the batch test (`parseUrls drops private/reserved hosts`) only asserts localhost / 127.0.0.1 / 192.168 / 10.x / 169.254. It SKIPS the `172.16-31`, `0.0.0.0`, `[::1]`, and IPv6 fc00/fe80 branches the production code has — and the comment in that test even lists "172.16/12" while the assertion omits it. The single-URL `getUrlValidationErrors → errorUrlPrivateAddress` path has ZERO coverage anywhere.
- Severity is Medium not High: the real fetcher still blocks, so this VM copy is defense-in-depth + UX (don't render a doomed private-host row). A regression wouldn't open SSRF, only degrade the batch/validation UX. The deeper smell is the DUPLICATE guard — worth a note that the VM should ideally delegate to `HttpContentFetcher.isBlockedHost` so there's one tested implementation.

Pattern reaffirmed (good, not a gap): the `shared_recipes_by_friend_view` widget test's `_FakeRecipeViewModel` overrides exactly the four surfaces the view's `_buildBody` branches on (`isLoading`/`hasError`/`error`/`recipesSharedBy`) — it controls inputs, not the subject. The retry test (BUT-1296) reads a real dispatch counter on a singleton-registered fake coordinator rather than asserting button presence — the correct "prove the callback fires" shape. Note its loading branch is `isGloballyLoading || recipeViewModel.isLoading` and only the second half is exercised (both render the same spinner, so splitting is low-value).

Generator test (`menu_shopping_list_generator_test.dart`): the `alreadyRunning` re-entrancy sentinel defined in `MenuShoppingGenerationResult` is NOT this file's concern — the guard lives in `WeeklyMenuPlanViewModel.generateShoppingList` (`if (isLoading) return alreadyRunning`) and is covered in `weekly_menu_plan_viewmodel_test.dart`. Correct separation; don't flag it as a generator gap.

Windows test-run note (reinforces the existing PATH gotcha): `flutter test` works when invoked as the absolute `"C:/tools/flutter/bin/flutter.bat"` directly through the Bash tool. The `cmd.exe /c "...batch..."` approach printed only the shell banner (UTF-16, no execution) — the sandbox launches an interactive cmd that ignores the trailing command. Use the direct flutter.bat path.

### 2026-06-14 — Injectable factory seams leave the production default untested (recipe_detail_viewmodel BUT-1300)
- **Trigger:** reviewing `recipe_detail_viewmodel_reextract_test.dart` against the BUT-1300 seam diff.
- **Pattern:** When a unit replaces inline construction with an injectable factory seam (here `ReextractStrategyFactory`, defaulted to a private `_defaultReextractStrategyFactory` that maps `SourceArtefactType.url → UrlImportStrategy`, else `TextImportStrategy`), the seam tests inject a FAKE factory and therefore NEVER exercise the production default. A regression flipping the url-vs-text condition ships green. The LLM-drafted suite covered strategy selection, identity preservation, parsed-field overwrite, and all three failure branches through the fake — but the one new production line the seam wrapped had zero coverage.
- **Fix applied:** exposed the default via `@visibleForTesting ImportStrategy defaultReextractStrategyForTest(type)` (foundation already exports the annotation — no `package:meta` import needed) and added two `isA<UrlImportStrategy>()` / `isA<TextImportStrategy>()` assertions (url + every non-url enum value). Safe because both strategy constructors are side-effect-free — network only happens in `import()`, so asserting the dispatched runtime type needs no mocking.
- **Rule:** for any "inject a factory/selector and default it to the old inline logic" seam, add ONE test that asserts the DEFAULT mapping directly, separate from the seam-injection tests. The seam tests prove "the VM calls the factory with the right arg"; only a default-factory test proves "the production wiring picks the right implementation."
- Both strategy constructors (`UrlImportStrategy()`, `TextImportStrategy()`) are cheap/inert on construction in this codebase — registry/parser/fetcher wiring is lazy. Confirmed by reading their constructors; relevant if you ever need to instantiate them in a test without the emulator.

### 2026-06-14 — Widget-extraction (BUT-1258) exposes testable behaviour that was previously private (veckomeny_selection_widgets)
- **Trigger:** coverage review of the BUT-1258 structural split — `VeckomenyViewModeToggle`, `VeckomenyCookingSessionCard`, `VeckomenyGeneratingOverlay` moved verbatim from private `_build*` methods on `_VeckomenyViewContentState` to public widget classes in `lib/widgets/menu/veckomeny_selection_widgets.dart`. Pure move, no behaviour change, but it changes testability.
- **Pattern:** "Pure structural split, no behaviour change" diffs aren't no-ops for the tester. Behaviour that was locked inside a view's private methods (untestable without booting the whole view + ServiceLocator) becomes a clean public `in → out` contract once extracted. That's exactly when to add the focused widget test the view never allowed. The toggle's contract: `mode` in, `onSelect(VeckomenyViewMode)` out, plus active-segment `Semantics(selected:)` for screen readers. Zero existing tests referenced any of the three widgets or the BUT-1241 `MenuPlacementChoiceFooter`.
- **Fix applied:** `test/widget/widgets/menu/veckomeny_view_mode_toggle_test.dart` — 4 tests: tap-fires-onSelect for each segment (assert the ENUM value, find segments by their lowercased localized text `'lista'`/`'kalender'`), active/inactive `Semantics(selected:)` split, and active-segment background captured from the live `cs.surface`.
- **`matchesSemantics` is EXHAUSTIVE — use `containsSemantics` for a subset.** `matchesSemantics(isSelected: true, isButton: true)` failed: a merged InkWell+Text node also carries `isFocusable`, `hasSelectedState`, and tap/focus actions, all of which the exhaustive matcher rejects. `containsSemantics(isSelected: true, isButton: true)` checks only the listed properties and ignores incidental ones. Reach for `containsSemantics` whenever asserting a flag on a real (merged) node rather than a hand-built SemanticsNode.
- **`SemanticsNode.hasFlag(SemanticsFlag.x)` is deprecated** (after Flutter 3.32) and `SemanticsFlag` isn't in scope from `flutter/material.dart` anyway. Don't reach for `flutter/semantics.dart` + `hasFlag` — use the `containsSemantics`/`matchesSemantics` matchers from `flutter_test`, no extra import.
- **Not worth a widget test (this diff):** `VeckomenyGeneratingOverlay` is a pure l10n pass-through to `PeaLoadingOverlay` (no logic — testing it asserts the two l10n keys exist, which the analyzer already guarantees). `VeckomenyCookingSessionCard` has real branch logic (module-null / no-groups / no-stream → `SizedBox.shrink`, else `StreamBuilder`) but needs `CookingSessionModule` + `UnifiedFriendsService` from the ServiceLocator and a fake presence stream — a heavier scaffold that belongs in a follow-up, flagged Medium below.

### 2026-06-14 — url_import_viewmodel re-review: SSRF gap CLOSED, batch CTA/clear getters were untested
- **Trigger:** scoped coverage review of `lib/viewmodels/url_import_viewmodel.dart` + `test/unit/viewmodels/url_import_viewmodel_test.dart` (both committed-clean, working tree clean). Supersedes the earlier same-day entry "duplicated SSRF guard … only half-tested" — that gap is now resolved.
- **SSRF gap is CLOSED (was the prior entry's main finding):** `_isPrivateOrReservedHost` no longer carries a string-prefix copy — it delegates to the single tested `HttpContentFetcher.isBlockedHost` (BUT-1303), and the test now has a dedicated `SSRF host guard — getUrlValidationErrors (BUT-1303)` group exercising every block branch (full 127/8, 0.0.0.0, 10/8, 172.16-31/12 incl. the 172.31 upper bound + 172.32 negative sibling, 192.168/16, 169.254/16, IPv6 ::1/fc00/fe80, IPv4-mapped ::ffff, plus public siblings). The single-URL `errorUrlPrivateAddress` path the prior entry called "ZERO coverage anywhere" is now fully pinned. One guard, one test surface — the duplicate-implementation smell is gone.
- **`successfulBatchText` gap (flagged 2026-06-14 import-url cont. entry) is also CLOSED** — group `successfulBatchText (BUT-1295)` covers [success, failure, success] join, lone-success no-separator, all-failure empty string.
- **Gaps caught this pass (fixed, Medium):** three new batch surfaces had ZERO coverage —
  1. `successfulUrlCount` (drives the "Importera N recept" CTA count): added a 2-of-3 test (failure excluded) + an all-failed→0 test. A regression counting total rows or off-by-one would offer to import more recipes than fetched and dead-end the paste step.
  2. `clearUrlResults()` (the explicit "discard batch" affordance): added empties-and-notifies + already-empty-is-silent-no-op. The `if (_urlResults.isEmpty) return` guard was untested — an inverted guard would either fail to clear or fire a needless rebuild.
  3. `UrlImportResult.isLoading` (per-row spinner state): added a mid-flight observer subclass (`_LoadingObservingUrlImportViewModel`, fires `onMidFetch` once during the first per-URL fetch before it resolves) asserting a row reports `isLoading` while seeded loading, and nothing is loading after settle.
- **Helper added & reusable:** `_LoadingObservingUrlImportViewModel extends UrlImportViewModel` — overrides only the leaf `fetchContentFromUrl` to run a callback the first time it's entered, exposing the in-flight `urlResults` snapshot. Same Fake-by-subclass pattern as the existing `ConcurrencyTrackingUrlImportViewModel` / `TestableUrlImportViewModel` in this file (legit: extends the REAL VM, passed as a value — not a Mock-with-bodies). Use it for any "observe VM state mid-async-loop" need.
- **Verdict:** High = none (no untested NEW production behaviour remains; the prior High-ish SSRF gap is closed). Medium = the three above, now fixed. File grew 68 → 87 tests, all green via the direct `C:/tools/flutter/bin/flutter.bat test ...` invocation (the cmd.exe-banner workaround is unnecessary — flutter.bat runs directly through the Bash tool). `dart analyze` clean.
- **Note for future passes on this file:** `fetchContentFromUrl` in production wraps the headless `WebScraper` (undriveable in unit tests) and is overridden in every test subclass — so the WebScraper/PlatformDetector integration (mobile→web URL conversion, platform-specific selectors, dispose-in-finally) is NOT unit-covered here by design. That seam belongs to a WebScraper-level test, not this VM file.

### 2026-06-14 — veckomeny_selection_widgets coverage sweep (Pattern discovered)

Trigger: test-coverage review of `lib/widgets/menu/veckomeny_selection_widgets.dart` (BUT-1258 structural extraction of 3 presentational widgets out of `veckomeny_view`).

- **Two of three widgets were already well-covered** and exemplary: `VeckomenyViewModeToggle` (`test/widget/widgets/menu/veckomeny_view_mode_toggle_test.dart` — onSelect fires per segment, Semantics selected/button via `containsSemantics`, active bg captured from live `cs.surface`) and `VeckomenyCookingSessionCard` (`..._cooking_session_card_test.dart` — all 3 hide-branches + populated StreamBuilder path, via Fake friends/module bridged into the production ServiceLocator). Both green, theme-resolved, behaviour-focused. Nothing to add.
- **Gap found (Medium):** the third widget `VeckomenyGeneratingOverlay` had ZERO coverage. It's a thin wrapper whose only owned behaviour is wiring `context.l10n.menuGeneratingOverlay` + `menuGeneratingSubtitle` into a `PeaLoadingOverlay`. A wrong-key regression would show the user the wrong text mid-generation with no test failing. Added `veckomeny_generating_overlay_test.dart`: captures the two strings via `context.l10n` in a `Builder`, asserts both render + `PeaLoadingOverlay` is present. Resolves through l10n (not hardcoded Swedish) so a copy tweak won't break it — only a wrong-key swap will.
- **Pattern for "thin l10n-wiring wrappers":** don't skip them as "trivial"; the wrapper OWNS which l10n key reaches the user. Capture the expected string via `context.l10n` in a Builder (not a literal) → `find.text(captured)`. One meaningful test, survives copy edits, catches key swaps.
- **Windows test invocation confirmed (again):** `/c/tools/flutter/bin/flutter test <path>` runs directly through the Bash tool — no cmd.exe banner workaround needed. `dart analyze <path>` likewise via `/c/tools/flutter/bin/dart`.
- **Verdict:** High = none (it was a pure structural split, no new logic; existing tests preserve the extracted contracts). Medium = the one overlay gap, now closed. No weakened assertions or mocked-away subjects found in the existing two files.

### 2026-06-14 — Revert-to-start test can't distinguish "reverted" from "never changed" (recipe_detail_viewmodel BUT-1304)
- **Trigger:** coverage review of BUT-1304 `updateRecipeTagOverrides` (View no longer hits `UnifiedRecipeService` directly; VM owns optimistic-update + persist + revert-on-failure, mirroring `rateRecipe`).
- **Pattern (the thin-coverage trap):** the failure-path test seeds the recipe with `previous` overrides, attempts an update to new `overrides` with `updateRecipe → false`, then asserts `viewModel.recipe.tagOverrides == previous`. But `previous` is ALSO the starting value, so the assertion passes both when the revert ran correctly AND when the optimistic `_recipe = copyWith(overrides)` step never executed at all. A refactor that drops the optimistic update (UI never reflects the in-flight edit — the whole point of the method) ships green. The optimistic-apply behaviour is only proven on the SUCCESS path.
- **Rule for optimistic-update-then-revert methods:** the revert test must prove the value was actually applied before being rolled back. Two cheap ways: (a) assert listener notifications fire ≥2× on the failure path (apply→notify, revert→notify) via `addListener(() => n++)` — neither BUT-1304 test asserts notifications at all, even though both paths call `notifyListeners()`; or (b) start from a THIRD distinct value so start≠previous≠overrides and assert the intermediate. Prefer (a) — it directly pins the user-visible "UI updates immediately" contract.
- **Not a blocker:** both behaviours (success persist + capture, failure revert + false) ARE covered and the contract is real; this is a Medium "strengthen the failure assertion" note, not a missing test. No mocked-away subject, no weakened assertion in the success path.
- **BUT-1300 default seam (line 1138 entry) already closed** — `defaultReextractStrategyForTest` assertions present in `recipe_detail_viewmodel_reextract_test.dart`. Re-confirmed this review.
- **Run confirmed:** `/c/tools/flutter/bin/flutter test test/unit/viewmodels/recipe_detail_viewmodel_test.dart test/unit/viewmodels/recipe_detail_viewmodel_reextract_test.dart` → 43/43 pass. (The `cmd.exe /c batch.bat` wrapper drops into an interactive prompt in this harness — do NOT use it; export PATH + call flutter directly through Bash, per the prior entry.)

### 2026-06-14 — BUT-1050 ShoppingCheckoffPantryService dedup assessment [Pattern]

The 4-test file for `ShoppingCheckoffPantryService.onItemCheckedOff` was assessed as intent-correct with one significant gap:

**What was correct (all 4 tests):** Each uses a real Mock (no concrete `@override` bodies), mocks the two dependencies (`PantryService`/`UserService`), and drives the real SUT. Every assertion is load-bearing — no test can be green-washed by a no-op. Test 1 (dedup) is exemplary: it pairs `expect(captured.quantity, 5)` WITH `verifyNever(addFromShoppingItem)` — both arms are required to prove the intent. Test 3 (pref-OFF) asserts `verifyNever(pantry.getAll)` not just `verifyNever(addFromShoppingItem)` — proves the gate fires before any pantry I/O.

**Gap found and closed — unit mismatch must NOT dedup (high priority):** The dedup predicate has TWO conditions: `p.ingredientName.toLowerCase() == item.name.toLowerCase() && p.unit == item.unit`. The 4 original tests only exercise the name match and the no-match-at-all paths. A regression dropping `p.unit == item.unit` from the predicate would aggregate "Mjölk dl" into "Mjölk l" — same name, different unit, wrong pantry math — and all 4 tests stay green. Added test 5: pantry contains "Mjölk l", check off "Mjölk dl" → `addFromShoppingItem` called, `updateItem` never called.

**Minor gap closed — profile==null (low priority):** The `null` and `autoAdd:false` branches share the same `return` statement, but the null side had no test. Added test 6: `currentUserProfile` returns `null` → no pantry I/O. Worth having because the null check is the first guard (protects against calling `.autoAddBoughtToPantry` on null) and took 2 lines.

**General rule confirmed:** When a compound AND predicate gates a dedup/aggregation branch, write one test per condition — name-only and unit-only tests are both needed. Missing either lets a one-operand regression through.

### 2026-06-14 — BUT-701 FocusTraversalGroup accessibility wrappers: testable but uncovered (5 views)
- **Trigger:** coverage review of the BUT-701 "accessibility" sprint scoped to `edit_recipe_view`, `skriv_sjalv_recept_view`, `auth/email_verification_view`, `settings/account_security_view`, `settings/notification_preferences_view`. Every diff is the SAME edit: wrap the form/screen body in `FocusTraversalGroup` so keyboard Tab walks fields in visual (reading) order. Verified pure-wrap via `git diff -w` — zero removed logic in all five; apparent removals in email_verification (the `if (_errorMessage != null)` block) and notification_preferences (4 `SizedBox`) are just re-indentation / dart-format line-wrap under the new wrapper.
- **The pattern is now applied to ~10 lib files** (`grep FocusTraversalGroup lib`: the 5 above + `mina_recept_view`, `messaging/conversations_list_view`, `personal_tags_view`, `widgets/common/input/shopping_item_dialog`, `widgets/common/navigation/adaptive_navigation`) and has **ZERO test coverage anywhere** — no test references `FocusTraversalGroup`, `nextFocus`, `sendKeyEvent(tab)`, or focus-traversal order. None of the 5 views has a widget OR journey test (test/views/ has journey tests for other flows only; existing tests for these surfaces are at VM/model level — `account_security_viewmodel_test`, `notification_preferences_test` — and are untouched by view-only changes).
- **Severity: Medium, not High.** It IS user-visible behaviour (keyboard / switch-access / external-keyboard users — the literal point of an a11y ticket) and IS testable. But `FocusTraversalGroup` with default policy delegates to Flutter's framework reading-order traversal; a regression would have to be *removing the wrapper entirely* (the only thing the diff adds). There's no app-specific ordering logic (no `FocusTraversalOrder`/`OrderedTraversalPolicy` with custom indices) to get wrong — so the failure surface is narrow. High would require custom order indices, which none of these use.
- **How to test it if a future pass wants to close this (the missing pattern):** boot the view via `createLocalizedTestApp` (these need `context.l10n` + ServiceLocator for the settings/auth VMs), `expect(find.byType(FocusTraversalGroup), findsOneWidget)` is the WEAK topology-only assert (DO-NOT per BUT-368) — instead do the BEHAVIOURAL version: focus the first field, `await tester.sendKeyEvent(LogicalKeyboardKey.tab)`, then assert `FocusScope.of(context).focusedChild` / the next field's `Focus` node `hasPrimaryFocus`. That proves Tab actually advances in order and survives the wrapper being renamed/moved. Requires ≥2 focusable fields in the pumped subtree (account_security password+email, skriv_sjalv title+fields are the richest targets; email_verification only has resend-button + continue-TextButton, thinnest).
- **Did NOT write tests this pass:** no existing view-test scaffold to extend, the behaviour is framework-delegated (low regression surface), and a meaningful keyboard-traversal test needs the full ServiceLocator boot for each view — a heavier scaffold than the Medium severity warrants for a same-shape edit replicated 5×. Flagged Medium for a single shared `focus_traversal_test` that pins the behaviour ONCE against the richest form (account_security) rather than 5 near-identical low-value tests — closing it 10× would be coverage-chasing.
- **Rule for "wrap-only a11y diffs" (Semantics/FocusTraversalGroup/ExcludeSemantics):** don't reflexively demand a per-file test. Check (a) is there custom ordering/labeling logic, or just a framework-default wrapper? Default wrapper → one shared behavioural test at most, Medium. (b) Never accept `findsOneWidget` on the wrapper type as the coverage — that's the topology anti-pattern; the assert must be Tab-advances-focus or the Semantics-node-carries-label, i.e. what the assistive-tech user experiences.

### 2026-06-14 — BUT-1306 checkoff→pantry seam: test the VM wiring, not just the service
- **Trigger:** review of unified_shopping_viewmodel + settings_hub_view + unified_shopping_view diff (auto-add-bought-items-to-pantry opt-in).
- **Pattern — "the seam lives in the VM, so the VM test must prove the wiring":** the service `ShoppingCheckoffPantryService.onItemCheckedOff` was already well covered by `test/unit/services/pantry/pantry_from_shopping_test.dart` (false→true gating, preference gating, dedup). But the diff's actual new behaviour was in the *VM*: `toggleItemBought` now captures the pre-toggle `bought` state from `items` and feeds it as `wasBought`. That capture is the whole reason the seam lives in the VM (the low-level module has no userId/pre-state). The service test can't catch a regression where the VM passes the wrong `wasBought`. → Added a "seam" group to the VM test that `verify`s `onItemCheckedOff(userId, any(), wasBought: <pre-toggle value>)` for both a fresh check-off (false) and an un-check (true).
- **Gotcha — lazy `tryGet` fields cache on construction:** the VM resolves `_checkoffPantryService` / `_userService` via `ServiceLocator.tryGet` in `late final` initialisers. The existing VM test's `setUp` builds the VM with only UnifiedShoppingService + PermissionService registered, so those fields are null. To test the seam you must register the mocks in GetIt **then rebuild the VM** (`viewModel.dispose(); viewModel = UnifiedShoppingViewModel();`) inside the group's own setUp.
- **Helper registry:** `MockUserService` in `production_mocks.dart` is a *hybrid* Mock (concrete `@override isLoading/error/getUserProfiles` bodies). It does NOT override `currentUserProfile`, so `when(() => m.currentUserProfile)` still works — but for the seam test I used a local bare `extends Mock implements UserService` to avoid the hybrid's quirks (cleaner `verify` of setAutoAddToPantry/markPantryAutoAddPrompted). `pantry_from_shopping_test.dart` already does the same — prefer a local bare mock when you need clean `when`/`verify` on UserService.
- **Public-for-test widget pattern:** `AutoAddPantryTile` was deliberately made public (not `_AutoAddPantryTile`) so it renders in isolation without the full SettingsHubView DI graph (needs ReportService + LocaleProvider too). Wrote `test/widget/views/settings/auto_add_pantry_tile_test.dart` mirroring `settings_hub_food_tile_test.dart`: reflects-stored-value, tap-persists, rebuilds-on-UserService-notify (capture the registered listener via `verify(() => m.addListener(captureAny())).captured`, flip the `thenAnswer` profile, fire it, pump), and sv+en title. The notify-rebuild test is the load-bearing one — it proves the Settings toggle mirrors the first-checkoff prompt enabling it elsewhere.
- **Did NOT test:** the one-time first-checkoff `AlertDialog` in `unified_shopping_view.dart` (`_maybeShowFirstCheckoffPrompt`). It's reachable only through the full UnifiedShoppingView boot (per the BUT-387 "views = journey test, not view test" rule). Flagged Medium for a journey test that taps a fresh item → asserts the dialog → "Yes" persists the opt-in once and never re-nags.

### 2026-06-14 — HoverableCard wrapper: new shared widget shipped with zero tests (recipe_card + menu_card refactor)
- **Trigger:** coverage review of `lib/widgets/common/hoverable_card.dart` (NEW, 96 LoC), `lib/widgets/recipe/recipe_card.dart`, `lib/widgets/common/content_cards/menu_card.dart`. Both cards swapped their static decorated `Container` for the new `HoverableCard`, which adds a real behaviour: pointer-hover cross-fades restDecoration→hoverDecoration, reverts on exit, suppresses both the swap AND the click cursor when `enabled:false`, and (WCAG 2.3.3) collapses the AnimatedContainer duration to zero under reduce-motion while STILL swapping the decoration.
- **Gap:** HoverableCard had no test file at all; menu_card.dart has no test file at all; recipe_card_test.dart didn't touch hover. All new user-visible behaviour was uncovered. → Wrote `test/widget/common/hoverable_card_test.dart` (8 behavioural tests). Verified the existing recipe_card 40 tests still pass after the structural change (the `Container.first` margin assert at line 372/424 still resolves — HoverableCard keeps its margin on a plain outer Container, deliberately per the production doc-comment, so margin-based tests are unaffected).
- **Helper learned — simulating hover in widget tests:** `tester.createGesture(kind: PointerDeviceKind.mouse)` → `addPointer(location: Offset.zero)` → `moveTo(tester.getCenter(finder))` → `pumpAndSettle()`. `addTearDown(gesture.removePointer)`. Exit = `moveTo(Offset(-500,-500))`. Read the live decoration off the single `AnimatedContainer` and compare to the *caller-passed* decoration objects (`restDeco`/`hoverDeco` consts) — this proves the swap happened without asserting any theme/shadow value, so a caller's shadow tweak can't break the wrapper test.

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-1307 FocusTraversalGroup "test" greps the source file as text — DO-NOT-WRITE
- **Trigger:** coverage review of `test/widget/views/focus_traversal_group_test.dart` (NEW, uncommitted), written to close the Medium I flagged 2026-06-14 for the BUT-701 a11y wrappers on the 5 form views.
- **What it does (the anti-pattern):** `File(path).readAsStringSync()` on each production `.dart`, then `expect(source.contains('FocusTraversalGroup('), isTrue)` AND `expect(source.contains('<intent-comment-prose>'), isTrue)`. It tests the *bytes of the source file*, not the running widget tree. This is strictly weaker than the `findsOneWidget`-on-wrapper assert I already rejected as the topology anti-pattern in the 2026-06-14 entry — a grep can't even confirm the widget is mounted, only that the substring is present somewhere in the file (incl. a comment or dead code).
- **Fails all three intent questions:** (1) won't fail if behaviour breaks — swap to a custom `OrderedTraversalPolicy` with wrong indices and Tab order regresses while the string `FocusTraversalGroup(` stays present → green; (2) breaks on harmless refactors — reword the intent comment, extract the form body into a helper widget in another file, or alias the constructor, all break it with zero behaviour change; the `contains(intentComment)` prose-pin is the worst, it literally tests a code comment. (3) The 2026-06-14 entry already wrote the correct test: boot account_security via `createLocalizedTestApp`, focus field 1, `sendKeyEvent(LogicalKeyboardKey.tab)`, assert next field `hasPrimaryFocus`.
- **Note the test's own rationale comment argues FOR grepping** ("pumping a shell copy proves only the copy has the group") — that's a real concern but the conclusion is wrong: the fix is to pump the *real* view behind its ServiceLocator boot (heavy but doable, the entry named account_security/skriv_sjalv as the richest targets), not to abandon behavioural testing for a string match. A passing source-grep gives false confidence that keyboard a11y is covered when nothing exercises focus at all.
- **Confirmed:** no behavioural focus test exists for any of the 5 views (`grep sendKeyEvent|hasPrimaryFocus|nextFocus test/` → only `keyboard_submittable_form_test` + `app_shortcuts_test`, unrelated). So real keyboard-traversal coverage for these surfaces is still ZERO; this test does not change that.
- **Verdict:** High — the new test is coverage theatre that should be replaced by one shared behavioural test (Tab advances focus on the richest form), per the 2026-06-14 prescription. Don't `touch` the testing marker on the strength of a source-grep test.
- **Reduce-motion test pattern:** wrap child in `MediaQuery(data: MediaQueryData(disableAnimations: true), ...)` and assert `AnimatedContainer.duration == Duration.zero` AND that hover still yields hoverDeco. This pins the WCAG intent (motion removed, feedback kept) that `AnimationUtils.getReducedDuration` implements — a regression that made reduce-motion drop the hover entirely (vs just instant) would be caught.
- **Cursor test:** read `MouseRegion.cursor` (first descendant) — `enabled:false` ⇒ `isNot(SystemMouseCursors.click)`, enabled ⇒ `== SystemMouseCursors.click`. Proves the "don't imply clickability on non-tappable cards" intent that recipe_card/menu_card both rely on via `enabled: onTap != null`.
- **Did NOT add (Medium gap flagged):** no test asserts that recipe_card/menu_card actually pass `enabled: onTap != null` down to HoverableCard, nor that menu_card renders/behaves at all (no menu_card_test.dart exists). The HoverableCard unit tests cover the mechanism; the wiring (each card gating hover on its tap callback) is a one-liner per card that a refactor could silently flip. A thin per-card test (tap-callback present ⇒ MouseRegion cursor is click; absent ⇒ not) would close it without topology-asserting.
- **Windows flutter-test PATH (reconfirmed + extended):** the cmd.bat wrapper from MEMORY needs MORE than a native PATH — flutter's launcher shells out to PowerShell AND git. Minimal working PATH for `flutter test`/`dart analyze`: `C:\Windows\System32;C:\Windows\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0;C:\Program Files\Git\cmd;C:\tools\flutter\bin;C:\tools\dart\bin`. Omitting PowerShell → "PowerShell executable not found"; omitting git → "Unable to determine engine version". Redirect output INSIDE the bat (`1> log 2>&1`), invoke via `cmd.exe //c "C:\tools\x.bat"` (double-slash escapes git-bash path munging). Forward-slash test paths get split by cmd if passed on the cmd line — keep them inside the bat or use backslashes.

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-1307 fix: replaced source-grep test, BUT the prescribed behavioural test does NOT guard the wrapper [Correction to own prescription]
- **What I did:** deleted the source-grep `focus_traversal_group_test.dart` (the DO-NOT-WRITE flagged 2026-06-15) and rewrote it as the prescribed behavioural test — boot `AccountSecurityView` via `createLocalizedTestApp(wrapInScaffold: false)` behind a `TestServiceLocator.registerMock<AuthService>(MockFactory.createAuthService(...))` boot (the view builds its own VM which resolves AuthService on construction), focus the top field, `sendKeyEvent(LogicalKeyboardKey.tab)`, assert the fields receive focus top-to-bottom.
- **CRITICAL discovery — the 2026-06-14/06-15 prescription is imperfect for THIS widget:** I removed the `FocusTraversalGroup` from production and re-ran — **the test stayed GREEN.** In a plain top-to-bottom `Column`, Flutter's default reading-order traversal already walks the fields in the same order, so the wrapper is a no-op for observable Tab order. `FocusTraversalGroup` (default policy) only changes behaviour when there are focusable siblings OUTSIDE the group to escape to, or a custom `OrderedTraversalPolicy`/indices — neither exists in these five views. So NO black-box keyboard test can distinguish "wrapper present" from "wrapper absent" here. The earlier entries' "boot + Tab + assert next field" prescription proves the *reading-order contract*, not the *wrapper*.
- **Gotcha — Tab does NOT go field→field:** each password TextField has a focusable show/hide-password suffix `IconButton`, so Tab order is field1 → its IconButton → field2 → … . My first draft asserted `orderedNodes[1].hasPrimaryFocus` after one Tab and failed (focus landed on the IconButton, a bare `FocusNode` on a `Focus` widget). Fix: tab repeatedly, record only the EditableText nodes' on-screen `getTopLeft().dy` as they gain focus, assert that sequence equals its sorted-ascending copy (reading order). Recognise a field by mapping `(element.widget as EditableText).focusNode -> top` from `find.byType(EditableText).evaluate()`.
- **Honest verdict for the reviewer/marker:** the rewrite is strictly better than the source-grep (it exercises real focus + survives refactors + tests the user-facing reading-order contract). But it is NOT a tight guard of the BUT-701 wrapper, because the wrapper has no observable effect in these layouts. The test header documents this explicitly so a future reader doesn't over-trust it. The deeper truth from the 2026-06-14 Medium entry holds: these wrap-only a11y diffs have a near-zero regression surface and arguably warranted no test at all — the correct fix to the bad test was "replace with the best honest behavioural test OR delete," and I chose replace-with-honest-caveats. Do not claim the test "guards the FocusTraversalGroup."

## 2026-06-15 — Review of BUT-1308 hover tests (trigger: test-coverage review of recipe_card_hover_test + menu_card_hover_test)
- **Pattern (recurring): "mounts a HoverableCard ancestor" is the BUT-368 topology anti-pattern.** `expect(find.descendant(of: Card, matching: byType(HoverableCard)), findsOneWidget)` asserts the *wrapper exists*, not that hover *works*. Breaks on a behaviour-preserving refactor (different wrapper), survives the feature breaking. Don't write these.
- **The HoverableCard subject was mocked away by reading constructor args.** Every assertion read `widget.restDecoration` / `widget.hoverDecoration` / `widget.enabled` straight off the `HoverableCard` instance. NONE simulated a pointer. So the tests pass even if `_HoverableCardState` never wired `onEnter`/`onExit` — i.e. hover fully broken still goes green. This is the "mock away the behaviour you claim to verify" trap.
- **How to actually test HoverableCard behaviour (the missing test):** drive a mouse gesture and assert the RENDERED decoration flips.
  ```dart
  final gesture = await tester.createGesture(kind: PointerDeviceKind.mouse);
  await gesture.addPointer(location: Offset.zero);
  addTearDown(gesture.removePointer);
  await tester.pump();
  await gesture.moveTo(tester.getCenter(find.byType(HoverableCard)));
  await tester.pumpAndSettle();
  final ac = tester.widget<AnimatedContainer>(find.descendant(
      of: find.byType(HoverableCard), matching: find.byType(AnimatedContainer)));
  expect(ac.decoration, hoverDecoration);   // flipped from rest under the cursor
  ```
  And for `enabled`, assert the rendered `MouseRegion.cursor` is `SystemMouseCursors.click` vs `MouseCursor.defer` — that's what the user's cursor actually does — instead of reading the `enabled` bool.
- **Hardcoded token equality (`expect(rest, equals(InputThemes.recipeCardDecoration))`, `borderWidthThin`, `borderRadiusM` literals)** is the "should use AppDimensions.X" anti-pattern — cosmetic-tweak-fragile, proves nothing user-facing. MenuCard test did correctly capture `cs.surface`/`cs.outline` via a `Builder` (good) but still pinned literal radius/width.
- **Untested real branch:** `RecipeCard(isSelected: true)` swaps to a distinct green-outline rounded decoration and hover must NOT apply. Tests only ever build the unselected card. That conditional is genuine user-visible logic and is the test worth having here.
- **Reinvented helper:** menu_card_hover_test inlined a private `_wrap()` MaterialApp instead of `createLocalizedTestApp` (the recipe test used the helper correctly). Use the registered helper.

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-1309: tab-order guard extended to 3 of 5 BUT-701 views; settings view still uncovered
- **Trigger:** coverage review of `test/widget/views/focus_traversal_group_test.dart` (modified, staged). The single-view pin from BUT-1307 (account_security) grew to a shared `expectTabWalksControlsTopToBottom` harness covering account_security + edit_recipe + email_verification. All 3 pass (`flutter test` green, ~3s).
- **Quality of what landed (good, record as a positive pattern):** the harness is the BEHAVIOURAL version called for in the BUT-1307 knowledge entry above — it focuses controls via real `LogicalKeyboardKey.tab` presses and asserts monotonic top-to-bottom Y over one traversal cycle. Three robustness wins worth reusing: (1) cycle detection + de-dup keyed on focus-node IDENTITY, not Y, because tabbing below the fold scrolls the viewport and shifts every control's measured Y (can go negative) — a position key would false-cycle; (2) capture each control's Y the FIRST time it's focused = the order the user perceives; (3) a `minControls` floor that fails loudly if the form rendered empty (setup regression) instead of vacuously passing. The header comment is honest that the `FocusTraversalGroup` wrapper is a no-op for observable order in a plain Column (verified by removing it → still green) and that the test guards the user-facing reading-order contract, NOT widget presence. This is NOT a weakened assertion; it's the correct contract per BUT-368.
- **Gap (Medium, in-scope "settings"):** BUT-701 wrapped FIVE views; this file covers THREE. Uncovered: `settings/notification_preferences_view.dart` (settings-area form, `FocusTraversalGroup` over multiple `SwitchListTile`s — the harness's button-fallback branch would handle it as-is, just needs a 4th `testWidgets` block + the notification-prefs VM/service mocks in `setUp`) and `skriv_sjalv_recept_view.dart`. The shared harness makes adding them ~10 lines each; no new harness logic needed.
- **Latent fragility (Low) in the email_verification case:** its `minControls=2` floor only passes because `_lastResendTime` is null on first render → resend button enabled (`onPressed != null` → focusable). If a future change starts the resend cooldown on mount (resend disabled at t=0), only the continue TextButton is focusable and the test fails with a misleading "form failed to render its controls" reason rather than catching a real order regression. If that screen changes, give the button-only case its own explicit 2-button assertion or advance `clock` past any initial cooldown. The view reads `clock.now()` (package:clock) so a `withClock`/`fakeAsync` wrapper can control it deterministically.
- **No mocked-away subject:** real views render with real Flutter focus traversal; only leaf services (AuthService, PermissionService, SocialRecipeService, OfflineService against test repos, PersonalTagService) are mocked. The behaviour under test (traversal) is not stubbed.

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-948 shopping multi-select: review of new test suite [Review + Gaps]
**Overall verdict: well-structured, two concrete actions required before merging, one pre-existing rot category to track.**

**What is adequately covered:**
- `ShoppingSelectionManager` state machine (enter/toggle/auto-exit/selectAll/clear) — 6 unit tests, all user-visible invariants proven.
- `bulkRemoveItems` happy-path (all 3 ids forwarded), partial-failure path (returns false), `restoreItems` re-add (both items forwarded by name) — 3 VM tests, reason: strings on the partial-failure test document the WHY.
- Tile long-press enters selection + circle shown + per-item actions hidden, tap-on-only-selected exits selection, normal-tap check-off fires callback when not selecting — 3 widget tests with real `ShoppingSelectionManager`.
- Nullable-provider degradation (no `ShoppingSelectionManager` in tree → tile degrades to plain check-off) — implicitly exercised by every test in `shopping_item_tiles_test.dart` (no provider injected anywhere in that file). Sufficient.
- `buildDraggableItemTile` no longer wraps in `LongPressDraggable`, `buildItemTile` with `onMoveToCategory` shows grip handle and `Draggable` — two backward-compat drag tests correctly updated.

**Gap 1 (P2, add before merge): `bulkRemoveItems` permission-gate is untested.** Production has `if (!canEditActiveList) return false; ...` — a `removeItem` call never fires. The three existing tests only run under the default `FakePermissionService` with `defaultHasPermission: true`. Add one test: set `mockPermissionService.setPermissionState(currentUserId: null, isAuthenticated: false)` then call `bulkRemoveItems({'a'})` and assert `ok == false` AND `verifyNever(() => mockShoppingService.removeItemFromActiveList(any()))`. Without it, stripping the permission guard from `bulkRemoveItems` (while leaving it on `addItem`) passes all tests silently.

**Gap 2 (P3, acceptable omission with caveat): view-level `_deleteSelectedShopping` flow is not widget-tested.** The analogous pantry flow IS tested in `test/widget/views/pantry/pantry_bulk_delete_test.dart` (happy + failure path). The shopping view flow is structurally identical: snapshot → clearSelection → bulkRemoveItems(ok?) → if ok: snackbar with undo. The one behaviour the pantry analogue does NOT cover for shopping: the `if (!mounted || !ok) return` check means a failed delete shows no undo. If that guard is accidentally changed to `if (!mounted) return`, the VM-level tests stay green (VM returns false, nobody checks the view respected it). A single targeted `testWidgets` asserting `find.text('Ångra'), findsNothing` after stubbing `bulkRemoveItems` to return false would close this. Acceptable to defer if `UnifiedShoppingView` is heavy to pump; document as a known gap.

**Pre-existing rot in `shopping_item_tiles_test.dart` (not introduced by this PR, track for cleanup):**
- Lines ~651–819: asserts `find.byType(AnimatedContainer)`, `find.byType(ScaleTransition)`, `find.byType(AnimatedSwitcher)`, and `animatedContainer.duration == ThemeConstants.durationStandard` — these are widget-tree structure assertions, not user-visible behaviour. The animation-runs tests (midScale > 1.0, check-icon appears on toggle) are fine; the widget-TYPE-exists tests and the timing-constant-matches-exact-constant tests are the DO-NOT-WRITE pattern. They break on any animation-class refactor with no user behaviour change.
- Priority indicator tests (lines ~160–222) assert `decoration.color == AppColors.lightColorScheme.primary` — a static constant, not a theme-captured value. The 2026-05-02 BUT-755..758 entry documents exactly this: `AppColors.X → cs.X` migrations break these silently. Should use `Builder` + `late ColorScheme cs` and assert `cs.primary`.

**Decision rule reinforced: when the partial-failure return value gates whether undo is offered, pin BOTH the VM returning false AND a view-level test that the snackbar is absent on false. The pantry analogue (pantry_bulk_delete_test.dart) is the template.** The shopping PR has half of this (VM side) but not the view side.

### 2026-06-15 — BUT-1331: offline-degrade repo test + sealed-collection injection seam
- **Trigger:** wrote a regression test for `FirebaseIngredientRepository._doLoadCache()` swallowing `FirebaseException('unavailable')` (cold offline cache) instead of `rethrow`-ing.
- **New helper pattern — flaky-firestore via `collection()`, NOT `.get()`:** `CollectionReference`/`Query` are **sealed** in cloud_firestore 6.x (`subtype_of_sealed_class` analyzer error) — you CANNOT `implements CollectionReference` to override `.get()`. Inject the failure one level up: a `class _FlakyFirestore implements FirebaseFirestore` that throws `FirebaseException(code:'unavailable')` inside `collection(path)` for the target collection, else delegates to a real `FakeFirebaseFirestore` via `noSuchMethod`. The repo resolves its collection getter *inside* the `_doLoadCache` try-block (`_firestore.collection(...).get()`), so throwing from `collection()` is the same failure surface as a throwing `.get()`. Seed writes go through the backing fake **directly** (not the wrapper) so they don't trip the fail counter.
- **`failFirstGet` vs `failAll`:** the repo self-heals — any post-failure call retries because `_cacheLoadedAt` stays null on failure. So a "cache stays empty after failure" assertion needs `failAll:true` (every load fails); a "self-heal" assertion needs `failFirstGet:true` (fail once, then `forceRefresh()` populates). Don't assert emptiness with a single-shot failure firestore — call #2 (`count()`→`_ensureCacheLoaded`→`loadCache`) succeeds and repopulates, which looks like a test bug but is correct production behaviour.
- **Intent-gate confirmed:** all 3 tests FAIL against a temporary `rethrow` (the `unavailable` exception propagates out of `findByName`); pass once swallowed. Verified by temp-reverting production then restoring. Genuinely gates the fix.
- **File:** `test/unit/repositories/firebase_ingredient_repository_offline_degrade_test.dart`. Construction pattern reused from the sibling `firebase_ingredient_repository_cache_test.dart` (plain `FakeFirebaseFirestore` injected via the `firestore:` ctor param + mutable `_Clock` for TTL); only the throwing wrapper is new.

### 2026-06-17 — FeedbackInboxViewModel + FeedbackEntry status tests [Pattern]
**Review of new admin-inbox tests (21 tests, all green).**
- `_FakeFeedbackRepository` is correctly a `Fake implements` (concrete bodies, no mocktail `when()`), not a Mock. Pattern is sound.
- `Future.delayed(Duration.zero)` for draining the stream-listen microtask is acceptable per the 2026-05-23 Wave-14 entry — pumpEventQueue is flutter_test-coupled; fakeAsync adds unnecessary complexity for a single yield.
- **FeedbackEntry status tests**: wire-name stability test asserts `.wireName` directly (not via toMap) for each enum value — this WILL catch a Dart enum rename. Correct layering.
- **P2 gap identified (not blocking):** `_subscribe` `onError` handler calls `setError(err.toString())`. No test proves a stream error surfaces as `vm.error != null`. A refactor swallowing that error leaves admins with a silent empty list. Worth closing in a follow-up with: `repo.emit via addError + Future.delayed + expect(vm.error, isNotNull)`.
- **P3 gaps (low priority):** `setStatusFilter(same value)` no-op guard (no re-subscribe); `start()` idempotency (second call is a no-op); `dispose` cancels subscription. All have production guards but no tests. Low priority because the guards are one-liners; only escalate if the VM grows complexity around these paths.
- `canLoadMore` boundary test probes both sides (length == limit → true; length < limit → false). Correct.
- `loadMore` limit-arithmetic test is the right level — asserts the parameter passed to the repo, not that more records appeared.

## Discovered patterns

### 2026-06-19 — AppColors→theme color-source swap needs NO test change (trigger: BUT-572 guard refactor in import_via_url_view.dart)
- **Change:** `_UrlResultRow._statusIcon()` in `lib/views/import_via_url_view.dart` now takes `BuildContext` and resolves status-icon colors from the theme (`context.butleryColors.success`, `Theme.of(context).colorScheme.error`) instead of `const AppColors.success/error`. Which icon shows for which `UrlFetchStatus` is unchanged.
- **Assessment: no test additions or updates required.** This is a pure color-SOURCE swap with identical behaviour — exactly the "harmless refactor" the DO-NOT-WRITE rules say tests must survive.
- **Existing test:** `test/widget/import/import_via_url_view_multi_test.dart` line 240 ("per-row status icons distinguish success / failure / loading") asserts icon PRESENCE via `find.byIcon(Icons.check_circle)` / `Icons.error` / `CircularProgressIndicator` — never icon color. So it does not break and does not need updating. This is the correct level (behaviour, not theme value).
- **Rule reinforced:** Do NOT add a color assertion here. Asserting `icon.color == AppColors.success` would be the exact hardcoded-theme-value anti-pattern from BUT-368. If a color assertion were ever wanted, it must capture the live `ColorScheme`/`context.butleryColors` via a `Builder` and compare against that — but for a presence-already-covered status icon it adds no user-visible-behaviour coverage and is not worth writing.
- **Safety note:** `_wrap` in the multi-test already pins `AppTheme.lightTheme`, which registers the `ButleryColors` ThemeExtension (see `app_theme.dart` `extensions:`), and `context.butleryColors` has a `?? ButleryColors.light` fallback — so even a theme missing the extension can't throw. No runtime risk from the new `context` lookup in tests.
- **Static-inspection only:** this container has no flutter/dart toolchain; assessment is from reading the diff + grepping test/, not from running `flutter test`.

### 2026-06-20 — Auth routing + seed-filter test review [Pattern + Review]
**Files:** `test/widget/views/auth/auth_view_register_routing_test.dart` (new, untracked), `test/unit/viewmodels/recipe_list_viewmodel_test.dart` (diff-only, seeded-recipe group added).
- **Auth routing test:** Intent is sound — `_ReplaceObserver.replaceCount` catches a re-introduced `pushReplacement` after `register()` instantly. The login test provides a positive control that proves the observer works (replaceCount==1 for login). Moderate gap: `replaceCount==0` is also vacuously true if `_handleSubmit` short-circuits before reaching `register()` (e.g. form validator fires). Mitigation: assert `realViewModel.error == null` after the submit pump, which distinguishes "didn't push because register succeeded" from "didn't push because submit bailed early." Adding a `registerCallCount` counter to the concrete `registerWithEmail` body in `MockAuthService` is the airtight fix.
- **Seed-filter tests:** Direction is correct. Test 3 (non-seed excluded) is the tightest and most important — it would catch `if (tagResult == null) return true` over-broadening. Tests 1/2 (seeds survive) pass even if only some seeds survive filtering; `expect(viewModel.recipes.length, RecipeSeeds.allRecipes.length)` would catch partial drops.
- **Decision rule for negative-space routing tests:** always add a canary proving the tested-path was actually reached (call-count, error-state, or explicit `expect(realViewModel.foo, X, reason: 'proves X path was taken')`), otherwise `replaceCount==0` is structurally identical to "nothing fired at all."
- **`MockAuthService extends Mock` with concrete `@override` method bodies** — this is the anti-pattern from BUT-368, but it's intentional here (the concrete body IS the fake behavior). Since `when()` can't override it, a test author trying to stub `signInWithEmail` via mocktail would silently use the concrete body. Document in the class header that `when()` on these methods has no effect.

### 2026-06-20 — WS4 ChatViewModel Poll Operations: one gap [Pattern + Review]
**File:** `test/unit/viewmodels/chat_viewmodel_test.dart` — "Poll Operations" group (4 tests).
- **Four tests evaluated against the production `votePoll`/`closePoll` branch map.** Production has three early-return guards: (a) `_isDisposed` (lifecycle, covered by Lifecycle group), (b) `target == null` (message not in list), (c) `pollData == null` (message present, no `poll` metadata key). Tests cover `allowMultiple=true`, `allowMultiple=false`, `target == null`, and the `closePoll` delegation. All four are intent-driven and would fail on real regressions.
- **One missing case:** guard (c) — a message with the target ID is in the cached list but its `metadata` has no `'poll'` key (e.g. a plain text message or a message whose poll key was stripped). `verifyNever(votePoll(...))` is the correct assertion. The existing no-op test only exercises guard (b); guard (c) is distinct production code and untested.
- **No action needed on `closePoll`:** returns void, swallows exceptions, no caller-visible error surface — the delegation test is sufficient.
- **`Future.delayed` vs `fakeAsync`:** the stream pump pattern uses `Future.delayed(50ms)` throughout the file. Technically fragile on slow CI, but consistent with the surrounding 37 tests; not a blocker in isolation.
- **Decision rule:** for a metadata-resolution method with N distinct early-return guards, write one no-op test per guard path, not just one combined "nothing happened" test. Guards (b) and (c) look similar but exercise different lines — a future developer deleting guard (c) and relying on guard (b) would not be caught by the current suite.

### 2026-06-21 — fetchFriendRecipe: subclass-override as cache-injection seam [Pattern + Review]
**File:** `test/unit/services/unified_recipe_service_friend_fetch_test.dart` (3 tests, all pass).
- **Subject:** `UnifiedRecipeService.fetchFriendRecipe` — 5-line method that checks `getRecipeById(recipeId)` (O(1) map lookup into private `_recipeById`) and, on miss, delegates to `_getRecipeRepository().readSharedRecipe(...)`.
- **Seam question:** `_recipeById` is private; the implementer subclassed `UnifiedRecipeService` as `_FakeUnifiedRecipeService` and overrode the public `getRecipeById(id)` with a fall-through: `_injectedCache[id] ?? super.getRecipeById(id)`. Is this mocking away the subject?
- **Assessment: ACCEPTABLE.** `getRecipeById` is the only public interface the service exposes for the map lookup. The override does NOT replace `fetchFriendRecipe` itself — the real method runs unchanged. The override replaces the DEPENDENCY it reads (the in-memory recipe index), exactly as injecting a fake repo replaces the Firestore dependency. The subject under test is the branching logic in `fetchFriendRecipe`; the cache state is an input to that logic. This is legitimate seam exploitation, not mocking-away-the-subject.
- **Critical check: verifyNever IS present on cache-hit test.** Line 208–213 asserts `verifyNever(() => mockRecipeRepository.readSharedRecipe(ownerId: any, recipeId: any))`. This means test (1) would fail if `fetchFriendRecipe` called the repository even when `getRecipeById` returned non-null. This is the key proof-of-intent assertion — the test earns its existence.
- **Tests 2 and 3:** `verify(...).called(1)` on cache-miss (correct ids pinned), `expect(result, isNull)` on repo-null — both sound and would catch real regressions.
- **One minor observation (not a blocker):** `_getRecipeRepository()` resolves via `_recipeRepository ?? ServiceLocator.get<RecipeRepository>()`. The test wires `mockRecipeRepository` both as a ctor arg AND via `TestServiceLocator.registerMock` — doubly safe. This is fine.
- **Decision rule codified:** When a service accesses private state via a public getter (`getRecipeById` reads `_recipeById`), subclassing and overriding THAT GETTER is the canonical seam — it avoids `@visibleForTesting` leaks while still testing the real method. The seam is legitimate as long as: (a) the override is a straight fall-through (`injected ?? super.method()`), (b) the real method under test is NOT overridden, and (c) the cache-hit test pairs `verifyNever` on the downstream dep.

### 2026-06-19 — EngagementViewModel admin-tab test review [Pattern + Review]
**File:** `test/unit/viewmodels/admin/engagement_viewmodel_test.dart` (5/5 green, untracked). Reviewed against the `import_health_viewmodel_test.dart` template.
- **Pattern confirmed sound.** `_FakeEngagementRepository extends EngagementRepository` with a `FakeFirebaseFirestore()` super-call that is never exercised (overrides short-circuit first) matches the import_health template exactly. `throwOnLoad` arms both methods simultaneously — correct because the production VM calls them sequentially inside a single `executeAsyncVoid`.
- **All 5 tests pass the intent gate.** (1) zero-data no-crash — would fail if `days.first` were accessed unconditionally. (2) user count without activity — covers the decoupled read path (regression if only `days` was fetched on success). (3) latest-day max-across-features — feeds an older day with a higher single-feature count (cooked:99) and a newer day with max in a different feature per window; pins sort direction AND `FeatureCounts.max` fold; would fail on ascending sort or a sum-instead-of-max regression. (4) load failure → error state, `days` empty — covers `executeAsyncVoid` swallow + state hygiene. (5) `refresh()` delegates to `load()` — entry-point coverage for the refresh button.
- **P2 gap (not blocking):** no test covers the partial-success scenario — `getUserCount()` succeeds while `getDailyFeatureRetention()` throws (or vice versa). The `throwOnLoad` flag arms both methods. Depending on production intent (atomic-or-nothing vs best-effort partial), `userCount` may be non-zero with `error != null`. Worth a follow-up test once the intent is decided.
- **Consistent omission with the sibling file:** neither this file nor `import_health_viewmodel_test.dart` asserts `notifyListeners()` fires. `executeAsyncVoid` drives it through the base class lifecycle, but a regression removing the base calls would leave a frozen admin tab. Low priority.
- **Decision rule for admin tab VMs:** the main risk is crash-on-zero-data (pre-launch state); the zero-data and error-state tests are the load-bearing ones. The remaining tests cover the active-data derivation contract (stat cards). This is the right coverage shape for a read-only admin view.

### 2026-06-21 — RecipeDetailView readOnly flag: overflow-menu gap is acceptable, favorite anchor is load-bearing [Pattern + Review]
**File:** `test/widget/views/recipe_detail_read_only_test.dart` (2/2 green).
- **Subject:** `RecipeDetailView(readOnly: true)` hides favorite toggle and overflow-menu items (Edit, Edit-tags, Delete); retains save-copy and non-owner actions.
- **Test 1 intent:** "favorite toggle absent + save-copy present when readOnly:true" — proves the guard fires and does not destroy the retained action in one pump. Would fail if `if (!widget.readOnly)` were removed from the favorite block, OR if `showForkInAppBar` stopped returning true for a friend's recipe.
- **Test 2 intent:** "favorite toggle visible when readOnly:false (default)" — regression guard for the normal path; would fail if readOnly defaulted to true or if the toggle were unconditionally hidden.
- **False-confidence analysis:** `showForkInAppBar(friendRecipe.createdBy, 'test-user-123')` returns true because `_friendUserId ('friend-user-456') != _testUserId ('test-user-123')`, and `createPermissionService()` defaults `currentUserId` to `'test-user-123'`. The save-copy visibility is NOT gated on `readOnly` — it is gated on `showForkInAppBar`, which is independent. So a test of "save-copy present when readOnly:true" does NOT prove that save-copy ALSO appears when readOnly:false with the same recipe — but that is tested by the existing fork-placement unit tests (BUT-1178). No structural false confidence here.
- **Overflow-menu gap (Edit, Edit-tags, Delete): acceptable skip.** Opening `PopupMenuButton` in a widget test requires `.tap(find.byType(PopupMenuButton))` + `pumpAndSettle`, which works BUT the view also has `if (showForkInAppBar...)` placing the save-copy in the app bar, meaning `PopupMenuButton` in the app bar may require `.hitTestable()`. The scope note on lines 11–18 of the test is honest. The three hidden items share a single structural pattern — all three are under the same `if (!widget.readOnly)` guard in the same `itemBuilder` list. Testing the favorite guard is sufficient to prove the guard mechanism works; the overflow items all use the IDENTICAL `if (!widget.readOnly)` expression with no additional logic. A regression that removed one `if` guard and not another would be caught by `flutter analyze` (compile error if a `PopupMenuItem` were mis-indented out of the `if` block) or a manual smoke pass. **The gap is real but low-risk given the structural homogeneity of the guards.**
- **Decision rule: when N production-code guards are structurally identical (same boolean expression, same nesting pattern, same file), testing ONE of them is sufficient IF the intent test would fail on a regression to the boolean and a code-review / analyze catch would catch an indent/scope error on the others. Document the conscious skip in the test's library comment.**

### 2026-06-21 — SocialRequest.recipeShareRequest model review [Pattern]

**copyWith-survival gap is the canonical missing test for discriminated-union models.** When a model has type-specific nullable fields (recipeId/recipeTitle for `recipeShareRequest`, groupId/groupName/etc. for `groupInvitation`), the critical invariant is that a `copyWith(status: ...)` call (the Phase 2 accept flow) preserves those fields. Production `copyWith` threaded all fields correctly, but no test verified it. The factory + round-trip tests are necessary but not sufficient — add a third test: `copyWith(status: accepted)` on an instance built with the factory, assert the type-specific fields survive.

**Mutual-exclusivity test for a new `is*` predicate.** Per the 2026-05-23 Wave-17 lesson, every new `is*` getter on a discriminated union needs a negative test on the OTHER concrete types. The new file only asserted `isRecipeShareRequest == true` for the new type; it missed `isRecipeShareRequest == false` for `friend` and `groupInvitation`. Pattern: always pair the positive with a negative covering all other enum variants.

**`toFirestore` omission symmetry test.** When a model uses `if (field != null) 'key': value` in `toFirestore`, add a test asserting the key is ABSENT for a type that should not have it — mirrors the existing `toFirestore omits group fields for a friend request` pattern in `social_request_test.dart`.

**Two tests submitted → five tests APPROVED**: factory+payload, round-trip, mutual-exclusivity negative, field-omission symmetry, copyWith-survival.

### 2026-06-21 — RecipeDetailView share-request banner: registerFallbackValue pattern + owner-gate false-confidence gap [Pattern + Review]
**File:** `test/widget/views/recipe_detail_read_only_test.dart` — banner test (1/1 green); full suite 3/3 green.

**Behavior under test:** when `RecipeDetailView` receives a non-null `shareRequest` AND the current user owns the recipe, a banner with the requester's name and a "Dela med {name}" button renders; tapping it calls `SocialRecipeService.acceptRecipeShareRequest(request)` exactly once.

**Assessment against the four questions:**

1. **Real contract proved?** YES. The test asserts both text presence (`find.textContaining('Anna')` + `find.textContaining('Dela med Anna')`) AND call count (`verify(...).called(1)`). The verify is on the real stub registered via `TestServiceLocator.registerMock<SocialRecipeService>` — not on a hand-rolled counter or side-channel capture.

2. **SocialRecipeService mocked (not the subject)?** YES. `_MockSocialRecipeService extends Mock implements SocialRecipeService` — no concrete `@override` bodies, so `when()/verify()` work cleanly. The banner widget + its `_onShare()` method are the subject; the service is the dependency.

3. **`registerFallbackValue(SocialRequest(...))` legitimate?** YES, and this is the correct pattern. Mocktail requires a registered fallback for any non-primitive type used with `any()`. `SocialRequest` is a plain Dart class (not sealed; public constructor; copyable) so constructing a minimal fallback instance is safe. The fallback does NOT get called in the stub — it only satisfies the type system when mocktail needs to produce a typed placeholder for `captureAny`/`any()` assertions. This is the same pattern as the existing `registerFallbackValue(RecipeFactory.build(id: 'fallback'))`. CORRECT, NOT masking a problem.

4. **False-confidence on owner-gate?** PARTIAL RISK — documented below. The test pumps `ownedRecipe` (which has `createdBy == _testUserId == 'test-user-123'`) and the default `createPermissionService()` sets `currentUserId = 'test-user-123'`. The gate `recipe.createdBy == PermissionService.currentUserId` therefore passes correctly. **However, there is no complementary "non-owner gets no banner" test** — a regression that removed the `recipe.createdBy == ...` guard would still pass this test (the banner would render for non-owners, but this test never pumps a non-owner scenario with a `shareRequest`). This is a MISSING COVERAGE gap, not a false green in the existing test.

**Missing test (P2 — medium priority):** Add a second test in the same group:
```
testWidgets('banner is suppressed when the current user does not own the recipe', ...)
  // pump: shareRequest non-null, recipe.createdBy = _friendUserId (NOT _testUserId)
  // expect: find.textContaining('Anna'), findsNothing
  // This would fail today if the owner-guard were removed.
```
Without this, the owner-gate can be silently removed and the test suite stays green.

**Pattern rule:** whenever a widget is gated on `currentUser == owner`, always write BOTH the positive case (owner → renders) AND the negative case (non-owner → absent). One-sided ownership tests are a recurring false-confidence shape.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1336 SocialMediaExtractor failure-path gap [Pattern + Bug documented]

**Criterion 1 (ContentDetectorService) was already fully covered.** The existing `content_detector_service_test.dart` tests every case in the acceptance criteria: Instagram/TikTok → `socialMediaUrl`, plain recipe URL → `recipeUrl`, YouTube → `recipeUrl` + `SourcePlatform.website` (NOT `socialMediaUrl`), recipe-keyword text → `recipeText`, arbitrary text → `plainText`. Nothing new added there — do not duplicate.

**Criterion 2 gap: `extractFromUrl` failure path never asserted `success==false` or `error!=null`.** The existing `social_media_extractor_test.dart` failure tests all did `expect(result, isA<ExtractionResult>())` — type-check only, no assertion on `success` or `error`. This means a regression changing `success: false → success: true` or nulling `error` would have gone green.

**Injection seam: `SocialMediaExtractor(manager: ...)`.** `ExtractionManager` is constructor-injectable. The correct seam is a LOCAL concrete subclass (`_FakeExtractionManager extends ExtractionManager` overriding only `extractFromUrl`) — NOT `extends Mock` with `@override` bodies (which silently blocks mocktail's `when()`). The base class `BaseService` is abstract with `serviceName`/`onDispose` abstract; subclassing the concrete `ExtractionManager` satisfies them automatically.

**Two failure modes tested:** (1) manager returns `ExtractionResult(success:false, error: <msg>)` → verify the facade forwards `success==false` and the exact error string; (2) manager throws → verify the null-coalescing fallback (`?? ExtractionResult(success:false, error:'Extraction operation failed')`) fires.

**`metadata['reason']` is a real bug, NOT fixed here.** `receive_share_view.dart` line 191 reads `result.metadata['reason'] as String?` for analytics, but `ExtractionResult` never populates `metadata['reason']` — it always arrives as `null`. Added a CHARACTERIZATION test that pins `metadata['reason'] == null` with an inline flip instruction for when it's fixed. Filed as follow-up on BUT-1336.

**Test file:** `test/unit/services/extraction/social_media_extractor_test.dart` — new group `extractFromUrl failure path (BUT-1336)`, 3 tests appended.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1335 RecipeListViewModel allergen/dietary safety gate [Pattern discovered]

**Trigger:** Writing dedicated tests for the five acceptance criteria of the allergen/dietary filter safety gate in `RecipeListViewModel`.

**Key patterns:**

**Existing tests already covered:** seed-recipe bypass (null tagResult + `createdBy=='system'` → pass), non-seed null-tagResult exclusion. Do NOT duplicate them; extend with a sibling group.

**Inject real `TagEditingService()`** — it is pure-compute (reads `recipe.tagResult.allergenStatus` and `recipe.tagOverrides`, no async, no Firebase). No need to mock it. This pattern is already established in the sibling `setUp` and is correct.

**Build TagResult directly:** Use `TagResult(tags: const {}, allergenStatus: const {'gluten': TriState.free}, dietaryStatus: const {'vegetarisk': TriState.free}, coverage: ..., generatedAt: DateTime(2024), generatorVersion: ..., hasCoverageAnomaly: false)`. Three variants cover the three gates:
1. `fullyTaggedFree()` — `coverage: 1.0`, `generatorVersion: kTagGeneratorVersion` → passes all gates.
2. `partialCoverageFree()` — `coverage: 0.6`, same version → excluded by coverage gate.
3. `needsRetaggingFree()` — `coverage: 1.0`, `generatorVersion: null` → `needsRetagging==true` → excluded before status is read.

**Import needed:** `package:butlery/services/tagging/tag_generator.dart` (show `kTagGeneratorVersion`), `package:butlery/models/tagging/tag_result.dart`, `package:butlery/models/tagging/tri_state.dart`.

**Attach tagResult via `Recipe.copyWith(tagResult: ...)`** — `RecipeFactory.build()` produces a recipe with null `tagResult`, so `copyWith` is the correct extension point.

**Local helper functions inside `group(...)` body** must NOT start with underscore — lint rule `no_leading_underscores_for_local_identifiers` fires. Name them `fullyTaggedFree()` etc., not `_fullyTaggedFree()`.

**`untaggedExclusionMessage` is only non-null when `hasTagBasedFilters == true`** (an allergen OR dietary filter is active). Toggle a filter before asserting the message. The message counts ALL service-level recipes that are unanalysed (`tagResult == null || hasFailed || needsRetagging || coverage < 1.0`), not just the ones excluded from the filtered results — so a mix of one analysed + one unanalysed recipe with a filter active yields `'1 recept analyseras...'`.

**`untaggedRecipeCount` observable via `untaggedExclusionMessage`** — the message embeds the count; assert `contains('1 recept')` rather than calling the count getter directly (DRY + survives a getter rename).

**hasFailed discrepancy noted:** `untaggedRecipeCount` includes `hasFailed` recipes in its denominator, but `_applyAllergenFilters` returns `false` for `needsRetagging` (which includes `hasFailed`) — so hasFailed recipes ARE excluded from filter results AND counted in the untagged banner. No production bug; just a subtle relationship to document.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1333 AuthMfaService: callback-based MFA and private-ctor Firebase types [Pattern]

**Callback-based service methods need captured-invocation stubs.** `startMfaEnrollment` and `startMfaSignIn` are `Future<void>` but drive results through callbacks (`onCodeSent`, `onError`, `onAutoVerified`). The test stub captures the named-param callback from `invocation.namedArguments[#codeSent]` and invokes it synchronously inside `thenAnswer`. Declare local `String? capturedId` / `MfaError? capturedError` before the `await`, assert after.

**Firebase types with private constructors need `Mock implements`, not instantiation.** `MultiFactor`, `MultiFactorResolver` both have private ctors — mock them with `class _MockMultiFactor extends Mock implements MultiFactor {}`. Their methods (getSession, enroll, unenroll, hints, session, resolveSignIn) then stub cleanly with `when()`. Contrast: `MultiFactorSession(id)` and `PhoneMultiFactorInfo(...)` and `TotpMultiFactorInfo(...)` have public ctors and can be instantiated directly as plain test values.

**`no-phone-factor` is a custom MfaError, NOT a Firebase error code.** The service emits it directly (`onError(const MfaError(code: 'no-phone-factor', ...))`) before calling any Firebase API. Testing it requires checking `capturedError?.code` from the `onError` callback — NOT checking `mapAuthErrorToMessage`. Mapping it through the error-mapper returns the generic fallback, which is correct and itself worth pinning as a documentation test.

**BUT-582 pattern applies:** pin BOTH positive (`invalid-phone-number → errorInvalidPhoneNumber`) AND negative (`isNot(AppLocale.current.errorAuthentication)`) for each MFA error code — the negative catches a refactor collapsing MFA branches back to generic.

**`user.multiFactor` is a concrete getter on `User`** that builds a `MultiFactor` from a platform delegate. Since `MockUser extends Mock implements User`, the getter has no concrete body in the mock and can be stubbed: `when(() => mockUser.multiFactor).thenReturn(mockMultiFactor)`.

**`MockAnalyticsService.logLogin` has no concrete `@override` body** (unlike `logEvent` which IS overridden). Stub it with `when(() => mockAnalytics.logLogin(method: any(named: 'method'))).thenAnswer((_) async {})` or it will throw via `noSuchMethod`.

**Test file:** `test/unit/viewmodels/recipe_list_viewmodel_test.dart` — new group `BUT-1335 Allergen/Dietary Safety Gate`, 11 tests. `dart analyze` clean on both the production file and the test file.

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1344 COOK-12: groups appended outside main() parse error [Pattern]

When appending `group(...)` blocks to an existing test file, the blocks MUST be inside `main()`. Placing them after the closing `}` of `main()` causes Dart to parse them as top-level function declarations, producing a cascade of `missing_identifier` / `duplicate_definition` / `missing_function_body` errors. **Always read the tail of the existing file before appending, confirm the `main()` closing brace location, and insert the new groups before it — not after.**

### 2026-06-21 — BUT-1344 COOK-12: IngredientLookupService article+adjective gap [Pattern]

`test/unit/services/tagging/ingredient_lookup_service_test.dart` already covered LRU cache, cache key collision, variation generation (compound suffixes, plurals, definite forms), and offline degrade. Two gaps remained:

1. **Swedish article stripping** (`_cleanForLookup` regex `^(en|ett|den|det|de)\s+`): no test exercised "en tomat" → "tomat". Added 3 tests (en/ett/den).
2. **Adjective prefix/suffix stripping** (`_generateLookupVariations` adjective list): no test exercised "färsk lax" → "lax" or "parmesan riven" → "parmesan". Added 4 tests (2 prefix: farsk/torkad, 2 suffix: riven/hackad).

Each test stubs the exact name the normalizer produces (SwedishCharacterNormalizer output), then leaves the exact match returning null so the variation path is exercised. **Decision rule: for a lookup service with multiple fallback tiers, enumerate each tier in test coverage; a passing "compound suffix" test does NOT cover "adjective stripping" since they are distinct code paths.**

`AddPantryItemSheet` is cleanly widget-testable: it reads `PantryViewModel` via `context.read<PantryViewModel>()`, so wrapping with `ChangeNotifierProvider<PantryViewModel>.value(value: mockVm, ...)` is sufficient — no `TestServiceLocator.initialize()` or production ServiceLocator bridge needed. Same pattern as the a11y chunk-4 test.

Three behavioral tests added to `test/widget/views/pantry/add_pantry_item_sheet_test.dart`:

- Raw text typed, no suggestion → `addItemFromText`.
- Edit mode (`existingItem != null`) → form pre-populates name, submit calls `updateItem` with matching `id`.

**Gotcha: a local helper function named `_buildSheet` inside `main()` triggers `no_leading_underscores_for_local_identifiers`.** Rename to `buildSheet`. Local helper functions inside `main()` that are not `testWidgets`/`setUp` callbacks must not start with `_` per the linter rule.
